Stable rationality of hypersurfaces of mock toric variety I
Taro Yoshino

Abstract
. We introduce a mock toric variety, a generalization of a toric variety. For a non-toric example, Del-Pezzo surfaces are mock toric varieties. These new varieties inherit some properties of mock toric varieties. In application, we give sufficient conditions for the concrete construction of a strictly toroidal model of a hypersurface in a mock toric variety.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
14M25

1.
Introduction

1.1.
Toric variety and its application for construction of a strictly toroidal model

Toric varieties are ”simple” objects.
Hence, they are suitable for concretely constructing various abstract concepts.
For example, we give examples of compactification and a resolution of singularities.
In contexts of toric varieties, a complete fan induces a compactification of an algebraic tori, and an unimodular subdivision induces the resolution of a toric variety.

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field with
\operatorname{char}(k)=0
.
Let
\mathscr{R}
be a valuation ring defined as follows:

\mathscr{R}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}k[[t^{\frac{1}{n}}]]

Let
\mathscr{K}
be a fraction field of
\mathscr{R}
.
We remark that
\mathscr{K}
is written as follows:

\mathscr{K}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}k((t^{\frac{1}{n}}))

In this article, we consider the following definition:

Definition 1.1

(
[
6
]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{X}
be a flat and separated
\mathscr{R}
-scheme of finite type, and let
x\in\mathscr{X}_{k}
be a point.
We say that
\mathscr{X}
is strictly toroidal at
x
if there exist a toric monoid
S
, an open neighbourhood
U
of
x
in
\mathscr{X}
, and a smooth morphism of
\mathscr{R}
-schemes
U\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R}[S]/(\chi^{\omega}-t^{q}))
, where
q
is a positive rational number,
\omega
is an element of
S
, and
\chi^{\omega}
is a torus invariant monomial in
k[S]
associated with
\omega
such that
k[S]/(\chi^{\omega})
is reduced.
We call that
\mathscr{X}
is
strictly toroidal
if it is so at any
x\in\mathscr{X}_{k}
.

For a proper and smooth
\mathscr{K}
-variety
X
, a strictly toroidal model of
X
is essential to compute a stably birational model of
X
.(cf.
[
6
, Theorem 3.3.5]
)
Although its construction is generally challenging, it is easy for toric varieties and Newton non-degenerate hypersurfaces of toric varieties over
\mathscr{K}
(cf.
[
5
]
).

This article gives new examples of strictly toroidal models of some varieties.
In particular, we would like to mention the following two points in this article:

•

An introduction to mock toric varieties, which are the generalization of toric varieties

•

Sufficient conditions for the concrete (NOT abstract) construction of a strictly toroidal model of a hypersurface in a mock toric variety(See Proposition
7.7
)

These new examples are applied to check the stable rationality of some varieties.
For further details, please refer to the preprint by the same author, which will be released on arXiv soon.

In the following subsections, we summarize this article’s key concepts and outline.

1.2.
Mock toric varieties

Section 3 defines mock toric varieties(cf. Definition
3.1
).
In short, a mock toric variety
Z
is a closed subscheme of a toric variety
X
, and it is covered by open subschemes of toric varieties.
In particular, it is a toroidal rational variety.
For example, toric varieties are mock toric varieties.
Moreover, the ”base point free” hyperplane arrangement induces a mock toric variety(See Proposition
5.4
).
As a corollary of it, we show that a Del Pezzo surface is a mock toric variety(See Corollary
5.6
).

While the definition is complicated, mock toric varieties inherit various properties with toric varieties as follows:

•

The torus orbit decomposition of the ambient space
X
induces the stratification of
Z
.
Similar to toric varieties, there is a correspondence between the inclusion relationships of the closure of each stratum and the inclusion relationships of cones(cf. Corollary
3.8
).

•

Each closure of the stratum is also a mock toric variety(cf. Proposition
4.3
).

•

Let
\mu
be a dominant toric morphism
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
.
Then
Z\times_{X}X^{\prime}
is a mock toric variety(cf. Proposition
4.5
).

•

There exists discrete valuations on
Z
trivial on
k
corresponding to lattice points in a toric fan of
X
(cf. Proposition
4.7
).

•

If
Z
is proper over
k
, then a closed immersion
Z\hookrightarrow X
is a tropical compactification of
Z\cap T
in
X
, where
T
is a dense open torus orbit of
X
(cf. Proposition
3.9
).

1.3.
Hypersurfaces of mock toric varieties

Let
X
be a toric variety,
T
be a dense open torus orbit of
X
,
H^{\circ}
be a hypersurface in
T
, and
H
be a scheme theoretic closure of
H^{\circ}
in
X
.
For each torus orbit
O
, we can check whether
O\subset H
by using torus invariant valuations of the defining Laurent polynomial of
H^{\circ}
(cf. Lemma
8.18
).
In general,
H
contains some torus orbits of
X
.
However, there exists a toric resolution
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
such that
H^{\prime}
does not contain any torus orbits of
X^{\prime}
, where
H^{\prime}
denotes a scheme theoretic closure of
H^{\circ}
in
X^{\prime}
(cf.Propositon
8.15
(g)).
The condition that the closure of
H^{\circ}
does not contain any torus orbits of the toric variety is important for constructing a good model.

The same argument works in the case of mock toric varieties.
The details of the above are discussed in Section 6.

1.4.
Strictly toroidal models of hypersurfaces of mock toric varieties

One of the goals of this article is to give sufficient conditions for the concrete construction of a strictly toroidal model of a hypersurface in a mock toric variety.
Because mock toric varieties inherit most properties from toric varieties, sufficient conditions for them can be given, similarly to those of a strict toroidal model of a hypersurface in a toric variety.

As a note,
[
5
]
focused on an irreducible hypersurface over
\mathscr{K}
in an algebraic tori.
In this article, we start the argument from irreducible hypersurfaces in mock toric varieties over
k(t)
and then change it to those over
\mathscr{K}
by a base change.
In general, the irreducibility might be compromised due to the field extension.
Instead, we consider all irreducible components.
Although the argument is complicated, it is still applicable for practical purposes.

1.5.
Outline of the paper

This paper is organized as follows.
In Section 2, we organize the notation used in this article.
Section 3 introduces the definition of mock toric varieties and considers their basic properties.
Section 4 considers some mock toric varieties constructed from a mock toric variety.
We also discuss the set of discrete valuations on mock toric varieties corresponding to the lattice points of the ambient toric fan.
In Section 5, we give some examples of mock toric varieties.
In particular, we show that the ”base point free” hyperplane arrangements induce mock toric varieties.
In Section 6, we consider hypersurfaces of mock toric varieties.
Section 7 gives sufficient conditions for the concrete construction of a strictly toroidal model of a hypersurface in a mock toric variety.
In Section 8, we prove the lemmas needed in this article.

1.6.
Acknowledgment

The author is grateful to his supervisor, Yoshinori Gongyo, for his encouragement.

2.
Notation

In this paper, we use the following notations according to
[
1
]
and
[
2
]
:

•

Let
k
be a field.

•

For an integral scheme
X
,
let
K(X)
denote the function field of
X
.
Let
\operatorname{Val}(X)
denote a set of all discrete valuations on
K(X)
.

•

We call an integral separated scheme of finite type over
k
a variety over
k
.
Let
X
be a variety over
k
and
\operatorname{Val}_{k}(X)
denote a set of all discrete valuations on
K(X)
, which is trivial on
k
.

•

Let
f\colon X\dashrightarrow Y
be a dominant rational map of integral schemes,
f^{*}
denote a morphism of fields
K(Y)\rightarrow K(X)
induced by
f
, and
f_{\natural}
denote the map
\operatorname{Val}(X)\rightarrow\operatorname{Val}(Y)
defined by
f_{\natural}(v)=v\circ f^{*}
for
v\in\operatorname{Val}(X)
.

•

Let
V
be an
\mathbb{R}
-linear vector space of finite dimension,
W
be the dual space of
V
, and
B
be a subset of
V
.
Let
B^{\perp}
and
B^{\vee}
denote as the following subsets of
W
:

B^{\perp}=\{w\in W\mid w(x)=0\quad(\forall x\in B)\}

B^{\vee}=\{w\in W\mid w(x)\geq 0\quad(\forall x\in B)\}

•

Let
N
denote a lattice of finite rank and
M
denote the dual lattice of
N
.

•

Let
\sigma
be a convex cone in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
and
\tau
be a face of
\sigma
.
Then we write
\tau\preceq\sigma
.

•

Let
\sigma
be a convex cone in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Then
\sigma^{\circ}
denotes the relative interior of
\sigma
.

•

Let
\Delta
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Then
X_{k}(\Delta)
denote a toric variety corresponding to
\Delta
over
k
.
We sometimes write
X(\Delta)
insted of
X_{k}(\Delta)
.

•

Let
k[M]
denote a
k
-algebra induced by a monoid
M
.
For
\omega\in M
, let
\chi^{\omega}
denote a monomial in
k[M]
associated with
\omega
.

•

Let
T_{N}
denote
\operatorname{Spec}(k[M])
.
we remark that
X(\{0_{N}\})=T_{N}
.

•

Let
\Delta
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan and
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone.
Let
O_{\sigma}
denote the orbit of the torus action of
X(\Delta)
corresponding to
\sigma
.

•

Let
\sigma
be a convex cone in an
\mathbb{R}
-linear space
V
.
Let
\langle\sigma\rangle
denote an
\mathbb{R}
-linear subspace of
V
which is spanned by
\sigma
.

•

For a strongly convex rational fan
\Delta
in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
and
\sigma\in\Delta
, let
N[\sigma]
denote
N/(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)
,
\pi^{\sigma}
denote the quotient map from
N
to
N[\sigma]
,
k[\sigma^{\vee}\cap M]
denote
k
-algebra defined by a monoid
\sigma^{\vee}\cap M
,
M^{\sigma}
denote the dual lattice of
N[\sigma]
.
We remark that
k[\sigma^{\vee}\cap M]\cong\Gamma(X(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{X(\sigma)})
and
k[M^{\sigma}]\cong\Gamma(O_{\sigma},\mathscr{O}_{O_{\sigma}})
.

•

For the notation above, let
p^{\sigma}
denote a quotient map from
k[\sigma^{\vee}\cap M]
to
k[M^{\sigma}]
associated with the closed immersion
O_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow X(\sigma)
and
(\pi^{\sigma})^{*}
denote a lattice morphism
M^{\sigma}\rightarrow M
induced by
\pi^{\sigma}
.
We remark that
(\pi^{\sigma})^{*}
is injective,
(\pi^{\sigma})^{*}(M^{\sigma})=\sigma^{\perp}\cap M
, and an ideal
\ker(p^{\sigma})
is generated by
\{\chi^{\omega}\}_{\omega\in(\sigma^{\vee}\cap M)\setminus\sigma^{\perp}}
.

•

For the notation above, let
\Delta[\sigma]
denote the following set:

\Delta[\sigma]=\{\pi^{\sigma}_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\mid\sigma\preceq\tau\in\Delta\}

From Proposition
8.8
,
\Delta[\sigma]
is a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N[\sigma]_{\mathbb{R}}
.

•

Let
N
and
N^{\prime}
be lattices of finite rank and
f\colon N^{\prime}\rightarrow N
be a homomorphism,
f_{\mathbb{R}}
denote an
\mathbb{R}
-linear map
N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow N_{\mathbb{R}}
induced by
f
,
M^{\prime}
denote the dual lattice of
N^{\prime}
, and
f^{*}
denote a lattice morphism
M\rightarrow M^{\prime}
induced by
f
.
With a slight abuse of notation, We also use
f^{*}
for a morphism of
k
-algebras
k[M]\rightarrow k[M^{\prime}]
defined by
\chi^{\omega}\mapsto\chi^{f^{*}(\omega)}
for
\omega\in M
.

•

On the convention above, let
\Delta
and
\Delta^{\prime}
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
and
N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
respectively.
If for any
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}
, there exists
\sigma\in\Delta
such that
f_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma
, then we call that
f
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\prime}
and
\Delta
.

•

On the notation above, the map
f_{*}
denote the toric morphism from
X(\Delta^{\prime})
to
X(\Delta)
induced by
f
.

•

Let
\Delta_{!}
denote a convex fan
\{\{0\},[0,\infty)\}
in
\mathbb{R}
.

•

Let
\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2}
be fans in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\mathbb{R}^{m}
respectively and
\Delta_{1}\times\Delta_{2}
denote the following fan in
\mathbb{R}^{n+m}
:

\{\sigma_{1}\times\sigma_{2}\mid\sigma_{1}\in\Delta_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\Delta_{2}\}

•

Let
\Delta
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
,
f\in k[M]
denote a polynomial.
Let
H^{\circ}_{X(\Delta),f}
denote a hypersurface of
T_{N}
defined by
f=0
, and
H_{X(\Delta),f}
denote a scheme theoretic closure of
H^{\circ}_{X(\Delta),f}
in
X(\Delta)
.

•

Let
N
be a lattice of finite rank,
M
be the dual lattice, and
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
be a pairing of
N
and
M
.
For
v\in N
, we can identify
v
as a
T_{N}
-invariant valuation on
T_{N}
with trivial on
k
, and let
v(f)
denote a value of
f\in k(M)
by
v
, where
k(M)
denote the fraction field of
k[M]
.
On this identification,
v(\chi^{\omega})=\langle v,\omega\rangle
for any
v\in N
and
\omega\in M
.

•

Let
S
be a monoid.
If there exists a lattice
N
of finite rank and a full and strongly convex rational polyhedral cone
\sigma
in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
S
is isomorphic to
\sigma^{\vee}\cap M
, then we call
S
is a
toric monoid
.

3.
Mock toric variety

In this section,
k
denotes a field.
We define a structure of mock toric varieties for
k
-schemes.
Subsequently, we examine the properties of the mock toric varieties.
Surprisingly, these new varieties inherit certain properties of the toric varieties.

3.1.
Definition of the mock toric variety

In this subsection, we introduce mock toric varieties.
We remark that this variety has no torus action in general.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
.
The mock toric structure of
Z
consists of the following 6 data, which satisfy the following conditions from (1) to (5):

•

A lattice
N
of finite rank

•

A strongly convex rational polyhedral fan
\Delta
in
N_{\mathbb{R}}

•

A closed immersion
\iota\colon Z\hookrightarrow X(\Delta)

•

A finite set
\Phi

•

A family
\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
which consists of sublattices of
N

•

A family
\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
which consists of sub fans of
\Delta

(1)

For any
\varphi\in\Phi
and
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
,
N/((\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)+N_{\varphi})
is torsion-free.
In particular, from this condition,
N/N_{\varphi}
is torsion-free. (see proposition Proposition
3.2
(a))

(2)

The following equation holds:

\Delta=\bigcup_{\varphi\in\Phi}\Delta_{\varphi}.

(3)

For
\varphi\in\Phi
, let
q_{\varphi}
denote a quotient map
N\rightarrow N/N_{\varphi}
.
Then the map
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta_{\varphi})}
is injective.

(4)

For any
\sigma\in\Delta
,

Z\cap O_{\sigma}\neq\emptyset
.

(5)

From (3), The following set
\Delta(\varphi)
is a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
(N/N_{\varphi})\otimes\mathbb{R}
(see Proposition
3.2
(c)):

\Delta(\varphi)=\{(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)\mid\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}\}

Then the following composition
\iota_{\varphi}
is an open immersion:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta(\varphi)),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}

where the former one is a closed immersion restricted to
Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})
.

We call that
(N,\Delta,\iota,\Phi,\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi},\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
is a mock toric structure of
Z
.

The following lemma summarizes fundamental facts that immediately hold from Definition
3.1
.

Proposition 3.2
.

In the definition Definition
3.1
, the following statements follow:

(a)

For any
\varphi\in\Phi
,
N/N_{\varphi}
is a torsion-free module.

(b)

For any
\varphi\in\Phi
and any
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
,
\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N_{\varphi}=\{0\}
.

(c)

A set
\Delta(\varphi)
is a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
(N/N_{\varphi})\otimes\mathbb{R}
.

(d)

Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element and
\sigma\in\Delta\varphi
be a cone. Let
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
Then
q_{\varphi}|_{\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N}\colon\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N\rightarrow\langle\sigma_{\varphi}\rangle\cap N/N_{\varphi}
is isomorphic.

Proof.

We prove these statements from (a) to (d) in order:

(a)

Because
\Delta_{\varphi}
is a strongly convex fan, we have
\{0\}\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Thus,
N/N_{\varphi}
is torsion-free from the condition (1).

(b)

Let
v\in\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N_{\varphi}
be an element.
Then there exists
v_{1}
and
v_{2}\in\sigma
such that
v=v_{1}-v_{2}
.
Because
v\in N_{\varphi}
, we have
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(v_{1})=(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(v_{2})
.
Thus,
v_{1}=v_{2}
from the condition (3).
Therefore,
v=0
.

(c)

We remark that
N/N_{\varphi}
is a lattice from (a).
From the condition (3) and Lemma
8.1
(c),
\Delta(\varphi)
is a polyhedral convex fan in
(N/N_{\varphi})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
.
Because
q_{\varphi}
is a lattice morphism,
\Delta(\varphi)
is a rational polyhedral convex fan.
Moreover, because
\Delta_{\varphi}
is strongly convex,
\Delta(\varphi)
is strongly convex from
8.1
(d).

(d)

From the condition (1) in
3.1
, (b), and Lemma
8.2
, there exists a section
\theta
of
q_{\varphi}
such that
\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N\subset\theta(N/N_{\varphi})
.
In particular,
\langle\sigma\rangle\subset\theta_{\mathbb{R}}((N/N_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}})
.
Let
v^{\prime}\in\langle\sigma_{\varphi}\rangle\cap N/N_{\varphi}
be an element.
Then there exists
v_{1}\in\langle\sigma\rangle
and
v_{2}\in N
such that
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(v_{1})=v^{\prime}
and
q_{\varphi}(v_{2})=v^{\prime}
.
From the condition of
\theta
and the rational polyhedral convexity of
\sigma
,
v_{1}=\theta_{\mathbb{R}}(v^{\prime})
and
v_{2}=\theta(v^{\prime})
.
Thus,
v_{1}=v_{2}
because
\theta_{\mathbb{R}}
is injective.
This shows that
v^{\prime}\in q_{\varphi}(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)
.

On the other hand,
q_{\varphi}|_{\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N}
is injective form (b). Therefore, the statement holds.

∎

Example 3.3
.

We show some examples of mock toric varieties.

(1)

Let
N
be a lattice of finite rank and
\Delta
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Then,
Z=X(\Delta)
has a natural mock toric structure.
In fact,
\mathrm{id}_{Z}
is a closed immersion and let
\Phi
denote
\{0\}
,
N_{0}
denote
\{0\}
, and
\Delta_{0}
denote
\Delta
.
Then one can check easily that
(N
,
\Delta
,
\mathrm{id}_{Z}
,
\{0\}
,
\{N_{0}\}
,
\{\Delta_{0}\})
is a mock toric structure of
Z
.

(2)

Let
Z
be a projective line
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
A scheme
Z
has a natural toric structure, but it has another mock toric structure as follows:
Let
N
be a lattice of rank
2
.
Then there exists
e_{0},e_{1},e_{2}\in N
as follows:

–

These are generators of
N
.

–

e_{0}+e_{1}+e_{2}=0
.

Let
\Delta
denote the following fan:

\Delta=\{\{0\},\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}e_{0},\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}e_{1},\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}e_{2}\}

This toric variety
X(\Delta)
is an open subscheme of projective plane
\mathbb{P}^{2}
.
Let
\iota\colon Z\rightarrow X(\Delta)
be a closed immersion in which
Z
intersects transversely at one point on each torus invariant divisor of
X(\Delta)
.
Let
\Phi
denote
\{0,1,2\}
, and for
i\in\Phi
, we define sublattice
N_{i}
of
N
as
\mathbb{Z}\cdot e_{i}
and a sub fan
\Delta_{i}
of
\Delta
as
\Delta\setminus\{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}e_{i}\}
.
Then we can check easily that
(N
,
\Delta
,
\iota
,
\Phi
,
\{N_{i}\}_{i\in\Phi}
,
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in\Phi})
is a mock toric structure of
Z
.

(3)

For example (2), we can regard three points on
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{k}
as a ”base point free” hyperplane arrangement on
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{k}
.
In general, we show that a ”base point free” hyperplane arrangement induces a mock toric variety in Section 5.

From the definition of a mock toric structure, we can check easily the properties of mock toric varieties as follows:

Proposition 3.4
.

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
and
(N,\Delta,\iota,\Phi,\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi},\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
.
The following statements follow:

(a)

The scheme
Z
is a separated normal integral scheme of finite type over
k
.

(b)

For any
\varphi\in\Phi
, the rank of
N/N_{\varphi}
is
\dim(Z)
.

(c)

For any
\varphi\in\Phi
, the image of
\iota_{\varphi}
intersects generic points of all torus orbits of
X(\Delta(\varphi))
.

(d)

For any
\sigma\in\Delta
, we have
\dim(Z\cap O_{\sigma})+\dim(\langle\sigma\rangle)=\dim(Z)
.

Proof.

We prove these statements from (a) to (d) in order.

(a)

From the condition (2) and (5),
\{Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
is an open covering of
Z
.
From condition (5), each
Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})
is an integral normal scheme, and each of them contains a non-empty subset
Z\cap T_{N}
from condition (4).
Thus,
Z
is an integral normal scheme.
A scheme
Z
is a closed subscheme of toric variety over
k
, so
Z
is a separated scheme of finite type over
k
.

(b)

From the condition (5) and the above argument,
\dim(Z)=\dim(X(\Delta(\varphi))
for any
\varphi\in\Phi
.
Because
\Delta(\varphi)
is strongly convex,
\operatorname{rank}(N/N_{\varphi})=\dim(X(\Delta(\varphi)))
.
Thus, the statement holds.

(c)

From condition (3) and Lemma
8.1
(b), there exists a one-to-one correspondence of cones in
\Delta_{\varphi}
and those in
\Delta(\varphi)
by
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element and
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
be a cone.
Let
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
Then the following diagram of toric morphisms is a Cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta(\varphi)),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}

where vertical morphisms are open immersions.
Moreover, from the condition (1), Proposition
3.2
(b), Lemma
8.2
, and Lemma
8.3
(e), the following diagram is a Cartesian diagram too.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma_{\varphi}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}

Thus, there exists the following diagram whose two small squares are Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\cap O_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta(\varphi))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}

Hence, from the condition (5) and the above diagram,
\iota_{\varphi}(Z\cap O_{\sigma})
is an
open subset of
O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}
.
From the condition (4),
\iota_{\varphi}(Z\cap O_{\sigma})
is non-empty, so the statement follows.

(d)

We use the notation in the proof of (c).
We have that
\dim(\langle\sigma_{\varphi}\rangle)+\dim(O_{\sigma_{\varphi}})=\operatorname{rank}(N/N_{\varphi})
from the argument of the toric varieties.
Because
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\langle\sigma\rangle}
is injective,
\dim(\langle\sigma_{\varphi}\rangle)=\dim(\langle\sigma\rangle)
.
Moreover, from the above argument of proof of (c),
\dim(O_{\sigma_{\varphi}})=\dim(Z\cap O_{\sigma})
.
Thus, from (b),
\dim(\langle\sigma\rangle)+\dim(Z\cap O_{\sigma})=\dim(Z)
.

∎

A mock toric variety has a natural stratification and properties similar to a toric variety.
After defining the notation of the stratification at Definition
3.5
, we show the properties of this stratification in Proposition
3.6
.

Definition 3.5
.

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
,
(N,\Delta,\iota,\Phi,\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi},\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
, and
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone.

(1)

Let
Z(\sigma)
denote an open subscheme of
Z
defined as the following Cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

where vertical morphisms are open immersions.

(2)

Let
Z_{\sigma}
denote a scheme over
k
defined as the following Cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

where vertical morphisms are closed immersions.

(3)

Let
Z^{\circ}
denote
Z\cap T_{N}
.

(4)

Let
k[Z^{\circ}]
denote the section ring
\Gamma(Z^{\circ},\mathscr{O}_{Z})
.

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
and
(N,\Delta,\iota,\Phi,\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi},\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
.
The following statements follow.

(a)

For any
\sigma
and
\tau\in\Delta
, the following statements are equivalent:

(1)

\tau\subset\sigma
.

(2)

Z_{\sigma}\subset\overline{Z_{\tau}}
.

(3)

Z(\tau)\subset Z(\sigma)
.

(b)

For any
\sigma\in\Delta
,
Z(\sigma)
is an integral normal affine scheme of finite type over
k
.

(c)

For any
\sigma\in\Delta
,
Z_{\sigma}
is an integral smooth affine scheme of finite type over
k
.

(d)

A ring
k[Z^{\circ}]
is a UFD.

Proof.

We prove these statements from (a) to (c) in order.

(a)

First, when the statement (1) holds, we show that the statement (2) holds.
From the condition (2) in Definition
3.1
, there exists
\varphi\in\Phi
such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Because
\Delta_{\varphi}
is a sub fan of
\Delta
,
\tau\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Let
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
and
\tau_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)
.
From the proof of Proposition
3.4
(c),
\iota_{\varphi}(Z_{\sigma})
and
\iota_{\varphi}(Z_{\tau})
are dense open subset of
O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}
and
O_{\tau_{\varphi}}
respectively.
In
X(\Delta(\varphi))
,
O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}\subset\overline{O_{\tau_{\varphi}}}
because
X(\Delta(\varphi))
is a toric variety.
Thus, statement (2) holds.

Second, when the statement (2) holds, we show that the statement (3) holds.
From the statement (2) and the condition (2) in Definition
3.1
,
O_{\sigma}\cap\overline{O_{\tau}}\neq\emptyset
.
Thus, from the property of the torus orbit decomposition of toric varieties,
X(\tau)\subset X(\sigma)
.

Finally, when the statement (3) holds, we show that the statement (1) holds.
From statement (3), we can check that
O_{\tau}\cap X(\sigma)\neq\emptyset
.
Thus,
\tau\subset\sigma
from the property of the torus orbit decomposition of toric varieties.

(b)

From the definition of
Z(\sigma)
,
Z(\sigma)
is an open subscheme of a normal integral scheme of
Z
from Proposition
3.4
(a).
Moreover,
Z(\sigma)
is a closed subscheme of an affine scheme
X(\sigma)
.
Thus, the statement follows.

(c)

From the definition of
Z_{\sigma}
,
Z_{\sigma}
is a closed subscheme of an affine variety of
O_{\sigma}
, so that
Z_{\sigma}
is an affine scheme.
Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
we have already shown that
Z_{\sigma}
is an open subscheme of
O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}
in the proof of Proposition
3.4
(c), where
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
Thus,
Z_{\sigma}
is smooth over
k
.

(d)

Because
Z^{\circ}
is a closed subscheme of
T_{N}
,
k[Z^{\circ}]
is of finite type over
k
.
In particular,
k[Z^{\circ}]
is a Noether ring.
On the other hand, for any
\varphi\in\Phi
,
Z^{\circ}
is an open subscheme of
T_{N/N_{\varphi}}
.
Thus, it is enough to show the following basic statement of the ring theory:

–

Let
A
be a ring of finite type over
k
. Let
V
be an affine open subscheme of
\operatorname{Spec}(A)
and
B
denote
\Gamma(V,\mathscr{O}_{\operatorname{Spec}(A)})
.
We assume that
B
is of finite type over
k
and
A
is a UFD.
Then
B
is a UFD.

Indeed, it is enough to show that every hight 1 prime ideal of
B
is principal.
Let
\mathfrak{p}\in V
be a hight 1 prime of
B
and
\mathfrak{q}
denote
A\cap\mathfrak{p}
.
We remark that
\mathfrak{q}
is a hight 1 prime ideal of
A
because
V\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(A)
is an open immersion.
Thus, there exists
f\in A
such that
f
generates
\mathfrak{q}
.
We claim that
f
generate
\mathfrak{p}
.
Let
g\in\mathfrak{p}
be an element.
Then there exists
u,h\in A
such that
u
and
h
are coprime and
g=u^{-1}h
.
Hence, we can check that
\operatorname{Supp}(A/(u))\cap V=\emptyset
because
g\in\Gamma(V,\mathscr{O}_{\operatorname{Spec}(A)})
and
A
is a UFD.
In particular,
u\in B^{*}
and
h\in\mathfrak{q}
.
Thus, there exists
l\in A
such that
h=lf
.
Therefore,
g=u^{-1}lf
in
B
.

∎

The following proposition shows that the scheme theoretic closure of the torus orbit of the ambient toric variety gives the scheme theoretic closure of each stratum of the mock toric variety.

Proposition 3.7
.

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
and
(N,\Delta,\iota,\Phi,\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi},\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
.
Then the scheme theoretic closure of
Z_{\sigma}
in
Z
is equal to
\overline{O_{\sigma}}\times_{X(\Delta)}Z
for any
\sigma\in\Delta
.

Proof.

A family of open subset
\{Z(\tau)\}_{\tau\in\Delta}
is an open covering of
Z
.
From Lemma
8.30
, it is enough to show that the scheme theoretic closure of
Z_{\sigma}
in
Z(\tau)
is
\overline{O_{\sigma}}\times_{X(\Delta)}Z(\tau)=(\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau))\times_{X(\tau)}Z(\tau)
for any
\tau\in\Delta
.
Moreover, it is enough to check this claim for all
\tau\in\Delta
such that
\sigma\subset\tau
.
Indeed, for any
\tau\in\Delta
such that
\sigma\not\subset\tau
, we have
\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau)=\emptyset
and
Z_{\sigma}\cap Z(\tau)=\emptyset
.
There exists
\varphi\in\Phi
such that
\tau\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
let
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
and
\tau_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)
.
From Lemma
8.3
(d) and (e), all small squares of the following diagram are
Cartesian squares and the lower morphism from
Z(\tau)
to
X(\tau_{\varphi})
is an open immersion:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau))\times_{X(\tau)}Z(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}\cap X(\tau_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau_{\varphi})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}

By the way,
\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}\cap X(\tau_{\varphi})
is a scheme theoretic closure of
O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}
in
X(\tau_{\varphi})
.
Thus, from Lemma
8.29
,
(\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau))\times_{X(\tau)}Z(\tau)
is a scheme theoretic closure of
Z_{\sigma}
in
Z(\tau)
.
∎

As toric varieties, the following statement holds from Proposition
3.7
.

Corollary 3.8
.

We keep the notation in Proposition
3.6
.
The following statements are equivalent:

(i)

\tau\subset\sigma

(ii)

Z_{\sigma}\cap\overline{Z_{\tau}}\neq\emptyset

Proof.

We can check quickly that one direction follows from Proposition
3.6
(a).
Then, we assume that statement (ii) holds.
From Proposition
3.7
,
\overline{Z_{\tau}}=Z\cap\overline{O_{\tau}}
.
This indicates
O_{\sigma}\cap\overline{O_{\tau}}\neq\emptyset
.
Thus, the statement (i) holds from the argument of toric varieties.
∎

Fundamentally, one notable point of the superiority of
[
5
]
is to focus on tropical compactification.
Tropical compactification was initially defined in
[
8
]
and is known for various applications.
Interestingly, the following proposition asserts that a proper mock toric variety
Z
is a tropical compactification of
Z\cap T_{N}
.
This result indicates that the method of
[
5
]
can be applied to the mock toric varieties.

Proposition 3.9
.

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
and
(N,\Delta,\iota,\Phi,\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi},\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
.
Let
T
denote
T_{N}
.
Then the following action map
m
is faithfully flat:

m\colon T\times Z\rightarrow X(\Delta)

Moreover, if
Z
is proper over
k
, then
Z
is a tropical compactification of
Z^{\circ}
.

Proof.

From condition (4) in Definition
3.1
,
m
is surjective.
Thus, it is enough to show that the restriction
m|_{T\times Z(\sigma)}\colon T\times Z(\sigma)\rightarrow X(\sigma)
is flat for any
\sigma\in\Delta
.
Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
, and
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
From Lemma
8.3
, there exists the toric isomorphism
\lambda\colon X(\sigma)\rightarrow X(\sigma_{\varphi})\times T^{\prime}
such that
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\circ\lambda=(q_{\varphi})_{*}
, where
T^{\prime}
denotes an algebraic torus with
\dim(T^{\prime})=\operatorname{rank}(N_{\varphi})
.
Let
T_{\varphi}
denote
T_{N/N_{\varphi}}
,
m_{\varphi}
denote an action map
T_{\varphi}\times X(\sigma_{\varphi})\rightarrow X(\sigma_{\varphi})
, and
m^{\prime}
denote the multiplicative morphism
T^{\prime}\times T^{\prime}\rightarrow T^{\prime}
.
We remark that
\lambda
induces the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\times X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T_{\varphi}\times X(\sigma_{\varphi}))\times(T^{\prime}\times T^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma_{\varphi})\times T^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda\times\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{\varphi}\times m^{\prime}}

Moreover, we can check that
\lambda|_{T}\colon T\rightarrow T_{\varphi}\times T^{\prime}
is isomorphic.
From the condition (5) in Definition
3.1
,
\iota_{\varphi}(Z(\sigma))
is an open subset of
X(\sigma_{\varphi})
.
Let
V
denote
\iota_{\varphi}(Z(\sigma))
,
j\colon V\rightarrow Z(\sigma)
denote the inverse morphism of
\iota_{\varphi}
, and
p
denote the composition
\mathrm{pr}_{2}\circ\lambda\circ\iota\colon Z\rightarrow T^{\prime}
.
Let
\theta
denote the morphism from
T^{\prime}\times Z(\sigma)\rightarrow T^{\prime}\times V
defined as follows:

T^{\prime}\times Z(\sigma)\ni(t^{\prime},z)\mapsto(t^{\prime}\cdot p(z),\iota_{\varphi}(z))\in T^{\prime}\times V

Then we claim the following statements:

(1)

The morphism
\theta
is an isomorphism.

(2)

The following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\times Z(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\times X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\varphi}\times T^{\prime}\times Z(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\varphi}\times T^{\prime}\times V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T_{\varphi}\times T^{\prime})\times(X(\sigma_{\varphi})\times T^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma_{\varphi})\times T^{\prime},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}_{T}\times\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda\times\mathrm{id}_{Z(\sigma)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}_{T_{\varphi}}\times\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{\varphi}\times m^{\prime}}

where
l
denotes a morphism defined as follows:

T_{\varphi}\times T^{\prime}\times V\ni(t,t^{\prime},v)\mapsto((t,t^{\prime}),(v,1))\in(T_{\varphi}\times T^{\prime})\times(X(\sigma_{\varphi})\times T^{\prime})

Indeed, we can check that the inverse morphism of
\theta

is defined as follows:

T^{\prime}\times V\ni(t^{\prime},v)\mapsto(t^{\prime}\cdot{p(j(v))}^{-1},j(v))\in T^{\prime}\times Z(\sigma)

Moreover, we can check the commutativity of the above diagram because of the definition of
\theta
and the above remark.

Then the composition
(m_{\varphi}\times m^{\prime})\circ l
is equal to the restriction
(m_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{T^{\prime}})|_{(T_{\varphi}\times V)\times T^{\prime}}\colon

(T_{\varphi}\times V)\times T^{\prime}\rightarrow X(\sigma_{\varphi})\times T^{\prime}
.
Thus,
(m_{\varphi}\times m^{\prime})\circ l
is flat because
V
is an open subset of
X(\sigma_{\varphi})
and
m_{\varphi}
is flat.
In particular, the action map
m\colon T\times Z(\sigma)\rightarrow X(\sigma)
is flat from the above claim.
∎

The following proposition shows that the smoothness of the ambient space induces the smoothness of Z.

Proposition 3.10
.

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
and
(N,\Delta,\iota,\Phi,\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi},\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
.
We assume that
\Delta
is unimodular.
Then the following statements follow:

(a)

For any
\varphi\in\Phi
,
\Delta(\varphi)
is unimodular.

(b)

A scheme
Z
is smooth scheme over
k
.

Proof.

We prove the statements from (a) to (b).

(a)

Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone and
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Let
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
From the condition (1), Proposition
3.2
(b), and Lemma
8.2
, there exists a sub lattice
N_{1}
of
N
such that
\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N\subset N_{1}
and
N_{1}\oplus N_{\varphi}=N
.
Because
N_{1}/(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)
is a torsion free, there exists a sub lattice
N_{2}
of
N_{1}
such that
(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)\oplus N_{2}=N_{1}
.
Thus, we have
(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)\oplus N_{2}\oplus N_{\varphi}=N
.
Because
\sigma
is unimodular, there exists
v_{1},\ldots,v_{r}\in\sigma
such that
\{v_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq r}
is a basis of
\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N
and
\{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}v_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq r}
are all rays of
\sigma
, where
r
is
\dim(\sigma)
.
From Lemma
8.3
(c),
\{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}q_{\varphi}(v_{i})\}_{1\leq i\leq r}
is the set of all rays of
\sigma_{\varphi}
.
Moreover, from the above disjoint sum,
\{q_{\varphi}(v_{i})\}
is a basis of
\langle\sigma_{\varphi}\rangle\cap(N/N_{\varphi})
.
Therefore,
\sigma_{\varphi}
is unimodular.

(b)

From (a), for any
\varphi\in\Phi
,
X(\Delta(\varphi))
is smooth over
k
.
Thus, from condition (5) in Definition
3.1
,
Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})
is smooth over
k
.
Therefore,
Z
is smooth over
k
.

∎

4.
Induced mock toric structure

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
and
(N
,
\Delta
,
\iota
,
\Phi
,
\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
,
\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
.
In this section, we construct other mock toric varieties from
Z
.
To be precise, we construct two mock toric structures for these schemes:

(1)

A scheme
Z\times_{X(\Delta)}\overline{O_{\sigma}}
, where
\sigma\in\Delta
.
This is a scheme theoretic closure of
Z_{\sigma}
in
Z
from Proposition
3.7

(2)

The pullback of
Z
along a dominant toric morphism
X(\Delta^{\prime})\rightarrow X(\Delta)

4.1.
Induced mock toric structure for orbit closure

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
and
(N
,
\Delta
,
\iota
,
\Phi
,
\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
,
\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
.
Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone.
From Proposition
3.7
,
\overline{Z_{\sigma}}
is a closed subscheme of toric variety
\overline{O_{\sigma}}
.
In this subsection, we construct a mock toric structure of
\overline{Z_{\sigma}}
.

Definition 4.1
.

We use the above notation.
Let
N[\sigma]
denote the quotient lattice
N/(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)
and
\pi^{\sigma}
denote the quotient map
N\rightarrow N[\sigma]
.
Let
\Delta^{\sigma}
denote the following sub fan of
\Delta
:

\Delta^{\sigma}=\{\gamma\in\Delta\mid\exists\tau\in\Delta\ \ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \ \gamma\cup\sigma\subset\tau\}

, and
\Delta[\sigma]
denote the following set:

\Delta[\sigma]=\{(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\mid\sigma\subset\tau\in\Delta\}

From Proposition
8.8
,
\Delta[\sigma]
is a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan, and we regard
Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}}
as closed subscheme of
X(\Delta[\sigma])
from the following diagram whose all squares in the following diagram are Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{O_{\sigma}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\cap X(\Delta^{\sigma})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\sigma})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta[\sigma])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi^{\sigma})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

Let
\iota^{\sigma}
denote a closed immersion from
Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}}
to
X(\Delta[\sigma])
in the above diagram.
Let
\Phi^{\sigma}
denote a subset of
\Phi
as follows:

\Phi^{\sigma}=\{\varphi\in\Phi\mid\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}\}

For
\varphi\in\Phi^{\sigma}
, let
N[\sigma]_{\varphi}
denote
\pi^{\sigma}(N_{\varphi})
and
\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}
denote the following set:

\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}=\{\pi^{\sigma}_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\in\Delta[\sigma]\mid\tau\in\Delta_{\varphi}\ \ \mathrm{and}\ \ \sigma\subset\tau\}

In the following proposition, we check that
N[\sigma]_{\varphi}
is a lattice and
\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}
is a subfan of
\Delta[\sigma]
for any
\varphi\in\Phi^{\sigma}
.

Proposition 4.2
.

We keep the notation in Definition
4.1
and let
\varphi\in\Phi^{\sigma}
be an element.
Then
N[\sigma]_{\varphi}
is a lattice and
\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}
is a subfan of
\Delta[\sigma]
.

Proof.

A module
N/(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)
is torsion-free, so that subgroup
\pi^{\sigma}(N_{\varphi})
of
N[\sigma]
is torsion free too.
Thus, we only need to show that for any
\tau^{\prime}\in\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}
, all faces of
\tau^{\prime}
are in
\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}
.
There exists
\tau\in\Delta
such that
(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}
,
\tau\in\Delta_{\varphi}
, and
\sigma\subset\tau
.
Because
\Delta[\sigma]
is a fan, for any face
\gamma^{\prime}\preceq\tau^{\prime}
, there exists
\gamma\in\Delta
such that
(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\gamma)=\gamma^{\prime}
and
\sigma\subset\gamma
.
From Corollary
8.9
(a), we have
\gamma\subset\tau
.
Because
\Delta_{\varphi}
is a fan,
\gamma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
, in particular,
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}
.
∎

The following proposition shows that
\overline{Z_{\sigma}}
has a mock toric structure.

Proposition 4.3
.

We keep the notation in Definition
4.1
.
Then
(N[\sigma],\Delta[\sigma],

\iota^{\sigma},\Phi^{\sigma},

\{N[\sigma]_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sigma}},\ \ \

\{\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sigma}})
is a mock toric structure of
Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}}
.

Proof.

We check that this tuple satisfies the conditions in Definition
3.1
from (1) to (5) in order.

(1)

We show that for any
\varphi\in\Phi^{\sigma}
and
\tau^{\prime}\in\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}
,
N[\sigma]/((\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle\cap N[\sigma])+N[\sigma]_{\varphi})
is a torsion-free module.
Let
\tau\in\Delta_{\varphi}
be a cone such that
(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}
and
\sigma\subset\tau
.
Before the proof, we remark that
\pi^{\sigma}(\langle\tau\rangle\cap N)=\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle\cap N[\sigma]
.
Indeed, for any
v^{\prime}\in\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle\cap N[\sigma]
, there exists
v\in\langle\tau\rangle
and
w\in N
such that
v^{\prime}=(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(v)=(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(w)
.
Thus,
v-w\in\langle\sigma\rangle
, in particular,
w\in\langle\tau\rangle
because
\sigma\subset\tau
.
Therefore,
w\in\langle\tau\rangle\cap N
.

From the following equation,
N[\sigma]/((\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle\cap N[\sigma])+N[\sigma]_{\varphi})
is isomorphic to
N/((\langle\tau\rangle\cap N)+N_{\varphi})
:

\displaystyle(\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle\cap N[\sigma])+N[\sigma]_{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\pi^{\sigma}(\langle\tau\rangle\cap N)+\pi^{\sigma}(N_{\varphi})

\displaystyle=\pi^{\sigma}((\langle\tau\rangle\cap N)+N_{\varphi})

Thus,
N[\sigma]/((\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle\cap N[\sigma])+N[\sigma]_{\varphi})
is a torsion-free module from the condition (1) in Definition
3.1
.

(2)

We show that the following equation holds:

\Delta[\sigma]=\bigcup_{\varphi\in\Phi^{\sigma}}\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}.

For
\tau^{\prime}\in\Delta[\sigma]
, let
\tau\in\Delta
be a cone such that
\sigma\subset\tau
and
(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}
.
From the condition (2) in Definition
3.1
, there exists
\varphi\in\Phi
such that
\tau\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Because
\Delta_{\varphi}
is a subfan of
\Delta
, we have
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Thus,
\varphi\in\Phi^{\sigma}
and
\tau^{\prime}=(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\in\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}
.

(3)

For
\varphi\in\Phi^{\sigma}
, let
q^{\sigma}_{\varphi}
denote the quotient morphism

N[\sigma]\rightarrow N[\sigma]/N[\sigma]_{\varphi}
.
We show that the map
(q^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi})}
is injective.
Let
\pi^{\sigma}_{\varphi}\colon N/N_{\varphi}\rightarrow N/((\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)+N_{\varphi})=N[\sigma]/N[\sigma]_{\varphi}
be the natural quotient map, and we remark that the following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N[\sigma]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N/N_{\varphi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N[\sigma]/N[\sigma]_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\sigma}_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\sigma}_{\varphi}}

Let
x^{\prime}
and
y^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi})
be elements.
Then there exists
\tau^{\prime}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}
such that
x^{\prime}\in\tau^{\prime}
and
y^{\prime}\in\gamma^{\prime}
.
From the definition of
\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}
, there exists
\tau
and
\gamma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
such that
(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}
,
(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\gamma)=\gamma^{\prime}
, and
\sigma\subset\tau\cap\gamma
.
In particular, there exists
x\in\tau
and
y\in\gamma
such that
(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(x)=x^{\prime}
and
(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(y)=y^{\prime}
.

From now on, we assume that
(q^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(x^{\prime})=(q^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(y^{\prime})
and we show that
x^{\prime}=y^{\prime}
.
From the assumption,
x-y\in\langle\sigma\rangle+(N_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Thus, there exists
z,w\in\sigma
such that
(x-y)-(z-w)\in(N_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Hence,
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(x+w)=(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(y+z)
.
Because
x\in\tau
,
y\in\gamma
and
\sigma\subset\tau\cap\gamma
, we have
x+w\in\tau
and
y+z\in\gamma
.
Thus,
x+w=y+z
from the fact
x+w,y+z\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta_{\varphi})
and the condition (3) in Definition
3.1
.
Therefore, from the following equation, we have
x^{\prime}=y^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(x)

\displaystyle=(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(x+w)

\displaystyle=(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(y+z)

\displaystyle=(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(y)

\displaystyle=y^{\prime}

(4)

For any
\tau^{\prime}\in\Delta[\sigma]
, we show that
O_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap(Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}})\neq\emptyset
.
Let
\tau\in\Delta
be a cone such that
\sigma\subset\tau
and
\pi^{\sigma}_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}
.
Then
O_{\tau^{\prime}}
is equal to
O_{\tau}
, so that
O_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap(Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}})\neq\emptyset
from the condition (4) in Definition
3.1
.

(5)

Let
\Delta[\sigma](\varphi)
denote the following strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
(N[\sigma]/N[\sigma]_{\varphi})\otimes\mathbb{R}
:

\Delta[\sigma](\varphi)=\{(q^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau^{\prime})\mid\tau^{\prime}\in\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}\}

Then, we show that the following composition

\iota^{\sigma}_{\varphi}
is an open immersion:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}})\cap X(\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta[\sigma](\varphi)),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{*}}

where the former one is a closed immersion restricted to
(Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}})\cap X(\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi})
.

However, the proof of the above statement is very complicated, so we go through 4 steps.

Step.1

The codomain of
\iota^{\sigma}_{\varphi}
is a toric variety, so it is enough to show that for any open affine sub toric variety
V\subset X(\Delta[\sigma](\varphi))
, the restriction
\iota^{\sigma}_{\varphi}|_{(\iota^{\sigma}_{\varphi})^{-1}(V)}
is an open immersion.
In this step, we show that for any
\tau^{\prime\prime}\in\Delta[\sigma](\varphi)
, there unique exists
\tau\in\Delta_{\varphi}
such that
\sigma\subset\tau
and
\tau^{\prime\prime}=(q^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}\circ(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)
and all squares of the following diagram are Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\cap(\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau)}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}})\cap X((\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X((\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau^{\prime\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}})\cap X(\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta[\sigma](\varphi))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\sigma}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{*}}

Indeed, from the argument in (3), there unique exists
\tau^{\prime}\in\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi}
such that
(q^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau^{\prime})=\tau^{\prime\prime}
.
From Corollary
8.9
(a), there exists unique
\tau\in\Delta
such that
\sigma\subset\tau
,
(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}
, and
\tau\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Therefore
\tau
is a unique cone in
\Delta_{\varphi}
such that
\sigma\subset\tau
and
\tau^{\prime\prime}=(q^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}\circ(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)
.

From the injectivity of the restriction
(q^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta[\sigma]_{\varphi})}
, the lower right square of the above diagram is a Cartesian square.
All squares on the left side are Cartesian squares because they are given only by a pullback from a closed immersion
\iota^{\sigma}\colon Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}}\hookrightarrow X(\Delta[\sigma])
.

Step.2

We use the notation in Step.1 and proof of confirming the condition (5).
In this step, we show that the following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}\cap X(\tau_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X((\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau^{\prime\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\sigma}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{*}}

where
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
and
\tau_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)
.

Indeed, the upper square is commutative from Lemma
8.3
(e), and the lower one is commutative because
q^{\sigma}_{\varphi}\circ\pi^{\sigma}=\pi^{\sigma}_{\varphi}\circ q_{\varphi}
.

Step.3

In this step, we show that the right vertical morphism in the diagram in Step.2 from
\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}\cap X(\tau_{\varphi})
to
X(\tau^{\prime\prime})
is isomorphic.
From the definition of
\pi^{\sigma}_{\varphi}
, we have
\ker(\pi^{\sigma}_{\varphi})=((\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)+N_{\varphi})/N_{\varphi}
.
Thus,
\ker(\pi^{\sigma}_{\varphi})=q_{\varphi}(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)
.

Now, we claim that
q_{\varphi}(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)=\langle\sigma_{\varphi}\rangle\cap N/N_{\varphi}
.
One direction is obvious, so we only check that
\langle\sigma_{\varphi}\rangle\cap N/N_{\varphi}\subset q_{\varphi}(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)
.
Indeed, for any
x\in\langle(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)\rangle\cap N/N_{\varphi}
, there exists
y\in\langle\sigma\rangle
such that
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(y)=x
.
Moreover, from the condition (1) in Definition
3.1
, Proposition
3.2
, and Lemma
8.2
, there exists a section
s
of
q_{\varphi}
such that
s\circ q_{\varphi}|_{\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N}
is an identity map of a lattice
\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N
.
Thus, from the construction of
s
,
s_{\mathbb{R}}(x)=y
.

On the other hand,
x\in N/N_{\varphi}
, so that
s_{\mathbb{R}}(x)=s(x)=y\in N
.
Thus,
x=q_{\varphi}(y)\in(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)
.

We already checked that
\ker(\pi^{\sigma}_{\varphi})=\langle(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)\rangle\cap N/N_{\varphi}
.
Moreover, We recall that
(\pi^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau_{\varphi})=\tau^{\prime\prime}
.
Hence, from the proof of Proposition
8.8
(d), the morphism from
\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}\cap X(\tau_{\varphi})
to
X(\tau^{\prime\prime})
in the diagram in Step.2 is an isomorphism.

Step.4

In this step, we show that the lower morphism from
(Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}})\cap X((\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau))
to
X(\tau^{\prime\prime})
in the following diagram is an open immersion:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\cap(\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}\cap X(\tau_{\varphi})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}})\cap X((\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X((\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau^{\prime\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\sigma}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\sigma}_{\varphi})_{*}}

From Proposition
8.8
(d), the vertical middle morphism from
\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau)
to
X(\pi^{\sigma}(\tau))
is isomorphic.
Hence, from Step.1, the left vertical morphism from
Z\cap(\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau))
to
(Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}})\cap X((\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau))
is isomorphic too.
Thus, it is enough to show that the morphism from
Z\cap(\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau))
to
X(\tau^{\prime\prime})
in the above diagram is an open immersion.
Moreover, it is enough to show that the upper morphism from
Z\cap(\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau))
to
\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}\cap X(\tau_{\varphi})
is an open immersion because the right vertical morphism from
\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}\cap X(\tau_{\varphi})
to
X(\tau^{\prime\prime})
is isomorphic from Step.3.

By the way, we already claimed that this morphism is an open immersion in the proof of Proposition
3.7
because
(\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau))\times_{X(\tau)}Z(\tau)=Z\cap(\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau))
.
Therefore, the proof is completed.

∎

4.2.
The mock toric structure induced by dominant toric morphisms

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
and
(N
,
\Delta
,
\iota
,
\Phi
,
\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
,
\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
.

Let
\pi\colon N^{\prime}\rightarrow N
be a surjective group morphism of lattices of finite rank.
Let
\Delta^{\prime}
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
, and we assume that
\pi
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\prime}
and
\Delta
.
Let
Z^{\prime}
denote the pull back of the following diagram and
\iota^{\prime}\colon Z^{\prime}\rightarrow X(\Delta^{\prime})
be a closed immersion:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

In this subsection, we construct a mock toric structure of
Z^{\prime}
.

Definition 4.4
.

We use the above notation.
Let
s\colon N\hookrightarrow N^{\prime}
be a section of
\pi
.
For
\varphi\in\Phi
, let
N^{\prime}_{\varphi}
denote
s(N_{\varphi})
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}
denote the subset of
\Delta^{\prime}
defined as follows:

\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}=\{\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}\mid\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta_{\varphi})\}

We can check that
\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}
is a sub fan of
\Delta^{\prime}
.

The following proposition shows that
Z^{\prime}
has a mock toric structure.

Proposition 4.5
.

We keep the notation in Definition
4.4
.
Then
(N^{\prime},

\Delta^{\prime},

\iota^{\prime},

\Phi,

\{N^{\prime}_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi},

\{\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
is a mock toric structure of
Z^{\prime}
.
We call that
Z^{\prime}
is a mock toric variety induced by
Z
along
\pi_{*}\colon X(\Delta^{\prime})\rightarrow X(\Delta)
and
s
.

Proof.

We check that this tuple satisfies the conditions in Definition
3.1
from (1) to (5) in order.

(1)

For any
\varphi\in\Phi
and
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}
, we show that
N^{\prime}/((\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle\cap N^{\prime})+s(N_{\varphi}))
is a torsion-free module.
Let
x^{\prime}\in N^{\prime}
be an element and
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
be a positive integer.
We assume that
nx^{\prime}\in(\langle\tau\rangle\cap N^{\prime})+s(N_{\varphi})
.
Then there exists
y^{\prime}\in\langle\tau\rangle\cap N^{\prime}
and
z\in N_{\varphi}
such that
nx^{\prime}=y^{\prime}+s(z)
.
This shows that
n\pi(x^{\prime})=\pi(y^{\prime})+z
.
Because
\pi(y^{\prime})\in\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N
and
z\in N_{\varphi}
, there exists
y\in\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N
and
w\in N_{\varphi}
such that
\pi(x^{\prime})=y+w
from the condition (1) in Definition
3.1
.
Moreover, from Proposition
3.2
(b),
z=n\cdot w
.
This shows that
nx^{\prime}=y^{\prime}+ns(w)
and
x^{\prime}-s(w)\in\langle\tau\rangle\cap N^{\prime}
.
Thus,
x^{\prime}=(x^{\prime}-s(w))+s(w)\in(\langle\tau\rangle\cap N^{\prime})+s(N_{\varphi})
, and the statement follows.

(2)

We show the following equation holds:

\Delta^{\prime}=\bigcup_{\varphi\in\Phi}\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}.

For any
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}
, there exists
\sigma\in\Delta
such that
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma
.
Thus, from the condition (2) in Definition
3.1
, there exists
\varphi\in\Phi
such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Therefore, from the definition of
\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}
, we have
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}
.

(3)

For
\varphi\in\Phi
, let
q^{\prime}_{\varphi}
denote the quotient morphism
N^{\prime}\rightarrow N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi})
.
We show that the map
(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})}
is injective.
Let
x^{\prime}
and
y^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})
be elements, and assume that
(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(x^{\prime})=(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(y^{\prime})
.
We show that
x^{\prime}=y^{\prime}
.
From the assumption, there exists
z\in(N_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}=s_{\mathbb{R}}(z)
.
In particular,
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(x^{\prime})-\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(y^{\prime})=z
.
From the definition of
\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}
,
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(x^{\prime})
and
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(y^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta_{\varphi})
, so that
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(x^{\prime})=\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(y^{\prime})
from the equation
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(x^{\prime}))=(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(y^{\prime}))
and the condition (3) in Definition
3.1
.
Thus, it follows that
z=0
and
x^{\prime}=y^{\prime}
.

(4)

For any
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}
, we show that
Z^{\prime}\cap O_{\tau}\neq\emptyset
.
There exists
\sigma\in\Delta
such that
\sigma
is the smallest cone in
\Delta
which contains
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)
.
In particular,
\pi_{*}(O_{\tau})\subset O_{\sigma}
.
Because
\pi
is surjective,
\pi_{*}(O_{\tau})=O_{\sigma}
.
From the condition (4) in Definition
3.1
,
Z\cap O_{\sigma}\neq\emptyset
.
Thus, from the definition of
Z^{\prime}
,
Z^{\prime}\cap O_{\tau}\neq\emptyset
too.

(5)

Let
\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)
denote the following strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
(N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi}))\otimes\mathbb{R}
:

\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)=\{(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\mid\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}\}

Then we show that the following composition
\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi}
is an open immersion:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{*}}

where the former is a closed immersion restricted to
Z^{\prime}\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})
.
The codomain of
\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi}
is a toric variety, so it is enough to show that for any open affine sub toric variety
V\subset X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi))
, the restriction
\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi}|_{(\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi})^{-1}(V)}
is an open immersion.
In particular, from the definition of
\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)
, it is enough to show that the following morphism is an open immersion for any
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}\cap X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau_{\varphi})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{*}}

where
\tau_{\varphi}
denotes
(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)
.
Let
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
be a cone such as
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma
and
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
There exists a unique lattice morphism
\pi_{\varphi}\colon N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi})\rightarrow N/N_{\varphi}
such that
\pi_{\varphi}\circ q^{\prime}_{\varphi}=q_{\varphi}\circ\pi
.
Moreover, from Lemma
8.5
(a) and (b), the right square of the following diagram is commutative and a Cartesian square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}\cap X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\cap X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma_{\varphi})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}

where
(\pi_{\varphi})_{*}
is a toric morphism induced by
\pi_{\varphi}
.
On the other hand, the left square of the above diagram is a Cartesian square from the definition of
Z^{\prime}
, so the largest square of the above diagram is a Cartesian square.
From the condition (5) in Definition
3.1
, the lower morphism from
Z\cap X(\sigma)
to
X(\sigma_{\varphi})
in the above diagram is an open immersion, so that
\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi}
is an open immersion too.

∎

Corollary 4.6
.

We keep the notation in Definition
4.4
.
Let
\mu
denote the restriction
(\pi_{*})|_{Z^{\prime}}\colon Z^{\prime}\rightarrow Z
.
Then,
\mu
is a dominant morphism.

Proof.

From the definition of
Z^{\prime}
, there exists the following Cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

Because
\pi_{*}\colon T_{N^{\prime}}\rightarrow T_{N}
is an algebraic torus fibration, the restriction
\mu|_{Z^{\prime\circ}}
is so.
In particular,
\mu
is dominant.
∎

4.3.
Valuations of mock toric varieties

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
and
(N
,
\Delta
,
\iota
,
\Phi
,
\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
,
\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
.
We can regard a lattice point in
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)
as a torus invariant discrete valuation on
X(\Delta)
.
In this subsection, we try to induce discrete valuations on
Z
from
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
.
In general, a valuation ring does not induce a valuation ring of a closed subscheme.
However,
Z
is covered by open subschemes of toric varieties, and we can induce valuations on
Z
from valuations on
X(\Delta)
.

Let
W
be a variety over
k
and
\operatorname{Val}_{k}(W)
be a set of all discrete valuations on
W
, which is trivial on
k
.
We define a map
\operatorname{val}_{Z}\colon\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N\rightarrow\operatorname{Val}_{k}(Z)
as follows:
For any
w\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
, from the condition (4) in Definition
3.1
, there exists
\varphi\in\Phi
such that
w\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta_{\varphi})
.
Then
q_{\varphi}(w)
can be regarded as a discrete valuation on
X(\Delta(\varphi))
, which is trivial on
k
.
The morphism
\iota_{\varphi}\colon Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})\rightarrow X(\Delta(\varphi))
is an open immersion, so we define
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(w)
as
(\iota_{\varphi}^{-1})_{\natural}(q_{\varphi}(w))
.

Proposition 4.7
.

For the above construction of
\operatorname{val}_{Z}
, the following statements follow:

(a)

The definition of
\operatorname{val}_{Z}
is well-defined, i.e., the definition is independent of the choice of
\varphi\in\Phi
.

(b)

For any
v\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
, there unique exists
\sigma\in\Delta
such that
v\in\sigma^{\circ}
.
Then the valuation ring associated with
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)
dominates the local ring of the generic point of
Z_{\sigma}
.

(c)

The map
\operatorname{val}_{Z}
is injective.

Proof.

We prove these statements from (a) to (c) in order.

(a)

We divide the proof of (a) into three steps.

Step.1

Let
\operatorname{Ray}(\Delta)
denote a subset of
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
as follows:

\operatorname{Ray}(\Delta)=\{w\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N\mid\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\cdot w\in\Delta\}

In this step, we show that for any
v\in\operatorname{Ray}(\Delta)
, the definition of
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)
is well-defined.
Let
v\in\operatorname{Ray}(\Delta)
be an element.
If
v=0
, for any
\varphi
, we have
v=0\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta_{\varphi})
.
However,
(\iota_{\varphi}^{-1})_{\natural}(q_{\varphi}(v))
is a trivial valuation on
Z
, so we may assume that
v\neq 0
.
Let
\gamma
be a 1-dimensional cone generated by
v
, so
\gamma\in\Delta
from the definition of
\operatorname{Ray}(\Delta)
.
Then from Proposition
3.4
(d),
\dim(Z_{\gamma})=\dim(Z)-1
.
Besides that, from Proposition
3.6
(c),
\overline{Z_{\sigma}}
is a prime Weil divisor of a normal variety
Z
.
For any
\varphi\in\Phi
such that
\gamma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
, let
\gamma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\gamma)
.
Then
q_{\varphi}(v)
is a discrete valuation of the local ring of the generic point of
O_{\gamma_{\varphi}}
.
Thus, because of the proof of Proposition
3.4
(c),
(\iota_{\varphi}^{-1})_{\natural}(q_{\varphi}(v))
is a discrete valuation of the local ring of the generic point of
\overline{Z_{\gamma}}
.
Therefore, it is enough to show that for any
\varphi\in\Phi
such that
\gamma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
, the following subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}
is independent of the choice of
\varphi\in\Phi
:

\{q_{\varphi}(v)(g)\mid g\in K(X(\Delta(\varphi)))^{*}\}

Because
q_{\varphi}(v)
is a torus invariant valuation on
X(\Delta(\varphi))
, the above set is equal to
q_{\varphi}(v)(M_{\varphi})
, where
M_{\varphi}
denotes the dual lattice of
N/N_{\varphi}
.
Let
(q_{\varphi})^{*}
denote a morphism
M_{\varphi}\rightarrow M
induced by
q_{\varphi}
.
Then for any
\omega^{\prime}\in M_{\varphi}
, the following equation holds:

\displaystyle\langle q_{\varphi}(v),\omega^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle v,(q_{\varphi})^{*}(\omega^{\prime})\rangle

This indicates that
q_{\varphi}(v)(M_{\varphi})\subset v(M)
.
On the other hand, from Lemma
8.2
, there exists a section
s
of
q_{\varphi}\colon N\rightarrow N/N_{\varphi}
such that an image of
s
contains
v
.
In particular,
s\circ q_{\varphi}(v)=v
.
Thus, for any
\omega\in M
, the following equation holds:

\displaystyle\langle v,\omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle s\circ q_{\varphi}(v),\omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle q_{\varphi}(v),s^{*}(\omega)\rangle

This shows that
v(M)\subset q_{\varphi}(v)(M_{\varphi})
.
Hence,
v(M)=q_{\varphi}(v)(M_{\varphi})
, so that the definition of
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)
is independent of the choice of
\varphi\in\Phi
.

Step.2

Let
\Delta^{\prime}
be a refinement of
\Delta
,
\lambda
denote an identity map of
N
,
Z^{\prime}
denote a mock toric variety induced by
Z
along
\lambda_{*}\colon X(\Delta^{\prime})\rightarrow X(\Delta)
and
\lambda^{-1}
,
\iota^{\prime}
denote the closed immersion
Z^{\prime}\rightarrow X(\Delta^{\prime})
, and
\mu
denote a morphism
Z^{\prime}\rightarrow Z
.
In this step, we show that for any
w\in\operatorname{Ray}(\Delta^{\prime})
, the definition of
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(w)
is well-defined.

Let
0\neq w\in\operatorname{Ray}(\Delta^{\prime})
be an element,
\gamma^{\prime}
denote a 1-dimensional cone generated by
w
, and
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
w=\lambda(w)\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta_{\varphi})
.
Then
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}
.
From the proof of Proposition
4.5
, the following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Z^{\prime}}^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N/N_{\varphi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N/N_{\varphi}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\lambda_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}

where
(\lambda_{\varphi})_{*}
is a toric morphism induced by an identitiy map
\lambda_{\varphi}\colon N/N_{\varphi}\rightarrow N/N_{\varphi}
and
\mu
denote
\lambda_{*}|_{Z^{\prime\circ}}
.
Because the toric morphism
\lambda_{*}
is an identity morphism of
T_{N}
,
\mu
is an identity morphism, and the induced map
\mu_{\natural}\colon\operatorname{Val}_{k}(Z^{\prime})\rightarrow\operatorname{Val}_{k}(Z)
is an identity map too.
From the above diagram, Lemma
8.31
, and the equation
w=\lambda(w)
, we have
(\iota^{-1}_{\varphi})_{\natural}(q_{\varphi}(v))=\mu_{\natural}(\operatorname{val}_{Z^{\prime}}(w))
.
Thus, the definition of
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(w)
is independent of the choice of
\varphi\in\Phi
such that
w\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta_{\varphi})
.

Step.3

In this step, for general
w\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
, the definition of
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(w)
is well-defined.
Let
w\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
be an element.
Then there exists a refinement
\Delta^{\prime}
of
\Delta
such that
w\in\operatorname{Ray}(\Delta^{\prime})
.
Thus, from the argument in Step.2, the definition of
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(w)
is well-defined.

(b)

From the proof of Proposition
3.4
(c) and the definition of
\operatorname{val}_{Z}
, we can check the statement easily.

(c)

Let
v
and
v^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
be elements such that
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v^{\prime})
.
Then, from (b), there exists
\sigma\in\Delta
such that
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)
is a valuation ring that dominates a local ring of generic point of
Z_{\sigma}
.
In particular,
v
and
v^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\circ}
.
Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Then from the definition of
\operatorname{val}_{Z}
,
(\iota^{-1}_{\varphi})_{\natural}(q_{\varphi}(v))=(\iota^{-1}_{\varphi})_{\natural}(q_{\varphi}(v^{\prime}))
, in particular,
q_{\varphi}(v)=q_{\varphi}(v^{\prime})
.
Therefore, from the condition (3) in Definition
3.1
, we have
v=v^{\prime}
.

∎

For any
\sigma\in\Delta
,
Z(\sigma)
is an affine scheme from Proposition
3.6
(b).
The normal affine schme
Z(\sigma)
contains an affine scheme
Z^{\circ}
as an open subscheme.
The following proposition shows that the global section ring of
Z(\sigma)
is characterized by the valuations
\{\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)\mid v\in\sigma\cap N\}
like as affine toric varieties.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone.
We can regard
\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
as a subring of
\Gamma(Z^{\circ},\mathscr{O}_{Z})=k[Z^{\circ}]
.
Then the following equation holds:

\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})=\{f\in k[Z^{\circ}]\mid\operatorname{val}_{Z}(w)(f)\geq 0.\quad(\forall w\in\sigma\cap N)\}

Proof.

First, we show that the right-hand side of the above equation contains
\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
.
For any
v\in\sigma\cap N
, there exists a face
\tau\preceq\sigma
such that
v\in\tau^{\circ}
.
From Proposition
4.7
(b), The valuation ring of
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)
dominates the local ring of the generic point of
Z_{\tau}
.
Moreover, from Proposition
3.6
(a),
Z_{\tau}\subset Z(\sigma)
.
Thus, for any
f\in\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
, we have
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(f)\geq 0
.

Next, we show that
\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
contains the right-hand side of the above equation.
Let
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
be a polynomial such that
f
is contained in the right-hand side of the above equation and let
W
denote
Z(\sigma)\setminus Z^{\circ}
.
For showing
f\in\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
, it is enough to show that
f\in R
for all local rings
R
of the generic points of prime Weil divisors of
Z
in
W
, because
Z(\sigma)
is a normal affine scheme of finite type over
k
.
From the argument of the toric varieties and Proposition
3.4
(d) and Proposition
3.6
(a), the set of all prime Weil divisors in
W
is the following set:

\{\overline{Z_{\gamma}}\mid\gamma\preceq\sigma,\dim(\gamma)=1\}

Thus, from Proposition
4.7
(b), for each prime Weil divisor
D
of
Z
in
W
, there exists
v_{D}\in\sigma\cap N
such that
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v_{D})
is a valuation of the local ring
R_{D}
of the generic point of
D
.
Therefore, from the assumption of
f
, we have
f\in R_{D}
.
∎

Let
\omega\in M
be an element.
The following lemma shows that we can compute
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\iota^{*}(\chi^{\omega}))
briefly for any
v\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
v\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
and
\omega\in M
be elements.
Then
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\iota^{*}(\chi^{\omega}))=\langle v,\omega\rangle
.

Proof.

There exists
\varphi\in\Phi
and
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
such that
v\in\sigma
.
From Lemma
8.2
, there exists a section
s
of
q_{\varphi}\colon N\rightarrow N/N_{\varphi}
such that
\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N\subset s(N/N_{\varphi})
.
Let
s^{*}
denotes the morphism
M\rightarrow M_{\varphi}
induced by
s
, and
\omega^{\prime}\in M_{\varphi}
denotes
s^{*}(\omega)
.
Then
\omega-q^{*}_{\varphi}(\omega^{\prime})\in\ker(s^{*})
holds.
Let
v^{\prime}\in\sigma\cap N
be an element.
Then there exists
w^{\prime}\in N/N_{\varphi}
such that
s(w^{\prime})=v^{\prime}
.
Thus, there exists the following equation:

\displaystyle\langle v^{\prime},\omega-q^{*}_{\varphi}(\omega^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle s(w^{\prime}),\omega-q^{*}_{\varphi}(\omega^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle w^{\prime},s^{*}(\omega-q^{*}_{\varphi}(\omega^{\prime}))\rangle

\displaystyle=0

Hence, the equation above indicates that
\chi^{\omega-q^{*}_{\varphi}(\omega^{\prime})}\in\Gamma(X(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{X(\Delta)})^{*}
.
In particular,
\iota^{*}(\chi^{\omega-q^{*}_{\varphi}(\omega^{\prime})})\in\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})^{*}
.
Thus, the following equation holds:

\displaystyle\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\iota^{*}(\chi^{\omega}))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\iota^{*}(\chi^{q^{*}_{\varphi}(\omega^{\prime})}))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle=\langle q_{\varphi}(v),\omega^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle v,q^{*}_{\varphi}(\omega^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle v,\omega\rangle

∎

The base change of the field extension preserves the toric structure.
The following proposition shows that the mock toric structure is preserved by the base change of the field extension, too.

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
L/K
be a field extension.
Let
Z
be a scheme over
K
and
(N
,
\Delta
,
\iota
,
\Phi
,
\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
,
\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
.
Let
Z_{L}
denote a scheme over
L
defined as the following Cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{L}(\Delta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(L)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{K}(\Delta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}({K})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

where
\alpha
denote the morphism
Z_{L}\rightarrow Z
and
\iota_{L}
denote the closed immersion
Z_{L}\rightarrow X_{L}(\Delta)
.

Then the following statements hold:

(a)

The tuple
(N
,
\Delta
,
\iota_{L}
,
\Phi
,
\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
,
\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
is a mock toric structure of
Z_{L}
.

(b)

A morphism
\alpha
is a dominant morphism.

(c)

The following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Val}_{L}(Z_{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Val}_{K}(Z)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{val}_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{val}_{Z_{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\natural}}

Proof.

We prove these statements from (a) to (c) in order.

(a)

Because closed and open immersions are stable under a base change, we can check it easily.

(b)

Because
\operatorname{Spec}(L)\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(K)
is surjective,
\alpha
is surjective too.

(c)

There exists the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{L}\cap X_{L}(\Delta_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{L}(\Delta_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{L}(\Delta(\varphi))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\cap X_{K}(\Delta_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{K}(\Delta_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{K}(\Delta(\varphi))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*,L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}

where
\beta
and
\beta_{\varphi}
are morphisms induced by a base change along
\operatorname{Spec}(L)\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(K)
.
We remark that
\beta
and
\beta_{\varphi}
are dominant.
Let
v\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
be an element and
v_{L}
denote a torus invariant valuation of
T_{N,L}
which is trivial on
L
associated with
v\in N
.
Then
\beta_{\natural}(v_{L})=v
.
Thus, the statement follows from Lemma
8.31
.

∎

4.4.
Extended valuation
