We use the notation in the subsection 4.2.
In subsection 4.3, we define the valuation
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)
for a mock toric variety
Z
and any
v\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
.
Let
Z^{\prime}\rightarrow Z
be a morphism of mock toric varieties where the mock toric structure of
Z^{\prime}
is induced by that of
Z
along a dominant toric morphism
X(\Delta^{\prime})\rightarrow X(\Delta)
.
Similarly, we can define the valuation
\operatorname{val}_{Z^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})
for any
v^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime})\cap N^{\prime}
.

In this subsection, we show that
Z^{\prime}
has more valuations induced by
Z
.
In the case of toric varieties, the above statement can be explained as follows:
We assume that
N=0
and
\Delta^{\prime}=\{0\}
.
Then we have
Z=\operatorname{Spec}(k)
and
Z^{\prime}=T_{N^{\prime}}
.
Although
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime})
is a point, we know there exist infinite torus invariant valuations on
T_{N^{\prime}}
, which correspond to the lattice points in
N^{\prime}
.
Thus, this subsection aims to define valuations associated with lattice points outside
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime})\cap N^{\prime}
.

Definition 4.11
.

We use the following notation:

•

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
and
(N
,
\Delta
,
\iota
,
\Phi
,
\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
,
\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
.

•

Let
N^{\prime}
be a lattice of finite rank,
\pi\colon N^{\prime}\rightarrow N
be a surjective map, and
s
be a section of
\pi
.
Let
M^{\prime}
denote the dual lattice of
N^{\prime}
.

•

Let
N_{1}
denote
N^{\prime}/s(N)
and
p
denote the quotient map
N^{\prime}\rightarrow N_{1}
.
We remark that
\pi\times p\colon N^{\prime}\rightarrow N\oplus N_{1}
is an isomorphism.

•

Let
M_{1}
denote the dual lattice of
N_{1}
.
We remark that
\pi^{*}\oplus p^{*}\colon M\oplus M_{1}\rightarrow M^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.

•

Let
p_{\varphi}
and
\pi_{\varphi}
denote unique group morphisms such that the following diagrams commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\varphi}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N/N_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\varphi}}

•

Let
\Delta_{Z,\pi}
denote a strongly convex rational polyhedral cone
\{\{0_{N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}}\}\}
in
N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

•

Let
Z_{\pi}
denote the mock toric variety induced by
Z
along
\pi_{*}\colon X(\Delta_{Z,\pi})\rightarrow X(\Delta)
and
s
.

•

Let
\iota^{\prime}
denote the closed immersion
Z_{\pi}\rightarrow X(\Delta_{Z,\pi})
and
\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi}
denote a composition
(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{*}\circ\iota^{\prime}
.

•

Let
\epsilon
denote
Z_{\pi}\rightarrow Z
induced by
\pi_{*}
.
We remark that
\epsilon
is a dominant morphism from Corollary
4.6
.

•

Let
\epsilon^{*}
denote a ring morphism
k[Z^{\circ}]\rightarrow k[Z_{\pi}]
induced by
\epsilon
.

•

Let
p_{0}
denote the composition
p_{*}\circ\iota^{\prime}\colon Z_{\pi}\rightarrow T_{N_{1}}
.
Let
p^{*}_{0}
denote a ring morphism
k[M_{1}]\rightarrow k[Z_{\pi}]
induced by
p_{0}
.

Proposition 4.12
.

For the notation in Definition
4.11
, We define a map
\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}\colon(\pi_{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))\cap N^{\prime}\rightarrow\operatorname{Val}_{k}(Z_{\pi})
as follows:
For
w\in(\pi_{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))\cap N^{\prime}
, from the condition (4) in Definition
3.1
, there exists
\varphi\in\Phi
such that
w\in(\pi_{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta_{\varphi}))
.
Then
q^{\prime}_{\varphi}(w)
can be regarded as a discrete valuation on
X(\Delta_{Z,\pi}(\varphi))=T_{N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi})}
which is trivial on
k
.
The morphism
\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi}
is an open immersion, so we define an
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(w)
as
({\iota^{\prime}}_{\varphi}^{-1})_{\natural}(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}(w))
.

Then the following statements hold:

(a)

The definition of
\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}
is well-defined.

(b)

Let
\Delta^{\prime}
denote a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
We assume that
\pi
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\prime}
and
\Delta
.
Let
Z^{\prime}
denote the mock toric variety induced by
Z
along
\pi_{*}\colon X(\Delta^{\prime})\rightarrow X(\Delta)
and
s
.
Then
\operatorname{val}_{Z^{\prime}}=\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}|_{\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime})\cap N^{\prime}}
.

(c)

For any
\chi^{\prime}\in k[Z_{\pi}]^{*}
, there exists
\eta\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
and
\omega\in M_{1}
such that
\chi^{\prime}=\epsilon^{*}(\eta){p^{*}_{0}}(\chi^{\omega})
.

(d)

For any
v^{\prime}\in(\pi_{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))\cap N^{\prime}
,
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
, and
g\in k[M_{1}]
, the following equations hold:

\displaystyle\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v^{\prime})(\epsilon^{*}(f))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(\pi(v^{\prime}))(f)

\displaystyle\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v^{\prime})(p^{*}_{0}(g))

\displaystyle=p(v^{\prime})(g)

Proof.

We prove the statements from (a) to (d) in order.

(a)

Let
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
denote a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime\prime})=(\pi_{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))
and
\pi
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
and
\Delta
.
Let
W
denote a mock toric variety induced by
Z
along
\pi_{*}\colon X(\Delta^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow X(\Delta)
and
s
.
Let
w\in\pi^{-1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))\cap N^{\prime}_{+}
and
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\pi(w)\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta_{\varphi})
.
We show that
w\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{\varphi})
.
Indeed, there exists
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime\prime}
such that
w\in\tau^{\circ}
because
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime\prime})=(\pi_{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))
.
From the assumption of
\pi
, there exists
\sigma\in\Delta
such that
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma
.
On the other hand, there exists
\gamma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
such that
\pi(w)\in\gamma
.
Thus,
\sigma\cap\gamma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
and
\sigma\cap\gamma
is a face of
\sigma
.
Because
w\in\tau^{\circ}
,
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma\cap\gamma
.
Hence,
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{\varphi}
.
Thus,
({\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi}}^{-1})_{\natural}(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}(w))=\operatorname{val}_{W}(w)
from the definition of
\operatorname{val}_{W}
.
This equation shows that the definition of the
\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(w)
is independent of the choice of
\varphi\in\Phi
.

(b)

As the proof of (a), we can check that the statement holds.

(c)

There exists the following diagram whose all squares are Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{\pi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

Thus,
\epsilon^{*}\otimes{p^{*}_{0}}\colon k[Z^{\circ}]\otimes_{k}k[M_{1}]\rightarrow k[Z_{\pi}]
is isomorphic.
Because
k[Z]
is an integral domain, a unit in
k[Z_{\pi}]
can be decomposed into a product of that in
k[Z^{\circ}]
and that in
k[M_{1}]
.

(d)

For any
\varphi\in\Phi
, there exists the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{\pi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N/N_{\varphi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}

Hence, from Lemma
8.31
, and the definition of
\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}
and
\operatorname{val}_{Z}
, we have
\epsilon_{\natural}(\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v^{\prime}))=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(\pi(v^{\prime}))
.
Thus,
\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v^{\prime})(\epsilon^{*}(f))=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(\pi(v^{\prime}))(f)
.
On the other hand, because
p^{*}(g)=(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})^{*}(p_{\varphi})^{*}(g)
, the following equation holds:

\displaystyle\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v^{\prime})(p^{*}_{0}(g))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v^{\prime})({\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi}}^{*}({p_{\varphi}}^{*}(g)))

\displaystyle=((q^{\prime}_{\varphi})(v^{\prime}))({p_{\varphi}}^{*}(g))

\displaystyle=p(v^{\prime})(g)

∎

As Lemma
4.9
, the following corollary holds.

Corollary 4.13
.

We keep the notation of Proposition
4.12
.
Let
w\in(\pi_{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))\cap N^{\prime}
be an element and
\omega^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}
be an element.
Then
\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(w)({\iota^{\prime}}^{*}(\chi^{\omega^{\prime}}))=\langle w,\omega^{\prime}\rangle
holds.

Proof.

There exists
\omega\in M
and
\omega_{1}\in M_{1}
such that
\pi^{*}(\omega)+p^{*}(\omega_{1})=\omega^{\prime}
.
In particular,
\chi^{\omega^{\prime}}=\pi^{*}(\chi^{\omega})p^{*}(\chi^{\omega_{1}})
and
{\iota^{\prime}}^{*}(\chi^{\omega^{\prime}})=\epsilon^{*}(\iota^{*}(\chi^{\omega}))p_{0}^{*}(\chi^{\omega_{1}})
hold.
From Proposition
4.12
(d) and Lemma
4.9
, the following two equations hold for any
w\in(\pi_{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))\cap N^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(w)(\epsilon^{*}(\iota^{*}(\chi^{\omega})))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(\pi(w))(\iota^{*}(\chi^{\omega}))

\displaystyle=\langle\pi(w),\omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle w,\pi^{*}(\omega)\rangle

\displaystyle\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(w)(p_{0}^{*}(\chi^{\omega_{1}}))

\displaystyle=p(w)(\chi^{\omega_{1}})

\displaystyle=\langle p(w),\omega_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle w,p^{*}(\omega_{1})\rangle

Thus,
\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(w)({\iota^{\prime}}^{*}(\chi^{\omega^{\prime}}))=\langle w,\omega^{\prime}\rangle
holds.
∎

4.5.
Mock toric varieties over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}

In this article, we construct the scheme over a valuation ring.
To construct a scheme over a valuation ring, we first construct a scheme over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
and subsequently carry out a base change.
From now on, we consider the mock toric varieties over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
in this subsection.
The mock toric variety over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
means the mock toric variety induced by a toric variety which has a dominant toric morphism to
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
.

Proposition 4.14
.

We use the following notation:

•

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
and
(N
,
\Delta
,
\iota
,
\Phi
,
\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
,
\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
.

•

Let
N^{\prime}
be a lattice of finite rank,
\pi
be a surjective morphism
\pi\colon N^{\prime}\rightarrow N
, and
\pi^{1}
denote
\pi\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}}\colon N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow N\oplus\mathbb{Z}
.

•

Let
s
be a section of
\pi
and
s^{1}
denote a section of
\pi^{1}
such that
s^{1}=s\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}}\colon N\oplus\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z}
.

•

For
\varphi\in\Phi
, let

denote the quotient map

and

denote the quotient map

.

•

Let

and

denote the first and second projection of

or

.

•

Let

denote a section of
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\colon N\oplus\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow N
such that
\mathrm{pr}_{2}\circ j=0
.

•

Let
j^{\prime}
denote a section of
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\colon N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow N^{\prime}
such that
\mathrm{pr}_{2}\circ j^{\prime}=0
.

•

Let
Z^{1}
denote a mock toric variety induced by

along
(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{*}\colon X(\Delta\times\Delta_{!})\rightarrow X(\Delta)
and
j
.

•

Let
\Delta^{\prime}
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
(N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z})_{\mathbb{R}}
.
We assume that
\pi^{1}
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\prime}
and
\Delta\times\Delta_{!}
.

•

Let
W
denote a mock toric variety induced by
Z^{1}
along

and
s^{1}
.

Let
\iota^{\prime\prime}
denote the closed immersion
W\rightarrow X(\Delta^{\prime})

•

Let
\Delta^{\prime}_{0}
denote the following sub fan of
\Delta^{\prime}
:

\Delta^{\prime}_{0}=\{\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}\mid(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)=\{0\}\}

•

Let
\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}
denote the following set:

\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}=\{(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)\mid\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}_{0}\}

From Lemma
8.10
(a),
\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}
is a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan.

Then the following statements follow:

(a)

The morphism
\pi
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}
and
\Delta
.
Let

denote a mock toric variety induced by

along

and
s
.

(b)

The toric morphism
(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{*}\times(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{*}\colon X(\Delta^{\prime}_{0})\rightarrow X(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)})\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}
is isomorphic from Lemma

(b).
This isomorphism induces an isomorphism
p\colon W\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{0})\rightarrow Y\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}
.
Let
\sigma\in\Delta_{0}
be a cone and
\sigma_{0}
denote
(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
Then, this isomorphism induces the following three Cartesian diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(\sigma_{0})\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{W_{\sigma}}\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{Y_{\sigma_{0}}}\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\sigma_{0}}\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

where the upper morphisms in all diagrams are the restriction of
p
, and the vertical morphisms are open immersions, closed immersions, and locally closed immersions from top to bottom, respectively.

(c)

Let
\Psi
denote the composition of the following maps:

, where the first morphism is a base change of the generic fiber

along

, and the third one is an open immersion.

Then,

is a dominant morphism.
Moreover, the large square of the following diagram is a Cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{k(t)}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(k(t))}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{*}\circ\iota^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{*}\circ\iota^{\prime\prime}}

We keep the notation in (b) and (c).
The morphism
\psi
induces the following three Cartesian diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{k(t)}(\sigma_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{k(t)}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{k(t)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

where the upper morphisms in all diagrams are the restriction of
\psi
, and the vertical morphisms are open immersions, closed immersions, and locally closed immersions from top to bottom, respectively.

(e)

Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element.
Then there exists a one-to-one correspondence of cones of

and those of

by

.
In particular,

is compatible with the fans

and

.

(f)

We keep the notation in (e).
Let
\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)_{0}
denote the following sub fan of

:

\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)_{0}=\{\sigma_{\varphi}\in\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)\mid(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma_{\varphi})=\{0\}\}

Then

is equal to the following set:

\{(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)\mid\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}_{0}\cap\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}\}

In particular,
q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\prime}_{0}\cap\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}
and
\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)_{0}
.

(g)

We keep the notation in (f).
Let
\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)_{(0)}
denote the following set:

\{(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma_{\varphi})\mid\sigma_{\varphi}\in\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)_{0}\}

Then
\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)_{(0)}=\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}(\varphi)
.
Moreover,
\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)_{(0)}
is a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in

.
Furthermore,
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\colon N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi})\oplus\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi})
is compatible with the fans

and

.

(h)

The toric morphism
(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{*}\times(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{*}\colon X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)_{0})\rightarrow X(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}(\varphi))\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}
is isomorphic from (f), (g), and Lemma
8.10
(b).
Let

denote this isomorphism and

denote the composition of the following maps:

, where the first morphism is a base change of the generic fiber

along

, the second one is the inverse morphism of

, and the third one is an open immersion.
Then the following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{k(t)}(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0),\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{k(t)}(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}(\varphi))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{*,k(t)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\varphi}}

(i)

We assume
\Delta^{\prime}
is
\{\{0\}\}
.
Then we can check that
W
is
Z^{1}_{\pi^{1}}
and
Y
is

.
Let
\mu
denote

and

denote

.
From (b),

induces the isomorphism

.
Thus, for any

, there exists

and an integer

such that

.

(j)

We keep the notation in (i).

,

, and

Then

.

(k)

The following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)})\cap N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime})\cap(N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Val}_{k(t)}(Y_{k(t)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Val}_{k}(W)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{val}_{Y_{k(t)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{val}_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\natural}}

Proof.

We prove these statements from (a) to (k) in order.

(a)

Let
\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}_{0}
be a cone and
\sigma_{0}
denote
(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
Then
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma_{0})=(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}(\pi^{1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma))
.
From the definition of
\Delta^{\prime}_{0}
, we have
\pi^{1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)\subset\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\times\{0\}
.
Because
\pi^{1}
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\prime}
and
\Delta\times\Delta_{!}
, there exists
\tau\in\Delta
such that
\pi^{1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)\subset\tau\times\{0\}
.
Thus,
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma_{0})\subset\tau
.

(b)

There exists the following diagram of toric morphisms:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta\times\{\{0\}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{1}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}

In particular, all small squares in the above diagram are Cartesian squares.
Thus, from the definition of the mock toric structure of
Y
and
W
, all small squares in the following diagram are Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{1}\cap X(\Delta\times\{\{0\}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

where
\mu
denote
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\circ p
.
The latter part of the statement holds from Proposition
3.7
and Lemma
8.10
(b).

(c)

We show that
\psi
is dominant.
Indeed,
\psi
can be decomposed in the upper dominant morphisms in the following diagram from (b):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{k(t)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{k(t)}(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)})\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{-1}}

We remark that all squares in the above diagram are Cartesian squares from (b).
Thus, from Lemma
8.10
(c), the latter part of the statement follows.

(d)

We can check it from Proposition
3.7
and Lemma
8.10
(d).

(e)

Let
\sigma\in\Delta_{0}
be a cone and
\sigma_{0}
denote
(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
From Lemma
8.1
,
(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}
induces a one-to-one correspondence of cones in
\Delta^{\prime}_{0}
and those in
\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}
.
Thus, we need to check that
\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}_{0}\cap\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}
if and only if
\sigma_{0}\in\Delta^{\prime}_{(0),\varphi}
.
We recall that the mock toric structure of
W
and
Y
are defined by
(\pi^{1})_{*}
and
s^{1}
, and
\pi_{*}
and
s
respectively.
Moreover, there exists the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N\oplus\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}}

Thus,
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma_{0})\subset\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta_{\varphi})
if and only if
\pi^{1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)\subset\operatorname{Supp}((\Delta\times\Delta_{!})_{\varphi})=\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta_{\varphi})\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.

(f)

We recall that
\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)
is the following set:

\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)=\{(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)\mid\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}\}

Thus, from the following commutative diagram, the statement holds:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi})\oplus\mathbb{Z}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{2}}

(g)

We recall that
\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}(\varphi)=\{(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\mid\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}_{(0),\varphi}\}
.
From (e), (f), and the equation
q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\circ\mathrm{pr}_{1}=\mathrm{pr}_{1}\circ(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}})
, we have
\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)_{(0)}=\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}(\varphi)
.
Moreover, we remark that
\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}(\varphi)
is strongly convex rational polyhedral fan from Proposition
3.2
(c).

(h)

There exists the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{k(t)}(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0),\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0),\varphi})\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}_{0}\cap\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{k(t)}(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}(\varphi))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}(\varphi))\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{*,k(t)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{*}\times\mathrm{id}_{{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\varphi}^{-1}}

Thus, from the definition of
\Psi
and
\Psi_{\varphi}
, the statement holds.

(i)

From the mock toric structure of
Z^{1}_{\pi^{1}}
and
Z_{\pi}
, we can recognize
Z^{1}_{\pi^{1}}
as a mock toric variety induced by
Z_{\pi}
along
(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{*}\colon T_{N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow T_{N^{\prime}}
and
j^{\prime}
.
We remark that we can identify with the second projection
\mathrm{pr}_{2}
of
N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z}
and the quotient map
N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow(N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z})/j^{\prime}(N^{\prime})
.
Thus, the statement follows from Proposition
4.12
(c).

(j)

Let
\Delta^{\flat}
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\flat})=(\pi_{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))
and
\pi
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\flat}
and
\Delta
.
Let
Y^{\flat}
denote a mock toric variety by
Z
along
\pi_{*}\colon X(\Delta^{\flat})\rightarrow X(\Delta)
and
s
.
Then from Proposition
4.12
(b),
\operatorname{val}_{Y^{\flat}}=\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}
.
On the other hand, we can recognize
Z^{1}_{\pi^{1}}
as a mock toric variety induced by
Y^{\flat}
along
(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{*}\colon T_{N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow X(\Delta^{\flat})
and
j^{\prime}
.
We remark that
{(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}}^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\flat}))={(\pi\circ\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}}^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))\supset{(\pi^{1})_{\mathbb{R}}}^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta\times\Delta_{!}))
so that
\operatorname{val}_{Y^{\flat},\mathrm{pr}_{1}}(v^{\prime})=\operatorname{val}_{Z^{1},\pi^{1}}(v^{\prime})
for any
v^{\prime}\in({(\pi_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}}^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta\times\Delta_{!})))\cap(N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z})
.
Thus, from the remark in the proof of (i) and Proposition
4.12
(d),
\operatorname{val}_{Z^{1},\pi^{1}}(v^{\prime})(\mu^{*}(f)p^{*}_{0}(g))=\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(\mathrm{pr}_{1}(v^{\prime}))(f)+\mathrm{pr}_{2}(v^{\prime})(g)=\operatorname{val}_{Y^{\flat}}(\mathrm{pr}_{1}(v^{\prime}))(f)+\mathrm{pr}_{2}(v^{\prime})(g)
for any
v^{\prime}\in({(\pi_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}}^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta\times\Delta_{!})))\cap(N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z})
.

(k)

Let
v^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)})\cap N^{\prime}
be an element.
We regard
v^{\prime}_{k(t)}
as a torus invariant valuation on
T_{N^{\prime},k(t)}
, which is trivial on
k(t)
.
Thus, we remark that
\Psi_{\natural}(v^{\prime})
is equal to
(v^{\prime},0)=j^{\prime}(v^{\prime})
as a torus invariant valuation on
T_{N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z}}
which is trivial on
k
from the construction of
\Psi
.
Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\pi(v^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta_{\varphi})
.
We remark that
(v^{\prime},0)\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})
.
From (h), there exists the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{k(t)}\cap X_{k(t)}(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0),\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{k(t)}(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0),\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{k(t)}(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}(\varphi))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{*,k(t)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{*}}

where two left horizontal morphisms are closed immersions, and we can check that both horizontal composition morphisms are open immersion from the condition (5) in Definition
3.1
.
Hence, from Lemma
8.31
, and the definition of
\operatorname{val}_{Y_{k(t)}}
and
\operatorname{val}_{W}
, we have
\psi_{\natural}(\operatorname{val}_{Y_{k(t)}}(v^{\prime}))=\operatorname{val}_{W}(\Psi_{\natural}(v^{\prime}))
.
Therefore, from the above remark, the statement follows.

∎

5.
Example of mock toric varieties

We defined mock toric variety in Section 3.
In this section, we give some new examples of mock toric varieties.
This new example is given by “base point free” hyperplane arrangements.
In particular, we give a new non-toric mock toric structure of projective spaces and show that every Del Pezzo surface has a mock toric structure.
In this section, We use the following notation:

•

Let
n
and
d
be positive integers, and
k
be a field.

•

Let
\mathcal{B}=\{f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{d}\}
denote a finite subset of
\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k},\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}}(1))\setminus\{0\}
.
We assume that
\mathcal{B}
generates
\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k},\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}}(1))
as a
k-
vector space.
We regard
f_{i}
as a homogeneous polynomial with
\deg(f_{i})=1
.

•

Let
\iota_{0}
be a rational map
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}\dashrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{d}_{k}
defined by
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}\ni a\mapsto[f_{0}(a):f_{1}(a):\cdots:f_{d}(a)]\in\mathbb{P}^{d}_{k}
.
Then
\iota_{0}
is a closed embedding by the assumption of
\mathcal{B}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{V}
denote the following set:

\mathcal{V}=\{V\subset\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k},\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}}(1))\mid V=\sum_{i;f_{i}\in V}k\cdot f_{i}\}

•

For
V\in\mathcal{V}
, let
\rho(V)
denote a set
\{0\leq i\leq d\mid f_{i}\in V\}
.

•

Let
\{e^{0},e^{1},\ldots,e^{d}\}
denote a canonical basis of
\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}
, and
\mathbf{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}
be
\sum_{0\leq i\leq d}e_{i}
.

•

Let
N
denote
\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}/\mathbb{Z}\mathbf{1}
,
p
denote the quotient morphism
\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\rightarrow N
, and
e_{i}\in N
denote
p(e^{i})
for
0\leq i\leq d
.

•

Let
\Delta_{0}
denote a complete strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
such that all rays of
\Delta_{0}
are
\{e_{i}\}_{0\leq i\leq d}
. We regard
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}
as a toric variety
X(\Delta_{0})
.

•

For
V\in\mathcal{V}
, let
e_{V}
denote
\sum_{i\in\rho(V)}e_{i}\in N
.

•

Let
\mathcal{C}
denote the following set:

\mathcal{C}=\{(V_{1},V_{2},\ldots,V_{s})\mid s\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\\
0\subsetneq V_{1}\subsetneq V_{2}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq V_{s}\subsetneq\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k},\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}}(1)),V_{i}\in\mathcal{V},1\leq\forall i\leq s\}

•

For
c=(V_{1},V_{2},\ldots,V_{s})\in\mathcal{C}
,
\sigma_{c}
denote a strongly convex rational polyhedral cone in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
generated by
\{e_{V_{i}}\}_{1\leq j\leq s}
. Let
\Delta(\mathcal{B})
denote
\{\sigma_{c}\mid c\in\mathcal{C}\}
.

From
[
4
]
, we mention a basic and important result for
\Delta(\mathcal{B})
in the following proposition.

Proposition 5.1
.

[
4
, Theorem 4.2.6]

For the above notation,
\Delta(\mathcal{B})
is a strongly convex rational simplicial fan in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
.

The following proposition is also important for defining a new mock toric structure.

Proposition 5.2
.

An identity morphism
\mathrm{id}_{N}
is compatible with the fans
\Delta(\mathcal{B})
and
\Delta_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
c=(V_{1},V_{2},\ldots,V_{s})\in\mathcal{C}
be an element.
Because
\mathcal{B}
generates
\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k},\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}}(1))
and
V_{s}\neq\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k},\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}}(1))
,
\rho(V_{s})\neq\{0,1,\ldots,d\}
.
Thus, from the definition of
\sigma_{c}
,
\sigma_{c}
is contained in
\sum_{i\in\rho(V_{s})}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}e_{i}
.
This cone is in
\Delta_{0}
, and hence the statement follows.
∎

From now on, we will define some data from
\mathcal{B}
.

Definition 5.3
.

We will use the following notation:

•

Let
Z
denote the strictly transform of
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}
along a toric morphism
X(\Delta(\mathcal{B}))\rightarrow X(\Delta_{0})
.
In other words,
Z
is a scheme theoretic closure of
\iota_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k})\cap T_{N}
in
X(\Delta(\mathcal{B}))
.

•

Let
\iota
denote a closed immersion from
Z
to
X(\Delta(\mathcal{B}))
.

•

Let
\Phi
denote the following subset of
\{0,\dots,d\}
:

\Phi=\{\{i_{0},i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}\}\subset\{0,\ldots,d\}\mid\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k},\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}}(1))=\sum_{0\leq j\leq n}k\cdot f_{i_{j}}\}

•

For
\varphi=\{i_{0},i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}\}\in\Phi
, let
N_{\varphi}
denote
\sum_{j\notin\varphi}\mathbb{Z}e_{j}
.

•

For
\varphi\in\Phi
, let
\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}
denote the following subset of
\mathcal{C}
:

\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}=\{(V_{1},V_{2},\ldots,V_{s})\in\mathcal{C}\mid|\varphi\cap\rho(V_{j})|=\dim(V_{j})\quad(1\leq\forall j\leq s)\}

Let
\Delta(\mathcal{B})_{\varphi}
denote
\{\sigma_{c}\in\Delta(\mathcal{B})\mid c\in\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}\}
.
We remark that
\Delta(\mathcal{B})_{\varphi}
is a sub fan of
\Delta(\mathcal{B})
from the definition of
\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}
and simplicialness of
\Delta(\mathcal{B})
.

The following proposition shows that there are many non-toric examples of mock toric varieties.

Proposition 5.4
.

From the above notation,
(N,\Delta(\mathcal{B}),\iota,\Phi,\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi},\{\Delta(\mathcal{B})_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
is a mock toric structure of
Z
.
Moreover,
Z
is proper over
k
.

Proof.

We check that this tuple satisfies the conditions in Definition
3.1
from (1) to (5) in order:

(1)

We can check the condition (1) easily from the definition of
\Delta(\mathcal{B})
and
\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
.

(2)

It is enough to show that
\mathcal{C}=\cup_{\varphi\in\Phi}\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}
.
Let
c=(V_{1},V_{2},\ldots,V_{s})\in\mathcal{C}
be an element.
Then there exists
\{f_{i_{0}},\ldots,f_{i_{n}}\}\subset\mathcal{B}
be a basis of
\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k},\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}}(1))
such that
\{f_{i_{0}},\ldots,f_{i_{\dim(V_{j})-1}}\}
is a basis of
V_{j}
for any
0\leq j\leq s
.
Let
\varphi
denote
\{i_{0},i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}\}
.
In particular,
\varphi\in\Phi
and
c\in\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}
holds.

(3)

Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element.
Let
\mathcal{B}(\varphi)
denote
\{f_{i}\mid i\in\varphi\}
.
From the definition of
\varphi
,
\mathcal{B}(\varphi)
generates
\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k},\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}}(1))
.
Let
\iota_{0}(\varphi),\mathcal{V}(\varphi),N(\varphi),\Delta_{0}(\varphi),\mathcal{C}(\varphi),\Delta(\mathcal{B}(\varphi))
denote the notation above when we replace
\mathcal{B}
with
\mathcal{B}(\varphi)
.
A projection
\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{n+1}
whose kernel is generated by
\{e^{j}\}_{j\notin\varphi}
induces a surjective morphism
N\rightarrow N(\varphi)
.
We can check that the kernel of this morphism is
N_{\varphi}
.
Now, we identify
N(\varphi)
with
N/N_{\varphi}
.
Let
c\in(V_{1},\ldots,V_{s})\in\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}
be an element.
From the definition of
\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}
,
V_{j}\in\mathcal{V}(\varphi)
for any
1\leq j\leq s
.
Thus,
\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}=\mathcal{C}(\varphi)
.
Let
\Delta(\mathcal{B})(\varphi)
denote
\{(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma_{c})\mid c\in\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}\}
.
From the fact that
\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}=\mathcal{C}(\varphi)
,
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}
induces a one-to-one correspondence of cones in
\Delta(\mathcal{B})_{\varphi}
and those in
\Delta(\mathcal{B}(\varphi))
.
In particular,
\Delta(\mathcal{B})(\varphi)=\Delta(\mathcal{B}(\varphi))
over the identification with
N/N_{\varphi}
and
N(\varphi)
.
We can check that
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\sigma_{c}}
is injective for any
c\in\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}
.
Therefore,
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta(\mathcal{B})_{\varphi})}
is injective from the above one-to-one correspondence.

(4)

First, we show the properness of
Z
.
From
[
4
, Theorem. 4.1.11]
, the support of
\mathrm{trop(Z^{\circ})}
is
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta(\mathcal{B}))
.
Hence, from
[
4
, Theorem. 6.4.7(i)]
,
Z
is proper over
k
.
Next, we check the condition (4).
Indeed, from
[
4
, Theorem. 6.4.7(ii)]
,
Z\cap O_{\sigma_{c}}\neq\emptyset
for any
c\in\mathcal{C}
.

(5)

Let
\varphi=\{i_{0},\ldots,i_{n}\}\in\Phi
and
c=(V_{1},\ldots,V_{s})\in\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}
be elements.
We may change the index of elements in
\varphi
such that
\varphi\cap\rho(V_{j})=\{i_{0},i_{1},\ldots,i_{\dim(V_{j})-1}\}
for any
1\leq j\leq s
.
Let
\lambda(j)
denote
i_{\dim(V_{j})-1}
for
1\leq j\leq s
,
\lambda(s+1)
denote
i_{n}
,
V_{s+1}
denote
\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k},\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}}(1))
, and
V_{0}
denote
\{0\}
.
Then
\sigma^{\vee}_{c}
is generated by the following elements:

\{\omega_{\lambda(j)}-\omega_{\lambda(j+1)}\}_{1\leq j\leq s}\cup\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq s+1}\{\omega_{l}-\omega_{\lambda(j)},\omega_{\lambda(j)}-\omega_{l}\mid l\in\rho(V_{j})\setminus\rho(V_{j-1})\}

Let
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma_{c})\subset(N/N_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}
,
M_{\varphi}
denote the dual lattice of
N/N_{\varphi}
, and
q^{*}_{\varphi}
denote the morphism
M_{\varphi}\rightarrow M
induced by
q_{\varphi}
.
Then
k[\sigma^{\vee}_{c}\cap M]
is isomorphic to the following ring as
k[\sigma^{\vee}_{\varphi}\cap M_{\varphi}]
-algebra:

k[\sigma^{\vee}_{\varphi}\cap M_{\varphi}]\otimes_{k}k[\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq s+1}\{(\frac{\chi^{\omega_{l}}}{\chi^{\omega_{\lambda(j)}}})^{\pm}\}_{l\notin\varphi,l\in\rho(V_{j})\setminus\rho(V_{j-1})}]

Let
I_{c}
denote the kernel of
\iota^{*}\colon k[\sigma^{\vee}_{c}\cap M]\rightarrow\Gamma(Z(\sigma_{c}),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
and we take
l\notin\varphi
and
1\leq j\leq s+1
such that
l\in\rho(V_{j})\setminus\rho(V_{j-1})
.
Because
f_{l}\in V_{j}
and
\{f_{i_{0}},\ldots,f_{\lambda(j)}\}
is a basis of
V_{j}
, there exists
0\neq(a^{(l)}_{m})_{0\leq m\leq\dim(V_{j})-1}\in k^{\dim(V_{j})}
such that
f_{l}=\sum_{0\leq m\leq\dim(V_{j})-1}a^{(l)}_{m}f_{i_{m}}
.
Let
h_{l}\in k[\sigma^{\vee}_{c}\cap M]
denote the following element for
l\notin\varphi
:

\frac{\chi^{\omega_{l}}}{\chi^{\omega_{\lambda(j)}}}-(\sum_{0\leq m\leq\dim(V_{j})-1}a^{(l)}_{m}\frac{\chi^{\omega_{i_{m}}}}{\chi^{\omega_{\lambda(j)}}}),

where
j
is an integer such that
1\leq j\leq s+1
and
l\in\rho(V_{j})\setminus\rho(V_{j-1})
.
Let
g_{l}\in k[\sigma^{\vee}_{\varphi}\cap M_{\varphi}]
denote the second term of
h_{l}
.
Then we can check that
h_{l}\in I_{c}
for any
l\notin\varphi
.
Let
g\in k[\sigma^{\vee}_{\varphi}\cap M_{\varphi}]
denote
\prod_{l\notin\varphi}g_{l}
.
We remark that
g
is not a nilpotent element in
k[\sigma^{\vee}_{\varphi}\cap M_{\varphi}]
.
Then there exists the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k[\sigma^{\vee}_{\varphi}\cap M_{\varphi}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k[\sigma^{\vee}_{c}\cap M]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k[\sigma^{\vee}_{\varphi}\cap M_{\varphi}]_{g}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(Z(\sigma_{c}),\mathscr{O}_{Z})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{*}_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}}

where the left morphism is a localization map, and the bottom morphism is induced by the universality of a localization.
For each
l\notin\varphi
,
h_{l}=\frac{\chi^{\omega_{l}}}{\chi^{\omega_{\lambda(j)}}}-g_{l}\in I_{c}
where
1\leq j\leq s+1
is an integer such that
l\in\rho(V_{j})\setminus\rho(V_{j-1})
.
Thus, the bottom morphism is surjective because of the ring structure of
k[\sigma^{\vee}_{c}\cap M]
as a
k[\sigma^{\vee}_{\varphi}\cap M_{\varphi}]
-algebra.
The both ring
k[\sigma^{\vee}_{\varphi}\cap M_{\varphi}]_{g}
and
\Gamma(Z(\sigma_{c}),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
are
n
-dimentional integral domains because
Z(\sigma_{c})
is a scheme theoretic closure of an integral scheme
Z^{\circ}
in
X(\sigma_{c})
.
Thus, the bottom morphism is isomorphic.
Therefore,
\iota_{\varphi}
is an open immersion.

In (4), we already have shown that
Z
is proper over
k
.
∎

We already know a projective space is a toric variety.
The following proposition shows that there are many non-toric mock toric structures of a projective space:

Proposition 5.5
.

For the notation above, we assume that
\{f_{i_{0}},\ldots,f_{i_{n}}\}
is a basis of
\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k},\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}})
for any integers
0\leq i_{0}<\ldots<i_{n}\leq d
.
Then we remark that
\Phi
is the following set:

\Phi=\{\varphi\subset\{0,\ldots,d\}\mid|\varphi|=n+1\}

Let
\Delta_{1}
denote a sub fan of
\Delta_{0}
, which consists of all cones whose dimension is less than
n+1
.
From the assumption of
\mathcal{B}
,
\iota_{0}
passes through
X(\Delta_{1})
.
Let
\iota_{1}
denote the closed immersion from
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}
to
X(\Delta_{1})
and
\Delta_{1,\varphi}
denote
\{\sigma\in\Delta_{1}\mid\sigma\subset\sum_{i\in\varphi}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}e_{i}\}
for
\varphi\in\Phi
.
Then
(N,\Delta_{1},\iota_{1},\Phi,\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi},\{\Delta_{1,\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}\})
is a mock toric structure of
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}
.

Proof.

We check that this tuple satisfies the conditions in Definition
3.1
from (1) to (5) in order.

(1)

We can check the condition (3) from the definition of
\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
and
\{\Delta_{1,\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
.

(2)

We can check the condition (2) from the definition of
\Delta_{1}
and
\{\Delta_{1,\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
.

(3)

We can check the condition (3) from the definition of
\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
and
\{\Delta_{1,\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
.

(4)

From the assumption of
\mathcal{B}
,
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}\setminus T_{N}
is a simple normal crossing divisor of
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}
.
Thus, the condition (4) holds.

(5)

We fix
\varphi\in\Phi
.
Let
\Delta_{1}(\varphi)
denote
\{(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)\mid\sigma\in\Delta_{1,\varphi}\}
.
We can check that
X(\Delta_{1}(\varphi))
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}
and the rational map
\mathbb{P}^{d}_{k}\dashrightarrow X(\Delta_{1}(\varphi))
which induced by
q_{\varphi}\colon N\rightarrow N/N_{\varphi}
is only a linear projection.
Let
P_{\varphi}
denote this linear projection.
From the assumption of
\mathcal{B}
,
\iota(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k})
doesn’t intersect the indeterminant locus of
P_{\varphi}
.
Moreover, the restriction
P_{\varphi}|_{\iota_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k})}
is an isomorphism.
Thus, the composition
(q_{\varphi})_{*}\circ\iota_{1}|_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{k}\cap X(\Delta_{1,\varphi})}
is an open immersion.

∎

As a corollary of
5.5
, we show that Del Pezzo surfaces have a mock toric structure.

Corollary 5.6
.

Let
\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{m}\}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}_{k}(k)
be finite
k-
rational points on
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{k}
, and
X
denote
\mathrm{Bl}_{\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{m}\}}\mathbb{P}^{2}_{k}
.
Then,
X
has a mock toric structure.
In particular, Del Pezzo surfaces have a mock toric structure.

Proof.

Let
\{L^{0,i},L^{1,i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
be a family of lines in
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{k}
such that the following two conditions hold:

(a)

For any
1\leq i\leq m
,
L^{0,i}\cap L^{1,i}=\{p_{i}\}
.

(b)

These
2m
lines are all distinct and all three lines of
2m
lines has no common point.

Thus, from Proposition
5.5
,
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{k}
has a mock toric structure associated with the hyperplane arrangement.
In particular,
p_{1},\ldots,p_{m}
are strata of this mock toric structure.
Thus, from Proposition
4.5
,
\mathrm{Bl}_{p_{1},\ldots,p_{m}}\mathbb{P}^{2}_{k}
has a mock toric structure.
∎

6.
Hypersurfaces of mock toric varieties

Let
Z
be a scheme over
k
and
(N
,
\Delta
,
\iota
,
\Phi
,
\{N_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi}
,
\{\Delta_{\varphi}\}_{\varphi\in\Phi})
be a mock toric structure of
Z
.
Let
f
denote a function in
k[Z^{\circ}]
and
H
denote the hypersurface in
Z^{\circ}
defined by
f=0
.
We have an interest in the structure of the scheme theoretic closure
\overline{H}
of
H
in
Z
.
When
Z
is a toric variety, its structure is important to compute the stable birational volume of
H
.
Although
H
and
\overline{H}
are birational, it is important to take a compactification of
H
when we calculate it (cf.
[
5
]
).
For expanding on the previous point, the following two conditions are important for any
\sigma\in\Delta
:

•

Z_{\sigma}\not\subset\overline{H}

•

Z_{\sigma}\cap\overline{H}
is smooth over
k
.

In this subsection, we give the criterion of the first condition.
Moreover, by considering an appropriate subdivision
\Delta^{\prime}
of
\Delta
and the induced mock toric variety
Z^{\prime}\subset X(\Delta^{\prime})
, we observe that the first condition is satisfied for all cones in
\Delta^{\prime}
.
For checking these aspects, the valuations introduced in subsection 4.3 are valuable.
In the latter part of this section, we explore the inheritance of the first condition for the mock toric varieties defined by
Z
, as discussed in Section 4, even when assuming
Z
and
f
satisfy the first condition.

In this section, we use the following notation:

•

Let
M
denote the dual lattice of
N
and
k[M]
denote a global section ring of an algebraic torus
T_{N}
.

•

For
\varphi\in\Phi
, let
M_{\varphi}
denote the dual lattice of
N/N_{\varphi}
and
q_{\varphi}^{*}\colon M_{\varphi}\hookrightarrow M
be a group morphism induced by
q_{\varphi}\colon N\rightarrow N/N_{\varphi}
.

•

For
\omega\in M
, let
\chi^{\omega}
denote a torus invariant monomial in
k[M]
associated with
\omega
.

•

Let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\colon N\times M\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
be a natural pairing.
We identify elements in
N
as torus invariant valuations so that we can check that
\langle v,\omega\rangle=v(\chi^{\omega})
.

•

Let
k[Z^{\circ}]
denote a global section ring
\Gamma(Z^{\circ},\mathscr{O}_{Z})
.

•

For
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
, let
H^{\circ}_{Z,f}
denote a closed subscheme of
Z^{\circ}
defined by
f=0
.

•

For
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
, let
H_{Z,f}
denote a scheme theoretic closure of
H^{\circ}_{Z,f}
in
Z
.

6.1.
Fineness and non-degeneracy of
f
for
Z

In this subsection, we consider two conditions of
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
for
\Delta
.
In particular, for one condition, we provide a criterion using the valuations defined in subsection 4.3.

We prepare the notation for the hypersurfaces of the toric varieties.
The second condition in the following definition is called
Newton non-degenerate
.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
N
be a lattice of a finite rank,
\Delta
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
,
M
be the dual lattice of
N
,
f
be a polynomial in
k[M]
,
H^{\circ}_{X(\Delta),f}
denote a closed subscheme of
T_{N}
defined by
f=0
, and
H_{X(\Delta),f}
denote a scheme theoretic closure of
H^{\circ}_{X(\Delta),f}
in
X(\Delta)
.

Then we define the following conditions about
f\in k[M]
:

(1)

If
O_{\sigma}\not\subset H_{X(\Delta),f}
for all
\sigma\in\Delta
, then we call that
f
is
fine
for
\Delta
.

(2)

If
f
is fine for
\Delta
and
H_{X(\Delta)}\cap O_{\sigma}
is smooth over
k
for all
\sigma\in\Delta
, then we call that
f
is
non-degenerate
for
\Delta
.

Mock toric varieties are covered by some open subsets of toric varieties.
The following proposition indicates that hypersurfaces of the mock toric variety are also covered by some open subsets of hypersurfaces of toric varieties.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone,
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
be a function, and
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Then the following statements follow:

(a)

There exists
g_{\varphi}\in k[M_{\varphi}]
such that
f/\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(g_{\varphi})\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
.

(b)

For such
g_{\varphi}\in k[M_{\varphi}]
, the following equation holds:

H_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),g_{\varphi}}\times_{X(\Delta(\varphi))}(Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))=H_{Z,f}\cap(Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))

Proof.

We prove the statements from (a) to (b).

(a)

From the condition (5) in Definition
3.1
,
\iota_{\varphi}\colon Z^{\circ}\hookrightarrow T_{N/N_{\varphi}}
is an open immersion of affine schemes.
Moreover,
k[M_{\varphi}]
is a UFD, and
k[Z^{\circ}]
is a Noetherian normal integral scheme from Proposition
3.6
(b), so the statement follows.

(b)

There exists the following diagram whose two small squares are Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\circ}_{Z,f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\circ}_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),g_{\varphi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{N/N_{\varphi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta(\varphi))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\varphi}}

Thus, from Lemma
8.29
and the condition (5) in Definition
3.1
, the statement holds.

∎

In toric varieties, the values
v(\chi^{\omega})
has additivity for
v\in N
where
\omega\in M
.
The following proposition shows that the values
val_{Z}(-)(\chi)
also have additivity for lattice points in each cone in
\Delta
, where
\chi\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\chi\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
be a unit,
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone and
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Then there exists
\omega\in M_{\varphi}
such that
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\chi)=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega}))
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
.

Proof.

Because
k[M_{\varphi}]
is a UFD, there exists
a_{\varphi},b_{\varphi}\in k[M_{\varphi}]
such that these are co-prime and
\chi=\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(a_{\varphi})/\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(b_{\varphi})
.
In particular,
\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(a_{\varphi})
and
\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(b_{\varphi})
are units in
k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
.
Thus,
H_{Z,\iota^{*}(a_{\varphi})}=\emptyset
, so that
H_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),a_{\varphi}}\cap\iota_{\varphi}(Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))=\emptyset
from Proposition
6.2
(b).
Hence,
a_{\varphi}
is fine for
\Delta(\varphi)
from Proposition
3.4
(c).
Therefore, from Lemma
8.18
, there exists
\omega_{1}\in M_{\varphi}
such that
(q_{\varphi}(v))(a_{\varphi}\chi^{\omega_{1}})=0
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
.
In particular,
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(a_{\varphi}))=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{-\omega_{1}}))
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
.
The same argument follows for
b_{\varphi}
, so the statement holds.
∎

The following proposition shows the criterion for the conditions on the scheme theoretic closure of the hypersurface of
Z^{\circ}
in
Z
, as described in the initial part of the section.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
be a function and
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone.
Then the following statements are equivalent:

(a)

There exists
\chi\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
such that
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\chi f)=0
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
.

(b)

Z_{\sigma}\not\subset H_{Z,f}
.

In particular, if these conditions hold, an ideal associated with a closed subscheme
Z(\sigma)\cap H_{Z,f}
of an affine scheme
Z(\sigma)
is generated by
\chi f
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
and
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
From Proposition
6.2
(a) and (b) and Proposition
6.3
, there exists
g_{\varphi}\in k[M_{\varphi}]
such that
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(f)=(q_{\varphi}(v))(g_{\varphi})
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
, and the following equation holds:

H_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),g_{\varphi}}\times_{X(\Delta_{\varphi})}Z(\sigma)=H_{Z,f}\cap Z(\sigma)

Thus, from Proposition
6.3
, the condition of (a) is equivalent to the following condition (a’):

(a’)

There exists
\omega\in M_{\varphi}
such that
q_{\varphi}(v)(\chi^{\omega}g_{\varphi})=0
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
.

On the other hand, from the above equation and Proposition
3.4
(c), the condition of (b) is equivalent to the following condition (b’):

(b’)

O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}\not\subset H_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),g_{\varphi}}

Therefore, from Lemma
8.18
, The condition of (a’) and that of (b’) are equivalent, so that of (a) and that of (b) are so.

In this case, let
I_{f}
denote an ideal of
k[Z^{\circ}]
generated by
f
.
We show that
I_{f}\cap\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
is generated by
\chi f
.
If this claim holds, then the statement of the last part holds.
Let
g\in I_{f}\cap\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
be a function.
Then there exists
h\in k[Z^{\circ}]
such that
g=(\chi f)h
.
Because
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\chi f)=0
and
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(g)\geq 0
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
from Proposition
4.8
,
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(h)\geq 0
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
from the additivity of valuations.
Thus,
h\in\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
from Proposition
4.8
and this shows that
I_{f}\cap\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
is generated by
\chi f
.
∎

As defined in Definition
6.1
, we define two properties of
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
for
\Delta
in the following definition.

Definition 6.5
.

Let
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
be a function.
If
Z_{\sigma}\not\subset H_{Z,f}
for any
\sigma\in\Delta
, then we call that
f
is
fine
for
\Delta
.
If
f
is fine for
\Delta
and
H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}
is smooth over
k
for any
\sigma\in\Delta
, then we call that
f
is
non-degenerate
for
\Delta
.

6.2.
Valuation functions

For
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
and
v\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
, we can compute an integer
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(f)
.
In this subsection, we define a one map from
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)
to
\mathbb{R}
whose values is equal to
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(f)
at any
v\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
.
This extended map is useful to construct canonical refinement
\Delta_{f}
of
\Delta
such that
f
is fine for
\Delta_{f}
.

At first, we define it when
Z
is affine toric variety.

Definition 6.6
.

We keep the notation in Definition
6.1
.
Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone.

(1)

Let
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}
denote a function from
\sigma
to
\mathbb{R}
which satisfies the following three conditions

(i)

A function
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}
is continuous.

(ii)

A function
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}
is preserved by the scalar product of the non-negative real numbers, i.e.,
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}(rv)=r\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}(v)
for any
v\in\sigma
and
r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.

(iii)

For any
v\in\sigma\cap N
,
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}(v)=v(f)
.

We remark that such a function exists and is unique from Lemma
8.13
and Lemma
8.15
(c).

(2)

Let
\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
be a following subset of
M_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}=\{\omega\in M_{\mathbb{Q}}\mid\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}(v)\leq\langle v,\omega\rangle\quad\forall v\in\sigma\}

(3)

For
\omega\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
, let
C^{\sigma}_{f}(\omega)
denote a following subset of
\sigma
:

C^{\sigma}_{f}(\omega)=\{v\in\sigma\mid\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}(v)=\langle v,\omega\rangle\}

From now on, we define the map
\operatorname{val}_{f}\colon\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as follows and consider the basic property of
\operatorname{val}_{f}
.

Proposition 6.7
.

The following statements follow:

(a)

There unique exists a function
\operatorname{val}_{f}\colon\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that it satisfies the following three conditions:

(1)

A function
\operatorname{val}_{f}
is continuous.

(2)

A function
\operatorname{val}_{f}
preserves the scalar product of non-negative real numbers, i.e.
\operatorname{val}_{f}(rv)=r\operatorname{val}_{f}(v)
for any
v\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)
and
r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.

(3)

For any
v\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
,
\operatorname{val}_{f}(v)=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(f)
.

(b)

Let
f
and
g\in k[Z^{\circ}]
be functions.
Then
\operatorname{val}_{fg}=\operatorname{val}_{f}+\operatorname{val}_{g}
.

(c)

Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone and
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}
denote the restriction of
\operatorname{val}_{f}
for
\sigma
.
Then
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}
is an upper convex function.

(d)

Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone and
\chi\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
be a unit.
Then
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi}
is a linear function over
\sigma
.

Proof.

We prove the statements from (a) to (d) in order.

(a)

The uniqueness of
\operatorname{val}_{f}
follows from Lemma
8.13
.
First, we show that
\operatorname{val}_{f}|_{\sigma}
exists for any
\sigma\in\Delta
.
Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
and
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
From the proof of Proposition
6.4
, there exists
g_{\varphi}\in k[M_{\varphi}]
such that
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(f)=(q_{\varphi}(v))(g_{\varphi})
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
.
Let
G_{\sigma}
denote
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}\circ(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\sigma}
.
From Lemma
8.15
(c),
G_{\sigma}
is a continuous map and is preserved by the scalar product of non-negative real numbers because
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}
is an
\mathbb{R}-
linear map.
Moreover,
G_{\sigma}(v)=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(f)
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
.
The definition of
G_{\sigma}
is independent of the choice of
\varphi\in\Phi
such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
because of Lemma
8.13
.
Thus,
\operatorname{val}_{f}|_{\sigma}
exists.

In addition to this,
\{G_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Delta}
can be glued from Lemma
8.13
and this function is continuous because each
\sigma
is a closed subset of
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)
and
|\Delta|
is finite.

(b)

The functions
\operatorname{val}_{f}
and
\operatorname{val}_{g}
are continuous and preserved by the scalar product of non-negative real numbers so that
\operatorname{val}_{f}+\operatorname{val}_{g}
has the same conditions.
Moreover, for any
v\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
, the following equations hold, so that the statement holds from Lemma
8.13
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{val}_{fg}(v)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(fg)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(f)+\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(g)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{f}(v)+\operatorname{val}_{g}(v)

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{val}_{f}+\operatorname{val}_{g})(v)

(c)

We use the notation in the proof of (a).
We have already shown that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}\circ(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\sigma}
.
Thus, from Lemma
8.15
(d),
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}
is an upper convex function because
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}
is an
\mathbb{R}
-linear map.

(d)

Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
From Proposition
6.3
, there exists
\omega\in M_{\varphi}
such that
\operatorname{val}_{\chi}(v)=\langle q_{\varphi}(v),\omega\rangle
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
.
Thus,
\operatorname{val}_{\chi}(v)=\langle(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(v),\omega\rangle
for any
v\in\sigma
from Lemma
8.13
.
Therefore,
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi}
is a linear function over
\sigma
.

∎

In the following proposition, we construct the refinement
\Delta_{f}
of
\Delta
from
\operatorname{val}_{f}
.

Proposition 6.8
.

Let
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
be a function and
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone.

•

Let
\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
denote the following set:

\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}=\{(\chi,b)\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}\times\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\mid val_{f}(v)\leq\frac{1}{b}\operatorname{val}_{\chi}(v)\quad(\forall v\in\sigma)\}

•

For
(\chi,b)\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
, let
C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)
denote the following set:

C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)=\{v\in\sigma\mid val_{f}(v)=\frac{1}{b}\operatorname{val}_{\chi}(v)\}

Then the following statements follow:

(a)

Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone and
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Let
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
Then there exists
g_{\varphi}\in k[M_{\varphi}]
such that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}(v)=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}((q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(v))
for any
v\in\sigma
.

(b)

Let
g_{\varphi}\in k[M_{\varphi}]
be a function that satisfies the condition in (a).
For any
(\chi,b)\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
, there exists
\omega\in\Omega^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}
such that the following equation holds:

(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b))=C^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}(\omega)

Conversely, for any
\omega\in\Omega^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}
, there exists
(\chi,b)\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
such that the following equation holds:

C^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}(\omega)=(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b))

(c)

The set
\{C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)\}_{(\chi,b)\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}}
is a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan and a refinement of
\sigma
.

(d)

Let
\tau
be a face of
\sigma
.
Then
\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}\subset\Omega^{\tau}_{f}
.
Moreover, for any
(\chi,b)\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
, we have
C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)\cap\tau=C^{\tau}_{f}(\chi,b)
.
In particular,
C^{\tau}_{f}(\chi,b)\preceq C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)
.

(e)

The following set
\Delta_{f}
is a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
and a refinement of
\Delta
:

\Delta_{f}=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\Delta}\{C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)\}_{(\chi,b)\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}}

(f)

For any
(\chi,b)\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
, there exists
\chi^{\prime}\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
such that
\operatorname{val}_{f}(v)=\operatorname{val}_{\chi^{\prime}}(v)
for any
v\in C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)
.

Proof.

We prove the statements from (a) to (f) in order.

(a)

The argument about this statement was done in the proof of Proposition
6.7
(a).

(b)

We remark that the restriction
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\sigma}
is bijective from the condition (3) in Definition
3.1
.
Let
\chi\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
be a unit and
b\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
be a positive integer.
Then from Proposition
6.3
, there exists
\omega\in M_{\varphi}
such that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi}(v)=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{\chi^{\omega}}\circ(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(v)
for any
v\in\sigma
.
In particular,
\frac{1}{b}\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi}(v)=\langle(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(v),\frac{\omega}{b}\rangle
for any
v\in\sigma
.
Thus, if
(\chi,b)\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
, then
\frac{\omega}{b}\in\Omega^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}
and
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b))=C^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}(\frac{\omega}{b})
.

Conversely, let
\omega^{\prime}\in(M_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{Q}}
be an element.
Then there exists
\omega\in M_{\varphi}
and
b\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that
\omega^{\prime}=\frac{\omega}{b}\in(M_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Let
\chi\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
denote
\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega})
.
We can check that
\frac{1}{b}\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi}(v)=\langle(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(v),\omega^{\prime}\rangle
for any
v\in\sigma
.
Thus, if
\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}
, then
(\chi,b)\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
and
C^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}(\omega)=(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b))
.

(c)

We already have shown that
(q_{\varphi})|_{\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N}\colon\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N\rightarrow\langle\sigma_{\varphi}\rangle\cap(N/N_{\varphi})
is isomorphic in Proposition
3.2
(d).
Thus, from (b) and Lemma
8.15
(b),
\{C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)\}_{(\chi,b)\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}}
is a rational polyhedral convex fan in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
and it is a refinement of
\sigma
.
Because
\sigma
is strongly convex, this fan is also strongly convex.

(d)

From the definition of
\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
and
\Omega^{\tau}_{f}
, we can check easily that
\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}\subset\Omega^{\tau}_{f}
and
C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)\cap\tau=C^{\tau}_{f}(\chi,b)
for any
(\chi,b)\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
.
Let
(\chi,b)\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
and
\omega\in\sigma^{\vee}
be elements such that
\sigma\cap\omega^{\perp}=\tau
.
Because
C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)\subset\sigma
, we have
\omega\in(C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b))^{\vee}
too.
Thus,
C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)\cap\omega^{\perp}=C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)\cap\tau
is a face of
C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)
.

(e)

For each
\sigma\in\Delta
, we have already shown that
\{C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)\}_{(\chi,b)\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}}
is a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
and it is a refinement of
\sigma
.
Thus, it is enough to show that for any two cones in
\Delta_{f}
, the intersection of those is a common face of both cones.
Let
\sigma
and
\tau\in\Delta
be cones, and
(\chi_{1},b_{1})\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f}
and
(\chi_{2},b_{2})\in\Omega^{\tau}_{f}
be elements.
Then from (d),
\{(\chi_{1},b_{1}),(\chi_{2},b_{2})\}\subset\Omega^{\sigma\cap\tau}_{f}
.
Moreover,
C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi_{1},b_{1})\cap\tau=C^{\sigma\cap\tau}_{f}(\chi_{1},b_{1})
and
C^{\tau}_{f}(\chi_{2},b_{2})\cap\sigma=C^{\sigma\cap\tau}_{f}(\chi_{2},b_{2})
from (d).
Furthermore,
C^{\sigma\cap\tau}_{f}(\chi_{1},b_{1})\preceq C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi_{1},b_{1})
and
C^{\sigma\cap\tau}_{f}(\chi_{2},b_{2})\preceq C^{\tau}_{f}(\chi_{2},b_{2})
.
Thus,
C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi_{1},b_{1})\cap C^{\tau}_{f}(\chi_{2},b_{2})=C^{\sigma\cap\tau}_{f}(\chi_{1},b_{1})\cap C^{\sigma\cap\tau}_{f}(\chi_{2},b_{2})
.
The cone
C^{\sigma\cap\tau}_{f}(\chi_{1},b_{1})\cap C^{\sigma\cap\tau}_{f}(\chi_{2},b_{2})
is a common face of
C^{\sigma\cap\tau}_{f}(\chi_{1},b_{1})
and
C^{\sigma\cap\tau}_{f}(\chi_{2},b_{2})
from (b).
Therefore, this cone is a common face of
C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi_{1},b_{1})
and
C^{\tau}_{f}(\chi_{2},b_{2})
.

(f)

We use the notation in the proof of (a) and (b).
Then there exists
\omega\in\Omega^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}
such that
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b))=C^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}(\omega)
.
Thus, from Lemma
8.15
(g), there exists
\omega_{0}\in M_{\varphi}
such that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}(v^{\prime})=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{\chi^{\omega_{0}}}(v^{\prime})
for any
v^{\prime}\in C^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{g_{\varphi}}(\omega)
.
Let
\chi^{\prime}
denote
\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega_{0}})\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
.
Then
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi^{\prime}}=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma_{\varphi}}_{\chi^{\omega_{0}}}\circ(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\sigma}
.
Hence, from the definition of
g_{\varphi}
,
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}(v)=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi^{\prime}}(v)
for any
v\in C^{\sigma}_{f}(\chi,b)
.

∎

The following corollary indicates that for any
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
, there exists a refinement
\Delta^{\prime}
of
\Delta
such that
f
is fine for
\Delta^{\prime}
.

Corollary 6.9
.

We keep the notation in Proposition
6.8
.
Let
\Delta^{\prime}
be a refinement of
\Delta_{f}
.
Then
f
is fine for
\Delta^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We can check it easily from Proposition
6.4
and Corollary
6.8
(e) and (f).
∎

6.3.
The inheritance of fineness and non-degeneracy

In this subsection, we assume that
f
is fine for
\Delta
, and we consider what holds under the assumption.

In the previous subsection, we analyze
H_{Z,f}
through the intermediary of the hypersurfaces in open subschemes of toric varieties.
The following lemma shows that if
f
is fine for
\Delta
, the fineness also holds for these hypersurfaces of toric varieties.

Lemma 6.10
.

We keep the notation in Proposition
6.8
.
We assume that
f
is fine for
\Delta
.
Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element and
g_{\varphi}\in k[M_{\varphi}]
be a function such that
f/\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(g_{\varphi})\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
.
Then
g_{\varphi}
is fine for
\Delta(\varphi)
.

Proof.

From Proposition
3.4
(c) and Proposition
6.2
(b),

H_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),g_{\varphi}}
does not contain any torus orbits of
X(\Delta(\varphi))
.
∎

If
f
is fine for
\Delta
, then
H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}
is a hypersurface of
Z_{\sigma}
for any
\sigma\in\Delta
.
In the following proposition, this hypersurface also has fineness.

Proposition 6.11
.

Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone and
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
be a function such that
f
is fine for
\Delta
.
From Proposition
6.4
, let
\chi\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
be a unit such that
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\chi f)=0
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
.
Let
p^{\sigma}
denote the quotient morphism
\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})\rightarrow\Gamma(Z_{\sigma},\mathscr{O}_{\overline{Z_{\sigma}}})
associated with the closed immersion
Z_{\sigma}\rightarrow Z(\sigma)
, and
f^{\sigma}
denote
p^{\sigma}(\chi f)
.
Then the following statements follow:

(a)

As a closed subschme of
Z_{\sigma}
,
H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}=H^{\circ}_{\overline{Z_{\sigma}},f^{\sigma}}
.

(b)

We use the notation in Definition
4.1
. Then
f^{\sigma}
is fine for
\Delta[\sigma]
.

(c)

As a closed subschme of
\overline{Z_{\sigma}}
,
H_{Z,f}\cap\overline{Z_{\sigma}}=H_{\overline{Z_{\sigma}},f^{\sigma}}
.

(d)

Let
{}_{f}\Delta
denote the following subset of
\Delta
:

{}_{f}\Delta=\{\tau\in\Delta\mid H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\tau}\neq\emptyset\}

Then
{}_{f}\Delta
is a subfan of
\Delta
.

(e)

If
f
is non-degenerate for
\Delta
, then
f^{\sigma}
is non-degenerate for
\Delta[\sigma]
too.

Proof.

We prove the statements from (a) to (e) in order.

(a)

From Proposition
6.4
, the ideal of
\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
associated with a closed subscheme
H_{Z,f}\cap Z(\sigma)\subset Z(\sigma)
is generated by
\chi f
. Thus, the statement follows.

(b)

From (a), we remark that
H_{\overline{Z_{\sigma}},f^{\sigma}}\subset H_{Z,f}\cap\overline{Z_{\sigma}}
.
Let
\tau^{\prime}\in\Delta[\sigma]
be a cone.
Then there exists
\tau\in\Delta
such that
\sigma\subset\tau
and
(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}
.
Let
Z^{\sigma}
denote
\overline{Z_{\sigma}}=Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}}
.
Then
(Z^{\sigma})_{\tau^{\prime}}=Z_{\tau}
.
Thus,
H_{\overline{Z_{\sigma}},f^{\sigma}}
does not contain
(Z^{\sigma})_{\tau^{\prime}}
from the assumption of
f
, the above remark, and Proposition
3.6
(a).
Therefore,
f^{\sigma}
is fine for
\Delta[\sigma]
.

(c)

Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
From Proposition
6.2
(a) and Lemma
6.10
, there exists
g_{\varphi}\in k[M_{\varphi}]
such that
f/\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(g_{\varphi})\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
and
g_{\varphi}
is fine for
\Delta(\varphi)
.
Let
\eta\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
denote
f/\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(g_{\varphi})
.
Then
H_{Z,f}=H_{Z,\eta^{-1}f}
and
\chi f=(\chi\eta)(\eta^{-1}f)
, so we may assume that
\eta=1
, i.e.
f=\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(g_{\varphi})
.
Moreover, from Proposition
6.3
, there exists
\omega\in M_{\varphi}
such that
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\chi)=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega}))
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
.
In particular,
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(\chi^{-1}\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega}))=0
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
.
Thus, from Proposition
4.8
,
\chi^{-1}\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega})\in\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})^{*}
.
Thus, we may assume that
\chi=\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega})
for some
\omega\in M_{\varphi}
.
Especially,
q_{\varphi}(v)(\chi^{\omega}g_{\varphi})=0
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
and
\chi f=\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega}g_{\varphi})
.

By the way, let
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
and there exists the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(X(\sigma_{\varphi}),\mathscr{O}_{X(\Delta(\varphi))})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(O_{\sigma_{\varphi}},\mathscr{O}_{\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(Z_{\sigma},\mathscr{O}_{\overline{Z_{\sigma}}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\sigma}_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota^{\sigma})^{*}_{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\sigma}}

where horizontal maps are surjective ring morphisms induced by closed immersions, and vertical maps are injective ring morphisms induced by open immersions in the condition (5) in Definition
3.1
.
Let
g^{\sigma}_{\varphi}
denote
p^{\sigma}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega}g_{\varphi})

\in

\Gamma(O_{\sigma_{\varphi}},

\mathscr{O}_{\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}})
.
Then, from the above diagram, there exists the following diagram whose all small squares are Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\circ}_{\overline{Z_{\sigma}},f^{\sigma}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{Z_{\sigma}}\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\circ}_{\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}},g^{\sigma}_{\varphi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota^{\sigma})_{\varphi}}

Because
(\iota^{\sigma})_{\varphi}
is an open immersion, the following equation holds from Lemma
8.29
:

H_{\overline{Z_{\sigma}},f^{\sigma}}\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})=H_{\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}},g^{\sigma}_{\varphi}}\times_{\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}}(\overline{Z_{\sigma}}\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))

On the other hand, we recall that
g_{\varphi}
is fine for
\Delta(\varphi)
.
Thus, from Lemma
8.21
(c),
H_{\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}},g^{\sigma}_{\varphi}}=H_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),g_{\varphi}}\cap\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}
.
Hence, from Proposition
6.2
(b), the following equation holds:

\displaystyle H_{\overline{Z_{\sigma}},f^{\sigma}}\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})

\displaystyle=H_{\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}},g^{\sigma}_{\varphi}}\times_{\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}}(\overline{Z_{\sigma}}\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))

\displaystyle=(H_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),g_{\varphi}}\cap\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}})\times_{\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}}(\overline{Z_{\sigma}}\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))

\displaystyle=(H_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),g_{\varphi}}\times_{X(\Delta(\varphi))}\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}})\times_{\overline{O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}}(\overline{Z_{\sigma}}\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))

\displaystyle=(H_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),g_{\varphi}}\times_{X(\Delta(\varphi))}(Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})))\times_{(Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))}(\overline{Z_{\sigma}}\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))

\displaystyle=(H_{Z,f}\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))\times_{(Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))}(\overline{Z_{\sigma}}\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))

\displaystyle=(H_{Z,f}\cap\overline{Z_{\sigma}})\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})

Because
\overline{Z_{\sigma}}
is covering by a family of open subsets
\{X(\Delta_{\varphi})\}_{\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}}
of
X(\Delta)
, we have
H_{Z,f}\cap\overline{Z_{\sigma}}=H_{\overline{Z_{\sigma}},f^{\sigma}}
.

(d)

We assume that
H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}=\emptyset
.
Then
f^{\sigma}\in\Gamma(Z_{\sigma},\mathscr{O}_{\overline{Z_{\sigma}}})^{*}
from (a).
Thus, from (c),
H_{Z,f}\cap\overline{Z_{\sigma}}=\emptyset
.
In particular, from Proposition
3.6
(a),
H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\tau}=\emptyset
for any
\tau\in\Delta
such that
\sigma\subset\tau
.
This shows that
{}_{f}\Delta
is a subfan of
\Delta
.

(e)

From the definition of a mock toric structure of
\overline{Z_{\sigma}}=Z\cap\overline{O_{\sigma}}
in Definition
4.1
, the strata of
\overline{Z_{\sigma}}
can be identified with the strata of
Z
which is contained in
\overline{Z_{\sigma}}
.
Thus, from (c),
f^{\sigma}
is non-degenerate for
\Delta[\sigma]
.

∎

The following proposition shows that if
f
is decomposed in some non-units in
k[Z^{\circ}]
, then all factors are fine for
\Delta
.

Proposition 6.12
.

Let
f,f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}\in k[Z^{\circ}]
be nonzero functions.
We assume that
f=f_{1}\cdot f_{2}\cdot\cdots\cdot f_{r}
.
Then the following statement follows:

(a)

The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

A function
f
is fine for
\Delta
.

(2)

For any
1\leq i\leq r
,
f_{i}
is fine for
\Delta
.

(b)

If
f
is non-degenerate for
\Delta
, then
f_{i}
is so for any
1\leq i\leq r
.

(c)

We assume that
f
is non-degenerate for
\Delta
and
f=f_{1}\cdot f_{2}\cdot\cdots\cdot f_{r}
is an irreducible decomposition of
f
.
Then, as a closed subscheme of
Z
, the following equation holds:

H_{Z,f}=\coprod_{1\leq i\leq r}H_{Z,f_{i}}

Moreover, the above equation is a connected decomposition of
H_{Z,f}
.

Proof.

We prove these statements from (a) to (c) in order.

(a)

To show one direction is easy from Proposition
6.4
, so we assume that the condition (1) holds.
Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone and
v_{0}\in\sigma^{\circ}
be an element.
From the assumption and Proposition
6.4
, there exists
\chi\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
such that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}+\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi}\equiv 0
.
We repalce
f
with
\chi f
and
f_{1}
with
\chi f_{1}
, and we may assume that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}\equiv 0
.
From Proposition
6.8
(c), for any
1\leq i\leq r
, there exists
(\chi_{i},b_{i})\in\Omega^{\sigma}_{f_{i}}
such that
v_{0}\in C^{\sigma}_{f_{i}}(\chi_{i},b_{i})
.
Then the following equation and inequality hold for any
v\in\sigma
:

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f_{i}}(v)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\frac{1}{b_{i}}\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi_{i}}(v)

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f_{i}}(v_{0})

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\frac{1}{b_{i}}\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi_{i}}(v_{0})

From Proposition
6.7
(b) and the assumption of
f
, the left-hand sides of the above equation and inequality are equal to
0
, respectively.
Moreover, from Proposition
6.7
(d),
\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\frac{1}{b_{i}}\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi_{i}}
is a linear function on
\sigma
.
Thus,
\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\frac{1}{b_{i}}\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi_{i}}\equiv 0
because
v_{o}\in\sigma^{\circ}
.

Next, we show that
\bigcap_{1\leq i\leq r}C^{\sigma}_{f_{i}}(\chi_{i},b_{i})=\sigma
by a contradiction.
We assume that
w\in\sigma\setminus\bigcap_{1\leq i\leq r}C^{\sigma}_{f_{i}}(\chi_{,}b_{i})
exists.
From the definition of
C^{\sigma}_{f_{i}}(\chi_{i},b_{i})
, we have
\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f_{i}}(w)<\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\frac{1}{b_{i}}\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi_{i}}(w)=0
.
However,
\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}val^{\sigma}_{f_{i}}(w)=0
, so it is a contradiction.

Thus, for any
1\leq i\leq r
, we have
C^{\sigma}_{f_{i}}(\chi_{i},b_{i})=\sigma
.
Hence, from Proposition
6.8
(f), for any
1\leq i\leq r
, there exists
\chi^{\prime}_{i}\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
such that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f_{i}}=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi^{\prime}_{i}}
.
In particular,
\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(f_{i}\cdot{\chi^{\prime}_{i}}^{-1})=0
for any
v\in\sigma\cap N
.

(b)

Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone.
From (a) and Proposition
6.4
, there exists
\chi_{1},\cdots,\chi_{r}\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
such that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f_{i}}+\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi_{i}}\equiv 0
for any
1\leq i\leq r
.
Thus, for any
1\leq i\leq r
, the ideal of
\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
associated with the closed subscheme
H_{Z,f_{i}}\cap Z(\sigma)
of
Z(\sigma)
is generated by
\chi_{i}f_{i}
.
Let
\chi
denote
\chi_{1}\chi_{2}\cdots\chi_{r}\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
.
Then from Proposition
6.7
(b),
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}+\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi}\equiv 0
.
In particular, the ideal of
\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
associated with the closed subscheme
H_{Z,f}\cap Z(\sigma)
of
Z(\sigma)
is generated by
\chi f
.

Let
p^{\sigma}
denote a quotient ring morphism
\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})\rightarrow\Gamma(Z_{\sigma},\mathscr{O}_{Z_{\sigma}})
associated with the closed immersion
Z_{\sigma}\rightarrow Z(\sigma)
.
Let
f^{\sigma},f^{\sigma}_{1},\cdots,f^{\sigma}_{r}
denote
p^{\sigma}(\chi f)
,
p^{\sigma}(\chi_{1}f_{1}),\cdots,p^{\sigma}(\chi_{r}f_{r})
respectively.
From the construction of
\chi
, we have
