f^{\sigma}=f^{\sigma}_{1}f^{\sigma}_{2}\cdots f^{\sigma}_{r}
.
From Proposition
6.11
(a), for any
1\leq i\leq r
, the closed subscheme
H_{Z,f_{i}}\cap Z_{\sigma}
of
Z_{\sigma}
is defined by
f^{\sigma}_{i}
.
Similarly, the closed subscheme
H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}
of
Z_{\sigma}
is defined by
f^{\sigma}
.
From the assumption,
H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}
is smooth over
k
.
We recall that
k[Z_{\sigma}]
is a UFD from Proposition
3.6
(d).
Thus,
f^{\sigma}_{1},f^{\sigma}_{2},\cdots,f^{\sigma}_{r}
are co-prime, and the following equation holds as a closed subscheme of
Z_{\sigma}
.

H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}=\coprod_{1\leq i\leq r}H_{Z,f_{i}}\cap Z_{\sigma}

In particular,
H_{Z,f_{i}}\cap Z_{\sigma}
is smooth over
k
for any
1\leq i\leq r
.
Therefore,
f_{i}
is non-degenerate for
\Delta
for any
1\leq i\leq r
.

(c)

We use the notation of the proof of (b).
From the proof of (b),
H_{Z,f_{i}}\cap H_{Z,f_{j}}=\emptyset
for any
1\leq i<j\leq r
.
In particular,
\chi_{i}f
and
\chi_{j}f
generates a unit ideal of
\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
.
Thus, the following equation holds as a closed subscheme of
Z(\sigma)
for any
\sigma\in\Delta
:

H_{Z,f}\cap Z(\sigma)=\coprod_{1\leq i\leq r}H_{Z,f_{i}}\cap Z(\sigma)

Hence, the first part of the statement holds.

For any
1\leq i\leq r
,
H^{\circ}_{Z,f_{i}}
is irreducible.
Thus, from the definition of
H_{Z,f_{i}}
,
H_{Z,f_{i}}
is irreducible too.
Therefore, the right-hand side of the equation in the statement is a connected decomposition of
H_{Z,f}
.

∎

The following proposition gives sufficient conditions when
H_{Z,f}
is smooth over
k
.

Proposition 6.13
.

Let
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
be a function.
We assume that
f
is non-degenerate for
\Delta
and
\Delta
is unimodular.
Then
H_{Z,f}
is smooth over
k
.

Proof.

Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone and
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Let
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
Then from Proposition
6.2
(a) and (b), there exists
g_{\varphi}\in k[M_{\varphi}]
such that
H_{X(\sigma_{\varphi}),g_{\varphi}}\times_{X(\sigma_{\varphi})}Z(\sigma)=H_{Z,f}\cap Z(\sigma)
.
We remark that
\iota_{\varphi}(Z(\sigma))
is an open subscheme of
X(\sigma_{\varphi})
and
H_{X(\sigma_{\varphi}),g_{\varphi}}
does not contain
O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}
from Lemma
6.10
.
Moreover, from the proof of Proposition
3.4
(c),
(H_{X(\sigma_{\varphi}),g_{\varphi}}\cap O_{\sigma_{\varphi}})\times_{X(\sigma_{\varphi})}Z(\sigma)=H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}
.
Because
f
is nondegenerate for
\Delta
,
H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}\cong H_{X(\sigma_{\varphi}),g_{\varphi}}\cap O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}\cap\iota_{\varphi}(Z(\sigma))
is smooth over
k
.
Thus, from Lemma
8.25
, there exists

•

A toric monoid
S

•

A morphism
p\colon X(\sigma_{\varphi})\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k[S])
over
\operatorname{Spec}(k)

•

An open subset
U
of
\iota_{\varphi}(Z(\sigma))

such that

(1)

H_{X(\sigma_{\varphi}),g_{\varphi}}\cap O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}\cap\iota_{\varphi}(Z(\sigma))\subset U

(2)

The restriction
p|_{H_{X(\sigma_{\varphi}),f}\cap U}\colon H_{X(\sigma_{\varphi}),f}\cap U\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k[S])
is smooth.

Let
U_{\sigma}
denote an open subset
{(\iota_{\varphi})}^{-1}(U)
of
Z(\sigma)
.
Then
H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}\subset U_{\sigma}
and the restriction
(p\circ\iota_{\varphi})|_{H_{Z,f}\cap U_{\sigma}}\colon{H_{Z,f}\cap U_{\sigma}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k[S])
is smooth.
From the assumption that
\sigma
is unimodular,
\sigma_{\varphi}
is unimodular from Proposition
3.10
(a).
Thus, from the proof of Lemma
8.25
, we can take
S
such that
\operatorname{Spec}(k[S])
is smooth over
k
.
Therefore,
H_{Z,f}\cap U_{\sigma}
is smooth over
k
.
We recall that
H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}\subset U_{\sigma}
.
Thus, by considering for all
\sigma\in\Delta
, we can check that
H_{Z,f}
is smooth over
k
.
∎

We use the notation in the subsection 4.2.
We have already shown that a toric resolution of
X(\Delta^{\prime})\rightarrow X(\Delta)
induces a new mock toric variety
Z^{\prime}
.
The following proposition shows the structure of
H_{Z^{\prime},f}
explicitly.

Proposition 6.14
.

Let
N^{\prime}
be a lattice of finite rank,
\pi\colon N^{\prime}\rightarrow N
be a surjection,
s
be a section of
\pi
,
\Delta
and
\Delta^{\prime}
be strongly convex rational polyhedral fans in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
and
N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
respectively, and
f\in k[Z^{\circ}]
be a function.
We assume that
\pi
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\prime}
and
\Delta
.
Let
Z^{\prime}
denote a mock toric variety induced by
Z
along
\pi_{*}\colon X(\Delta^{\prime})\rightarrow X(\Delta)
and
s
,
\mu
denote the restriction
\pi_{*}|_{Z^{\prime}}\colon Z^{\prime}\rightarrow Z
, and
\mu^{*}\colon k[Z^{\circ}]\rightarrow k[{Z^{\prime}}^{\circ}]
be a ring morphism induced by
\mu
.
Then the following statements hold:

(a)

The following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime})\cap N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Val}_{k}(Z^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Val}_{k}(Z)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{val}_{Z^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{val}_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\natural}}

(b)

\operatorname{val}_{f}\circ\pi_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime})}=\operatorname{val}_{\mu^{*}(f)}

(c)

If
f
is fine for
\Delta
, then
\mu^{*}(f)
is fine for
\Delta^{\prime}
.

(d)

If
f
is fine for
\Delta
, then
H_{Z,f}\times_{Z}Z^{\prime}=H_{Z^{\prime},\mu^{*}(f)}
.

(e)

We assume that
f
is fine for
\Delta
.
Then
f
is non-degenerate for
\Delta
if and only if
\mu^{*}(f)
is non-degenerate for
\Delta^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We prove the statements from (a) to (e) in order.

(a)

Let
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}
and
\sigma\in\Delta
be cones, and
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma
and
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
We recall that
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}
.
Let
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
and
\tau_{\varphi}
denote
(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)
.
We remark that there exists the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau_{\varphi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma_{\varphi})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}

where
\pi_{\varphi}
denote a group morphism
N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi})\rightarrow N/N_{\varphi}
such that
\pi_{\varphi}\circ q^{\prime}_{\varphi}=q_{\varphi}\circ\pi
.
Let
w\in\tau\cap N^{\prime}
be an element.
Then
\mu_{\natural}(\operatorname{val}_{Z^{\prime}}(w))=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(\pi(w))
from Lemma
8.31
.

(b)

Because
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}
is a linear map, both functions are continuous maps and are preserved by the scalar product of non-negative real numbers.
Moreover, from (a), the following equation holds for any
w\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime})\cap N^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{val}_{f}(\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(w))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(\pi(w))(f)

\displaystyle=(\mu_{\natural}(\operatorname{val}_{Z^{\prime}}(w)))(f)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Z^{\prime}}(w)(\mu^{*}(f))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{\mu^{*}(f)}(w)

Thus, from Lemma
8.13
, the statement holds.

(c)

Let
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}
and
\sigma\in\Delta
be cones such that
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma
.
Then there exists
\chi\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
such that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}(v)=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi}(v)
for any
v\in\sigma
from Proposition
6.4
.
From (b),
\operatorname{val}_{f}\circ\pi_{\mathbb{R}}=\operatorname{val}_{\mu^{*}(f)}
and
\operatorname{val}_{\chi}\circ\pi_{\mathbb{R}}=\operatorname{val}_{\mu^{*}(\chi)}
.
Thus, the following equation holds for any
w\in\tau
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{val}_{\mu^{*}(f)}(w)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{f}(\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(w))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{\chi}(\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(w))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{\mu^{*}(\chi)}(w)

Therefore, from Proposition
6.4
,
H_{Z^{\prime},\mu^{*}(f)}
does not contain
Z^{\prime}_{\tau}
, so
\mu^{*}(f)
is fine for
\Delta^{\prime}
.

(d)

From Proposition
6.2
(a) and Lemma
6.10
, there exists
g_{\varphi}\in k[M_{\varphi}]
such that
f/\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(g_{\varphi})\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
and
g_{\varphi}
is fine for
\Delta(\varphi)
.
Then from Proposition
6.2
(b), the following equations hold:

H_{Z,f}\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})=H_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),g_{\varphi}}\times_{X(\Delta(\varphi))}(Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))

Let
\pi_{\varphi}^{*}\colon\Gamma(X(\sigma_{\varphi}),\mathscr{O}_{X(\Delta(\varphi))})\rightarrow\Gamma(X(\tau_{\varphi}),\mathscr{O}_{X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi))})
denote a ring morphism induced by a toric morphism
(\pi_{\varphi})_{*}\colon X(\tau_{\varphi})\rightarrow X(\sigma_{\varphi})
.
Let
f^{\prime}
denote
\mu^{*}(f)
and
g^{\prime}
denote
\pi_{\varphi}^{*}(g_{\varphi})
.
Then
f^{\prime}/(\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi})^{*}(g^{\prime})\in k[{Z^{\prime}}^{\circ}]^{*}
and the following equation holds:

H_{Z^{\prime},f^{\prime}}\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})=H_{X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)),g^{\prime}}\times_{X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi))}(Z^{\prime}\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}))

On the other hand, from Lemma
8.22
(b), the following equations hold:

H_{X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)),g^{\prime}}=H_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),g_{\varphi}}\times_{X(\Delta(\varphi))}X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi))

Thus, the following equation holds:

\displaystyle H_{Z^{\prime},f^{\prime}}\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})

\displaystyle=H_{X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)),g^{\prime}}\times_{X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi))}(Z^{\prime}\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}))

\displaystyle=(H_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),g_{\varphi}}\times_{X(\Delta(\varphi))}X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)))\times_{X(\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi))}(Z^{\prime}\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}))

\displaystyle=(H_{X(\Delta(\varphi)),g_{\varphi}}\times_{X(\Delta(\varphi))}(Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})))\times_{Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})}(Z^{\prime}\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}))

\displaystyle=(H_{Z,f}\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi}))\times_{Z\cap X(\Delta_{\varphi})}(Z^{\prime}\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}))

\displaystyle=(H_{Z,f}\times_{Z}Z^{\prime})\times_{Z^{\prime}}(Z^{\prime}\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}))

\displaystyle=(H_{Z,f}\times_{Z}Z^{\prime})\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})

Therefore,
H_{Z^{\prime},f^{\prime}}=H_{Z,f}\times_{Z}Z^{\prime}
because
\{Z^{\prime}(\tau)\}_{\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}}
is an open covering of
Z^{\prime}
.

(e)

We use the notation in the proof of (a).
There exists
\sigma\in\Delta
such that
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\cap\sigma^{\circ}\neq\emptyset
.
Thus, we remark that
(\pi_{\varphi})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau_{\varphi})\cap\sigma^{\circ}_{\varphi}\neq\emptyset
too.
Hence, there exists the following commutative diagram whose all small squares are Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}_{\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O_{\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O_{\tau_{\varphi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q^{\prime}_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{\varphi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{\varphi})_{*}}

In particular,
(\pi_{\varphi})_{*}\colon O_{\tau_{\varphi}}\rightarrow O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}
is a trivial algebraic torus fibration, so
\mu\colon Z^{\prime}_{\tau}\rightarrow Z_{\sigma}
is also a trivial algebraic torus fibration.
Therefore, from (d),
H_{Z^{\prime},\mu^{*}(f)}\cap Z^{\prime}_{\tau}
is isomorphic to a trivial algebraic torus fibration of
H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}
, so the smoothness of
H_{Z^{\prime},\mu^{*}(f)}\cap Z_{\tau}
and the smoothness of
H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}
are equivalent.

∎

In
4.10
, we considered the relation between mock toric varieties and the field extension.
From now on, we consider the relation with
H_{Z,f}
and the field extension.

Proposition 6.15
.

We use the notation in Proposition
4.10
.
Let
f\in K[Z^{\circ}]
be a function.
Then the following statements follow:

(a)

\operatorname{val}_{f}=\operatorname{val}_{\alpha^{*}(f)}

(b)

If
f
is fine for
\Delta
, then
\alpha^{*}(f)
is fine for
\Delta
.

(c)

If
f
is fine for
\Delta
, then
H_{Z,f}\times_{Z}{Z_{L}}=H_{Z_{L},\alpha^{*}(f)}
.

(d)

If
f
is non-degenerate for
\Delta
, then
\alpha^{*}(f)
is non-degenerate for
\Delta
.

Proof.

We prove the statements from (a) to (d) in order.

(a)

Both functions are continuous and are preserved by the scalar product of non-negative real numbers.
Let
v\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)\cap N
be an element.
Then from Proposition
4.10
(c), the following equation holds:

\displaystyle\operatorname{val}_{f}(v)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Z}(v)(f)

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{\natural}(\operatorname{val}_{Z_{L}}(v)))(f)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Z_{L}}(v)(\alpha^{*}(f))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{\alpha^{*}(f)}(v)

Thus, from Lemma
8.13
, the statement holds.

(b)

Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone.
Then from Proposition
6.4
, there exists
\chi\in K[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
such that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi}
.
From (a),
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\alpha^{*}(f)}=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\alpha^{*}(\chi)}
.
Thus, from Proposition
6.4
,
\alpha^{*}(f)
is fine for
\Delta
.

(c)

Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone.
Then from Proposition
6.4
, there exists
\chi\in K[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
such that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi}
.
We remark that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\alpha^{*}(f)}=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\alpha^{*}(\chi)}
too from (a).
Thus, the ideal of
\Gamma(Z_{L}(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z_{L}})
associated with the closed subscheme
H_{Z_{L},\alpha^{*}(f)}\cap Z_{L}(\sigma)
of
Z_{L}(\sigma)
is generated by
\alpha^{*}(\chi^{-1}f)
and the ideal of
\Gamma(Z(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{Z})
associated with the closed subscheme
H_{Z,f}\cap Z(\sigma)
of
Z(\sigma)
is generated by
\chi^{-1}f
.
This shows that
H_{Z_{L},\alpha^{*}(f)}\cap Z_{L}(\sigma)=(H_{Z,f}\times_{Z}Z_{L})\cap Z_{L}(\sigma)
, so that the statement holds.

(d)

Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone.
From (c),
H_{Z_{L},\alpha^{*}(f)}\cap(Z_{L})_{\sigma}=(H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma})\times_{\operatorname{Spec}({K})}\operatorname{Spec}(L)
.
From the assumption,
H_{Z,f}\cap Z_{\sigma}
is smooth over
K
, so
H_{Z_{L},\alpha^{*}(f)}\cap(Z_{L})_{\sigma}
is smooth over
L
.
Thus,
\alpha^{*}(f)
is non-degenerate for
\Delta
.

∎

6.4.
Extended valuation function

In this subsection, we use the notation in Definition
4.11
.

In Proposition
4.12
, we define
\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}\colon\pi^{-1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))\cap N^{\prime}\rightarrow\operatorname{Val}_{k}{Z_{\pi}}
.
Let
f\in k[Z_{\pi}]
be a function.
First, we define a map
\operatorname{val}_{f,\pi}\colon\pi^{-1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as in Proposition
6.7
.

Proposition 6.16
.

Let
f\in k[Z_{\pi}]
be a function.
Then there unique exists a function
\operatorname{val}_{f,\pi}\colon

\pi^{-1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that it satisfies the following three conditions:

(1)

A function
\operatorname{val}_{f,\pi}
is continuous.

(2)

A function
\operatorname{val}_{f,\pi}
is preserved by the scalar product of non-negative real numbers, i.e., for any
v\in\pi^{-1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))
and
r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, we have
\operatorname{val}_{f,\pi}(rv)=r\operatorname{val}_{f,\pi}(v)
.

(3)

For any
v\in\pi^{-1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))\cap N^{\prime}
, we have
\operatorname{val}_{f,\pi}(v)=\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v)(f)
.

Proof.

From Lemma
8.13
, we can check the uniqueness of
\operatorname{val}_{f,\pi}
.
We show the existence of
\operatorname{val}_{f,\pi}
.
Let
\Delta^{\prime}
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime})=\pi^{-1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))
and
\pi
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\prime}
and
\Delta
.
Let
W
be a mock toric variety induced by
Z
along
\pi_{*}\colon X(\Delta^{\prime})\rightarrow X(\Delta)
and
s
.
Then from Proposition
4.12
(b),
\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v^{\prime})=\operatorname{val}_{W}(v^{\prime})
for any
v^{\prime}\in\pi^{-1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))\cap N^{\prime}
.
We remark that
k[Z_{\pi}]=k[W^{\circ}]
and we already have defined the map
\operatorname{val}_{f}\colon\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by a mock toric variety
W
in Proposition
6.7
(a).
Thus,
\operatorname{val}_{f}(v^{\prime})=\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v^{\prime})(f)
for any
v^{\prime}\in\pi^{-1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))\cap N^{\prime}
.
Therefore,
\operatorname{val}_{f}
satisfies all conditions in the statement.
∎

For
A\subset\pi^{-1}_{\mathbb{R}}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta))
, let
\operatorname{val}^{A}_{f,\pi}
denote the restriction
\operatorname{val}_{f,\pi}|_{A}
.
The following proposition is analogous to Proposition
6.3
.

Proposition 6.17
.

Let
\chi^{\prime}\in k[Z_{\pi}]^{*}
be a unit,
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone, and
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
\sigma\in\Delta_{\varphi}
.
Let
\sigma^{\pi}
denote
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}^{-1}(\sigma)
, and
M^{\prime}_{\varphi}
denote the dual lattice of
N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi})
.
Then there exists
\omega\in M^{\prime}_{\varphi}
such that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma^{\pi}}_{\chi^{\prime},\pi}=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma^{\pi}}_{{\iota^{\prime}}^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega}),\pi}
.
In particular,
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma^{\pi}}_{\chi,\pi}
is a linear map on
\sigma^{\pi}
.

Proof.

From Proposition
4.12
(c), there exists
\chi\in k[Z^{\circ}]^{*}
and
\omega_{1}\in M_{1}
such that
\chi^{\prime}=\epsilon^{*}(\chi)p^{*}_{0}(\chi^{\omega_{1}})
.
Let
M_{\varphi}
denote the dual lattice of
N/N_{\varphi}
.
From Proposition
6.3
, there exists
\omega_{2}\in M_{\varphi}
such that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi}=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega_{2}})}
.
Hence, from Proposition
4.12
(d),
\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v^{\prime})(\epsilon^{*}(\chi))=\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v^{\prime})(\epsilon^{*}(\iota^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega_{2}})))
for any
v^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\pi}\cap N^{\prime}
.
We recall that
\iota_{\varphi}\circ\epsilon=\pi_{\varphi}\circ\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi}
.
Thus,
\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v^{\prime})(\chi^{\prime})=\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v^{\prime})({\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi}}^{*}(\pi^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega_{2}})))+\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(v^{\prime})({\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi}}^{*}(p^{*}_{\varphi}(\chi^{\omega_{1}}))
for any
v^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\pi}\cap N^{\prime}
.
Let
\omega
denote
p^{*}_{\varphi}(\omega_{1})+\pi^{*}_{\varphi}(\omega_{2})\in M^{\prime}_{\varphi}
.
Therefore, from Lemma
8.13
,
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma^{\pi}}_{\chi,\pi}=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma^{\pi}}_{{\iota^{\prime}_{\varphi}}^{*}(\chi^{\omega}),\pi}
.
∎

6.5.
Hypersurfaces of mock toric varieties over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}

In subsection 4.5, we considered the mock toric varieties over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
.
In this subsection, we consider hypersurfaces of them.
This analysis is crucial for the construction of strictly toroidal models of the hypersurfaces in mock toric varieties that we conduct in the next Section.

Proposition 6.18
.

We use the notation in Proposition
4.14
.
Let
f\in k[W^{\circ}]
be a function.
We assume that
\operatorname{char}(k)=0
.
Then the following statements hold:

(a)

\operatorname{val}_{f}\circ j^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}=\operatorname{val}_{\psi^{*}(f)}

(b)

If
f
is fine for
\Delta^{\prime}
, then
\psi^{*}(f)
is fine for
\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}
.

(c)

If
f
is fine for
\Delta^{\prime}
, then
H_{W,f}\times_{W}{Y_{k(t)}}=H_{Y_{k(t)},\psi^{*}(f)}
.

(d)

If
f
is non-degenerate for
\Delta^{\prime}
, then
\psi^{*}(f)
is non-degenerate for
\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}
.

Proof.

We prove the statements from (a) to (d) in order.

(a)

Both functions are continuous and are preserved by the scalar product of
non-negative real numbers because
j^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
is a linear function.
Let
v^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)})\cap N^{\prime}
be an element.
Then from Proposition
4.14
(k), the following equation holds:

\displaystyle\operatorname{val}_{f}\circ j^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{W}(j^{\prime}(v^{\prime}))(f)

\displaystyle=(\psi_{\sharp}(\operatorname{val}_{Y_{k(t)}}(v^{\prime})))(f)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Y_{k(t)}}(v^{\prime})(\psi^{*}(f))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{\psi^{*}(f)}(v^{\prime})

Therefore, from Lemma
8.13
, the statement holds.

(b)

Let
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}
be a cone.
Then there exists
\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}_{0}
such that
(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)=\tau
.
Moreover, from Proposition
6.4
, there exists
\chi\in k[W^{\circ}]^{*}
such that
\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{f}=\operatorname{val}^{\sigma}_{\chi}
.
From (a),
\operatorname{val}^{\tau}_{\psi^{*}(f)}=\operatorname{val}^{\tau}_{\psi^{*}(\chi)}
because
j^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)=\sigma
.
Therefore, from Proposition
6.4
,
\psi^{*}(f)
is fine for
\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}
.

(c)

We use the notation of the proof of (b).
From the definition of
\psi
, the following diagram is a Cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{k(t)}(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{k(t)}(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}

Thus, the ideal of
\Gamma(W(\sigma),\mathscr{O}_{W})
associated with the closed subscheme
H_{W,f}\cap W(\sigma)
of
W(\sigma)
is generated by
\chi^{-1}f
, and the ideal of
\Gamma(Y_{k(t)}(\tau),\mathscr{O}_{Y_{k(t)}})
associated with the closed subscheme
H_{Y_{k(t)},\psi^{*}(f)}\cap Y_{k(t)}(\tau)
of
Y_{k(t)}(\tau)
is generated by
\psi^{*}(\chi^{-1}f)
.
This shows that the following equation holds from Proposition
4.14
(d):

\displaystyle H_{Y_{k(t)},\psi^{*}(f)}\cap Y_{k(t)}(\tau)

\displaystyle=(H_{W,f}\cap W(\sigma))\times_{W(\sigma)}Y_{k(t)}(\tau)

\displaystyle=(H_{W,f}\times_{W}Y_{k(t)})\cap Y_{k(t)}(\tau)

Therefore, the statement holds.

(d)

We use the notation in the proof of (c).
Then all small squares in the following diagram are Cartesian squares from Proposition
4.14
(c) and (d):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{Y_{k(t)},\psi^{*}(f)}\cap(Y_{k(t)})_{\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{k(t)}(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(k(t))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{W,f}\cap W_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{pr}_{2})*}

Thus, from the assumption,
H_{W,f}\cap W_{\sigma}
is smooth over
k
.
Hence, from the generic smoothness, the lower morphism
H_{W,f}\cap W_{\sigma}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
in the above diagram is smooth over the generic point of
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
.
Therefore,
H_{Y_{k(t)},\psi^{*}(f)}\cap(Y_{k(t)})_{\tau}
is smooth over
\operatorname{Spec}(k(t))
.

∎

7.
Strictly toroidal models of hypersurfaces of mock toric varieties

At first, we consider the following definition.

Definition 7.1

(
[
6
]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{X}
be a flat and separated
\mathscr{R}
-scheme of finite type, and let
x\in\mathscr{X}_{k}
be a point.
We say that
\mathscr{X}
is strictly toroidal at
x
if there exist a toric monoid
S
, an open neighbourhood
U
of
x
in
\mathscr{X}
, and a smooth morphism of
\mathscr{R}
-schemes
U\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R}[S]/(\chi^{\omega}-t^{q}))
, where
q
is a positive rational number,
\omega
is an element of
S
, and
\chi^{\omega}
is a torus invariant monomial in
k[S]
associated with
\omega
such that
k[S]/(\chi^{\omega})
is reduced.
We call that
\mathscr{X}
is
strictly toroidal
if it is so at any
x\in\mathscr{X}_{k}
.

For example, if the closed fiber
\mathcal{X}_{k}
is a simple normal crossing divisor of
\mathcal{X}
,
\mathcal{X}
is strictly toroidal.
A strictly toroidal model of a smooth proper
\mathscr{K}
-variety
X
plays an important role in computing a stable birational volume of
X
(cf.
[
6
, Lemma 3.3.5]
).
We can construct a strictly toroidal model of a Newton non-degenerate hypersurface of a toric variety combinatorially and compute a stable birational volume of it(
[
5
, Theorem 3.14]
).

In this section, our goal is to consider sufficient conditions for constructing a strictly toroidal model of a hypersurface of a mock toric variety and construct it.
We remark that in
[
5
]
, they consider the hypersurfaces of toric varieties over
\mathscr{R}
.
However, in this article, we first construct a hypersurface of a mock toric variety over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
and subsequently carry out a base change from
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
to
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
.

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field with
\operatorname{char}(k)=0
.
In this section, we use the notation in Proposition
4.14
and the following notation.

•

From Proposition
4.14
,
\pi
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}
and
\Delta
.
Let
Y
denote a mock toric variety induced by
Z
along
\pi_{*}\colon X(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)})\rightarrow X(\Delta)
and
s
.

•

We remark that if
\Delta^{\prime}=\{\{0_{(N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z})_{\mathbb{R}}}\}\}
, we have
W=Z^{1}_{\pi^{1}}
and
Y=Z_{\pi}
.

•

Let
M
and
M^{\prime}
be dual lattices of
N
and
N^{\prime}
.

•

For
\varphi\in\Phi
, let
M_{\varphi}
and
M^{\prime}_{\varphi}
denote the dual lattice of
N/N_{\varphi}
and
N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi})
respectively.

•

Let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denote a natural pairing of
(N,M)
,
(N^{\prime},M^{\prime})
,
(N\oplus\mathbb{Z},M\oplus\mathbb{Z})
, or
(N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z},M^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z})
.
In particular, the pairings of
(N\oplus\mathbb{Z},M\oplus\mathbb{Z})
, or
(N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z},M^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z})
are defined as follows:

(N\oplus\mathbb{Z})\times(M\oplus\mathbb{Z})\ni((v,a),(\omega,b))\mapsto\langle v,\omega\rangle+ab\in\mathbb{Z}

(N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z})\times(M^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z})\ni((v^{\prime},a),(\omega^{\prime},b))\mapsto\langle v^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}\rangle+ab\in\mathbb{Z}

We identify elements in
N
as torus invariant valuations on
T_{N}
so that we can check that
\langle v,\omega\rangle=v(\chi^{\omega})
.

•

We use the notation in Proposition
4.14
(i).
Then there exists isomorphisms
\mu\times p_{0}\colon Z^{1}_{\pi^{1}}\rightarrow Z_{\pi}\times{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}
and
\mu^{*}\otimes p^{*}_{0}\colon k[Z_{\pi}]\otimes_{k}k[t,t^{-1}]\rightarrow k[Z^{1}_{\pi^{1}}]
.
We regard
k[Z_{\pi}]
and
k[t,t^{-1}]
as sub rings of
k[Z^{1}_{\pi^{1}}]
by
\mu^{*}
and
p^{*}_{0}
, so we sometimes omit
\mu^{*}
and
p^{*}_{0}
.

•

Let
\iota^{\prime\prime}
denote the closed immersion
W\rightarrow X(\Delta^{\prime})
.

•

For
\varphi\in\Phi
, let
\iota^{\prime\prime}_{\varphi}
denote
(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{*}\circ\iota^{\prime\prime}|_{W\cap X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi})}
.

•

Let
\mathscr{R}
be a valuation ring defined as follows:

\mathscr{R}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}k[[t^{\frac{1}{n}}]]

•

Let
\mathscr{K}
be a fraction field of R.
We remark that
\mathscr{K}
is written as follows:

\mathscr{K}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}k((t^{\frac{1}{n}}))

•

Let
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k[t])
be a morphism of affine scheme induced from a
k
-morphism
k[t]\hookrightarrow\mathscr{R}
whose image of
t
is
t
.

The following proposition concerns units in
k[Z^{1}_{\pi^{1}}]
.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
w\in(\pi^{1}_{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta\times\Delta_{!}))
be an element and
\chi^{\prime}\in k[Z^{1}_{\pi^{1}}]
be an unit.
From Proposition
4.14
(i), there exists
\chi\in k[Z_{\pi}]^{*}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\chi^{\prime}=\chi t^{n}
.
Then
\operatorname{val}_{\chi^{\prime},\pi^{1}}(w)=\operatorname{val}_{\chi,\pi}((\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}(w))+n(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{\mathbb{R}}(w)
.

Proof.

Both functions
\operatorname{val}_{\chi^{\prime},\pi^{1}}
and
\operatorname{val}_{\chi,\pi}\circ(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}+n(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{\mathbb{R}}
are continuous and are preserved by the scalar product of non-negative real numbers by Proposition
6.16
.
Thus, it is enough to show that
\operatorname{val}_{\chi^{\prime},\pi^{1}}(w)=\operatorname{val}_{\chi,\pi}((\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}(w))+n(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{\mathbb{R}}(w)
for any
w\in(\pi^{1}_{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta\times\Delta_{!}))\cap(N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z})
.
Let
w\in(\pi^{1}_{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta\times\Delta_{!}))\cap(N^{\prime}\oplus\mathbb{Z})
be an element.
Then from Proposition
4.14
(j), the following equation follows:

\displaystyle\operatorname{val}_{\chi^{\prime},\pi^{1}}(w)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Z^{1},\pi^{1}}(w)(\chi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{Z,\pi}(\mathrm{pr}_{1}(w))(\chi)+\mathrm{pr}_{2}(w)(t^{n})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{val}_{\chi,\pi}((\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}(w))+n(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{\mathbb{R}}(w)

∎

We note some properties of
\Delta^{\prime}
, and these properties are needed to construct strictly toroidal models of hypersurfaces of mock toric varieties.

Definition 7.3
.

We define some properties of
\Delta^{\prime}
as follows:

•

Let
\Delta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{sp}}
denote a subset of
\Delta^{\prime}
defined as follows:

\Delta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{sp}}=\{\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}\mid\sigma\cap(N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}\times\{1\})\neq\emptyset\}

We remark that the following equation holds:

\Delta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{sp}}=\{\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}\mid(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)\neq\{0\}\}

•

Let
\Delta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{bdd}}
denote a subset of
\Delta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{sp}}
defined as follows:

\Delta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{bdd}}=\{\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{sp}}\mid\sigma\cap(N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}\times\{1\})\mathrm{\ \ is\ bounded.}\}

•

We call that
\Delta^{\prime}
is
compactly arranged
if for every
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{bdd}}
, and
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}
such that
\sigma_{1}\cup\sigma_{2}\subset\tau
, there exists
\sigma_{3}\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{bdd}}
such that
\sigma_{1}\cup\sigma_{2}\subset\sigma_{3}
.

•

We call that
\Delta^{\prime}
is
generically unimodular
if every
\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}
such that
(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)=\{0\}
is unimodular.

•

We call that
\Delta^{\prime}
is
specifically reduced
if for every
\gamma\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{bdd}}
such that
\dim(\gamma)=1
, we have
\gamma\cap(N^{\prime}\times\{1\})\neq\emptyset
.

The following proposition gives sufficient conditions for some conditions in Definition
7.3
.

Proposition 7.4
.

We keep the notation in Definition
7.3
.
Then the following statements follow:

(a)

If
\Delta^{\prime}
is a simplicial fan, then
\Delta^{\prime}
is compactly arranged.

(b)

If
\Delta^{\prime}
is a unimodular fan, then
\Delta^{\prime}
is compactly arranged and generically unimodular.

Proof.

We show these statements from (a) to (b).

(a)

Let
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{bdd}}
, and
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}
be cones such that
\sigma_{1}\cup\sigma_{2}\subset\tau
.
Then
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
are faces of
\tau
.
Because
\tau
is a simplicial cone, there exists a face
\sigma_{3}
of
\tau
such that
\sigma_{3}
is generated by
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
.
From the definition of
\sigma_{3}
, we have
\sigma_{3}\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{bdd}}
and
\sigma_{1}\cup\sigma_{2}\subset\sigma_{3}
.

(b)

The sub fan
\Delta^{\prime}_{0}
of
\Delta^{\prime}
is unimodular too.
Thus,
\Delta^{\prime}
is generically unimodular.
Moreover,
\Delta^{\prime}
is compactly arranged from (a).

∎

When
\Delta^{\prime}
is specifically reduced,
\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)
has the ”same” conditions for any
\varphi\in\Phi
.

Proposition 7.5
.

We keep the notation in Definition
7.3
.
We assume that
\Delta^{\prime}
is specifically reduced.
Then for any
\varphi\in\Phi
and any
\gamma\in\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)
with
\dim(\gamma)=1
and
\gamma\not\subset(N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi}))_{\mathbb{R}}\times\{0\}
, we have
\gamma\cap((N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi}))\times\{1\})\neq\emptyset
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element.
From the condition (3) in Definition
3.1
and Lemma
8.1
, there is a one-to-one correspondence of the following two sets by
(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{\mathbb{R}}
:

\{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}\mid\dim(\gamma^{\prime})=1,\gamma^{\prime}\not\subset N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}\times\{0\}\}

\{\gamma\in\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)\mid\dim(\gamma)=1,\gamma\not\subset(N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi}))_{\mathbb{R}}\times\{0\}\}

Let
\gamma\in\Delta^{\prime}(\varphi)
be a cone such that
\dim(\gamma)=1
and
\gamma\not\subset(N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi}))\times\{0\}
.
Then there exists
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}
such that
(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{\mathbb{R}}(\gamma^{\prime})=\gamma
,
\dim(\gamma^{\prime})=1
, and
\gamma^{\prime}\not\subset N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}\times\{0\}
from the above argument.
In particular,
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\operatorname{bdd}}
.
Thus, from the assumption,
\gamma^{\prime}\cap(N^{\prime}\times\{1\})\neq\emptyset
.
Hence, there exists
v^{\prime}\in N^{\prime}
such that
(v^{\prime},1)\in\gamma^{\prime}
.
Because
(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{\mathbb{R}}(\gamma^{\prime})=\gamma
, we have
(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}(v^{\prime}),1)\in\gamma
.
Therefore,
\gamma\cap((N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi}))\times\{1\})\neq\emptyset
.
∎

The reason for introducing the definition of specifically reduced fans is evident from the following proposition.
While we later introduce strictly toroidal schemes over a valuation ring, the definition of specifically reduced fans is crucial for the construction of strictly toroidal models of hypersurfaces of mock toric varieties.

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
f\in k[W^{\circ}]
be a function.
We assume that
\Delta^{\prime}
is specifically reduced and
f
is non-degenerate for
\Delta^{\prime}
.
Let
x\in H_{W,f}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\{0\}
be a point.
Then there exists

(1)

An open neighborhood
U\subset W
of
x

(2)

A toric monoid
S

(3)

An element
\omega\in S

(4)

An
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
-morphism
p\colon U\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k[t][S]/(t-\chi^{\omega}))

such that

(i)

The restriction
p|_{U\cap H_{W,f}}\colon U\cap H_{W,f}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k[t][S]/(t-\chi^{\omega}))
is a smooth morphism.

(ii)

A ring
k[S]/(\chi^{\omega})
is reduced.

Proof.

Let
\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}
be a cone and
\varphi\in\Phi
be an element such that
x\in H_{W,f}\cap W_{\sigma}
and
\sigma\in\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}
.
From the assumption of
x
, we have
\sigma\not\subset N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}\times\{0\}
.
Let
\sigma_{\varphi}
denote
(q^{\prime}_{\varphi}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
Then from Proposition
6.2
, there exists
g_{\varphi}\in k[M^{\prime}_{\varphi}\oplus\mathbb{Z}]
such that
H_{X(\sigma_{\varphi}),g_{\varphi}}\times_{X(\sigma_{\varphi})}W(\sigma)=H_{W,f}\cap W(\sigma)
.
We remark that
\iota^{\prime\prime}_{\varphi}(W(\sigma))
is an open subscheme of
X(\sigma_{\varphi})
and
H_{X(\Delta^{\prime}_{\varphi}),g_{\varphi}}\cap O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}\not\subset O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}
from Lemma
6.10
.
Moreover, from the proof of Proposition
3.4
(c),
(H_{X(\sigma_{\varphi}),g_{\varphi}}\cap O_{\sigma_{\varphi}})\times_{X(\sigma_{\varphi})}W(\sigma)=H_{W,f}\cap W_{\sigma}
.
Because
f
is nondegenerate for
\Delta^{\prime}
,
H_{W,f}\cap W_{\sigma}\cong H_{X(\sigma_{\varphi}),g_{\varphi}}\cap O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}\cap\iota^{\prime\prime}_{\varphi}(W(\sigma))
is smooth over
k
.

From the assumption and Proposition
7.5
,
\sigma_{\varphi}\subset(N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi}))_{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and
\sigma_{\varphi}\not\subset(N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi}))_{\mathbb{R}}\times\{0\}
, and
\gamma\cap((N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi}))\times\{1\})\neq\emptyset
for every 1-dimensional ray
\gamma\preceq\sigma_{\varphi}
with
\gamma\cap((N^{\prime}/s(N_{\varphi}))_{\mathbb{R}}\times\{1\})\neq\emptyset
.

Thus, from Lemma
8.28
, there exists

•

A toric monoid
S

•

An alement
\omega\in S

•

A morphism
\theta\colon X(\sigma_{\varphi})\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k[t][S]/(t-\chi^{\omega}))
over
\operatorname{Spec}(k[t])=\mathbb{A}^{1}

•

An open subset
U^{\prime}
of
\iota^{\prime\prime}_{\varphi}(W(\sigma))

such that

(1)

H_{X(\sigma_{\varphi}),g_{\varphi}}\cap O_{\sigma_{\varphi}}\cap\iota^{\prime\prime}_{\varphi}(W^{\prime}(\sigma))\subset U^{\prime}

(2)

The restriction
\theta|_{H_{X(\sigma_{\varphi}),g_{\varphi}}\cap U^{\prime}}\colon H_{X(\sigma_{\varphi}),g_{\varphi}}\cap U^{\prime}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k[t][S]/(t-\chi^{\omega}))
is smooth.

(3)

A ring
k[S]/(\chi^{\omega})
is reduced.

Let
U
denote an open subset
{\iota^{\prime\prime}}_{\varphi}^{-1}(U^{\prime})
of
W
.
Then
H_{W,f}\cap W_{\sigma}\subset U
.
In particular,
x\in U
.
Let
p
denote a composition
\theta\circ\iota^{\prime\prime}_{\varphi}|_{U}
.
We remark that
\iota^{\prime\prime}_{\varphi}
is an
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
-morphism because of the mock toric structure of
W
, so
p
is an
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
-morphism.
Moreover, we can check that the restriction
p|_{H_{W,f}\cap U}\colon{H_{W,f}\cap U}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k[t][S]/(t-\chi^{\omega}))
is smooth.
∎

The following lengthy proposition provides sufficient conditions for the construction of a strictly toroidal model of a hypersurface of a mock toric variety, which is one of the aims of this article.

Proposition 7.7
.

We create an itemized list for the notation.

•

Let
f\in k[W^{\circ}]
be a function.
We assume that
f
is fine for
\Delta^{\prime}
.

•

We assume that
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime})=(\pi^{1}_{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta\times\Delta_{!}))
.

•

Let
p^{\prime}
denote the following composition:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta\times\Delta_{!})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{1}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{*}}

•

Let
q^{\prime}
denote the restriction
p^{\prime}|_{H_{W,f}}\colon H_{W,f}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
.

•

Let
p^{\prime}_{\mathscr{R}}\colon\mathscr{W}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
and
q^{\prime}_{\mathscr{R}}\colon\mathscr{H}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
denote the base change morphisms of
p^{\prime}
and
q^{\prime}
along
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
.

•

p^{\prime}_{\mathscr{K}}\colon\mathscr{W}_{\mathscr{K}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{K})
and
q^{\prime}_{\mathscr{K}}\colon\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{K})
denote the base change morphisms of
p^{\prime}_{\mathscr{R}}
and
q^{\prime}_{\mathscr{R}}
along
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{K})\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
.
We remark that natural morphisms
\mathscr{W}_{\mathscr{K}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{W}
and
\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{H}
are open immersions.

•

Let
\alpha\colon Y_{\mathscr{K}}\rightarrow Y_{k(t)}
be a dominant morphism defined by a base change along a field extension
\mathscr{K}/{k(t)}
.
We regard
Y_{\mathscr{K}}
as a mock toric over
\mathscr{K}
induced by
Y
along a field extension
\mathscr{K}/{k(t)}
(cf. Proposition
4.10
).

•

Let
\mathscr{H}^{\circ}
denote a Cartesian product
H^{\circ}_{W,f}\times_{{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{K})
.

Then the following statements follow:

(a)

The morphism
p^{\prime}_{\mathscr{R}}
is flat and
q^{\prime}_{\mathscr{R}}
is separated and of finite presentation over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
.

(b)

As a closed subscheme of
X(\Delta^{\prime})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{K})=X(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)})\times_{k}\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{K})
,
\mathscr{W}_{\mathscr{K}}
is equal to
Y_{\mathscr{K}}
.

(c)

If
Z
is proper over
k
, then
q^{\prime}_{\mathscr{R}}
is proper.

(d)

If
H^{\circ}_{W,f}
is reduced and every irreducible components of
H^{\circ}_{W,f}
dominates
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}
, then
q^{\prime}_{\mathscr{R}}
is flat.

(e)

The scheme theoretic closure of
\mathscr{H}^{\circ}
in
\mathscr{W}_{\mathscr{K}}
is equal to
\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}
.

(f)

If the assumption of (d) holds, then the scheme theoretic closure of
\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}
in
\mathscr{W}
is equal to
\mathscr{H}
.

(g)

If
\Delta^{\prime}
is generically unimodular and
f
is non-degenerate for
\Delta^{\prime}
, then
\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}
is smooth over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{K})
.

(h)

Let
y\in\mathscr{H}_{k}
be a point.
If
\Delta^{\prime}
is specifically reduced and
f
is non-degenerate for
\Delta^{\prime}
, then there exists

(1)

An open neighborhood
U\subset\mathscr{H}
of
y

(2)

A toric monoid
S

(3)

An element
\omega\in S

(4)

A
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
-morphism
\beta\colon U\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R}[S]/(t-\chi^{\omega}))

such that

(I)

The morphism
\beta
is a smooth morphism.

(II)

A ring
k[S]/(\chi^{\omega})
is reduced.

(i)

We assume that
f
is non-degenerate for
\Delta^{\prime}
and
f\in k[W^{\circ}]
is irreducible.
Let
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{r}\in\mathscr{K}[Y^{\circ}_{\mathscr{K}}]
be a prime decomposition of
\alpha^{*}(\psi^{*}(f))\in\mathscr{K}[Y^{\circ}_{\mathscr{K}}]
.
Then through the identification with
\mathscr{W}_{\mathscr{K}}
and
Y_{\mathscr{K}}
, the following equation holds as a closed subscheme of
Y^{\circ}_{\mathscr{K}}
:

\mathscr{H}^{\circ}=\coprod_{1\leq i\leq r}H^{\circ}_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},f_{i}}

Similarly, the following equation as a closed subscheme of
Y_{\mathscr{K}}
too:

\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}=\coprod_{1\leq i\leq r}H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},f_{i}}

(j)

We assume that the assumptions of (d), (h), and (i) hold.
We use the notation in (i).
For
1\leq i\leq r
, let
\mathscr{H}_{i}
denote the scheme theoretic closure of
H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},f_{i}}
in
\mathscr{W}
.
Then
\mathscr{H}_{i}
is flat over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
and
(\mathscr{H}_{i})_{\mathscr{K}}=H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},f_{i}}
for any
1\leq i\leq r
.
Moreover, as a closed subscheme of
\mathscr{W}
, the following equation holds:

\mathscr{H}=\coprod_{1\leq i\leq r}\mathscr{H}_{i}

(k)

We assume that the assumption of (c), (g), and (j) holds.
We use the notation in (j).
Then
\mathscr{H}_{i}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
is a proper strict toroidal model of a smooth variety
(\mathscr{H}_{i})_{\mathscr{K}}=H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},f_{i}}
for any
1\leq i\leq r
.

Proof.

We prove the statements from (a) to (q) in order.

(a)

From the definition of
W
, the restriction
(\pi^{1})_{*}|_{W}\colon W\rightarrow Z^{1}
is dominant from Corollary
4.6
.
Because
Z^{1}
is isomorphic to
Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
,
p^{\prime}
is a dominant morphism.
Moreover, from Proposition
3.4
(a),
W
is an integral scheme .
Thus,
p^{\prime}
is a flat morphism, so
p^{\prime}_{\mathscr{R}}
is flat too.

We show that
q^{\prime}
is separated and of finite presentation.
Indeed, toric varieties are separated and of finite type over
k
.
Thus, the toric morphism
(\mathrm{pr}_{2}\circ\pi^{1})_{*}\colon X(\Delta^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
is separated and of finite type over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
, in particular, of finite presentation over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
.
Moreover, a closed immersion
H_{W,f}\hookrightarrow X(\Delta^{\prime})
is separated and of finite presentation because
X(\Delta^{\prime})
is a Noetherian scheme.
Thus,
q^{\prime}
is separated and of finite presentation, so
q^{\prime}_{\mathscr{R}}
is so.

(b)

From Proposition
4.14
(c), we have already shown that the isomorphism
X(\Delta^{\prime})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}\operatorname{Spec}(k(t))\cong X(\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)})\times_{\operatorname{Spec}(k)}\operatorname{Spec}(k(t))
induces the isomorphism
W\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}\operatorname{Spec}(k(t))\cong Y_{k(t)}
.
Thus, by the base change along
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{K})\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k(t))
, the statement holds.

(c)

We show that
q^{\prime}
is proper.
Indeed, there exists the following diagram whose all small squares are Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\prime})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta\times\Delta_{!})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{1}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{pr}_{2})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{pr}_{1})_{*}}

Because
Z
is proper over
k
,
Z^{1}
is proper over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
.
Moreover, from the assumption of
\Delta^{\prime}
,
\pi^{1}_{*}\colon X(\Delta^{\prime})\rightarrow X(\Delta\times\Delta_{!})
is proper.
Thus,
W
is proper over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
.
Therefore,
p^{\prime}
and
q^{\prime}
are proper morphisms because
H_{W,f}\hookrightarrow W
is a closed immersion.
Therefore, the statement holds.

(d)

We show that
q^{\prime}
is flat.
We recall that
k
is algebraically closed, and
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
is integral and regular.
Moreover,
H_{W,f}
is a scheme theoretic closure of
H^{\circ}_{W,f}
, in particular,
H^{\circ}_{W,f}
is a dense open subset of
H_{W,f}
.
Thus,
H_{W,f}
is reduced and for any
y\in H_{W,f}
, there exists an irreducible components
E
of
H^{\circ}_{W,f}
such that
y\in\overline{E}
.
Therefore, from the assumption,
q^{\prime}
is a flat morphism.
In particular,
q^{\prime}_{\mathscr{R}}
is also flat.

(e)

From (b), We can identify with
\mathscr{W}_{\mathscr{K}}
and
Y_{\mathscr{K}}
.
Thus, there exists the following commutative diagram whose all squares are Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\circ}_{\mathscr{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\circ}_{k(t)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(k(t))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{1}}_{,k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

We use the notation Proposition
6.18
and Proposition
6.15
.
Then we have
\mathscr{H}^{\circ}=H^{\circ}_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},\alpha^{*}\psi^{*}(f)}
on the identification with
\mathscr{W}_{\mathscr{K}}
and
Y_{\mathscr{K}}
.
In particular, the scheme theoretic closure of
\mathscr{H}^{\circ}
in
\mathscr{W}_{\mathscr{K}}
is equal to
H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},\alpha^{*}\psi^{*}(f)}
.
Moreover, from the assumption of
f
, Proposition
6.18
(b) and (c), Proposition
6.15
(b) and (c), and Proposition
4.14
(c), there exists the following diagram whose all small squares are Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},\alpha^{*}\psi^{*}(f)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{Y_{k(t)},\psi^{*}(f)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{W,f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\mathscr{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{k(t)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spec}(k(t))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

The above diagram indicates that
H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},\alpha^{*}\psi^{*}(f)}
is equal to
\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}
as the closed subscheme
Y_{\mathscr{K}}=\mathscr{W}_{\mathscr{K}}
.
Therefore, the statement holds.

(f)

From (a) and (d),
\mathscr{H}
and
\mathscr{W}
are flat over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
.
Thus, from Lemma
8.32
,
\mathscr{H}
is a scheme theoretic closure of
\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}
in
\mathscr{W}
.

(g)

We use the notation in (e).
Then
\alpha^{*}\psi^{*}(f)
is non-degenerate for
\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}
from Proposition
6.18
(d) and Proposition
6.15
(d).
In the proof of (e), we have already checked that
H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},\alpha^{*}\psi^{*}(f)}=\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}
.
Thus, from Proposition
6.13
,
\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}
is smooth over
\mathscr{K}
.

(h)

We remark that
H_{W,f}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\{0\}=\mathscr{H}_{k}
as a
k
-scheme.
Thus, from Proposition
7.6
, there exists

(1)

An open neighborhood
U_{0}\subset W
of
y

(2)

A toric monoid
S

(3)

An element
\omega\in S

(4)

An
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
-morphism
\theta\colon U_{0}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k[t][S]/(t-\chi^{\omega}))

such that

(I)

The restriction
\theta|_{U_{0}\cap H_{W,f}}\colon U_{0}\cap H_{W,f}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(k[t][S]/(t-\chi^{\omega}))
is a smooth morphism.

(II)

A ring
k[S]/(\chi^{\omega})
is reduced.

By the base change along
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
, let
V
denote an open subscheme
U_{0}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
of
\mathscr{W}
and
\beta
denote a morphism
\theta\times\mathrm{id}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})}\colon V\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R}[S]/(t-\chi^{\omega}))
over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
.
Then the restriction
\beta|_{\mathscr{H}\cap V}\colon\mathscr{H}\cap V\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R}[S]/(t-\chi^{\omega}))
is a smooth morphism.
Let
U
denote an open subset
\mathscr{H}\cap V
of
\mathscr{H}
.
We remark that
y\in U
.

(i)

We use the notation in (e).
From the argument in (g),
\alpha^{*}\psi^{*}(f)
is non-degenerate for
\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}
.
Thus, from Proposition
6.12
(b),
f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}
are non-degenerate for
\Delta^{\prime}_{(0)}
.
In particular,
H^{\circ}_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},f_{i}}
is smooth over
\mathscr{K}
for any
1\leq i\leq r
.
Moreover, from the proof of (e), we can identify with
\mathscr{H}^{\circ}
and
H^{\circ}_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},\alpha^{*}\psi^{*}(f)}
.
Thus, the following equation is the connected decomposition of
\mathscr{H}^{\circ}
:

\mathscr{H}^{\circ}=\coprod_{1\leq i\leq r}H^{\circ}_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},f_{i}}

From (e) and Proposition
6.12
(c), we can identify with
\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}
and
H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},\alpha^{*}\psi^{*}f}
, and the following equation is the connected decomposition of
\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}
:

\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}=\coprod_{1\leq i\leq r}H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},f_{i}}

(j)

We use the notation in (i).
From (f) and (i), we remark that the number of connected components of
\mathscr{H}
is less than and equal to
r
.
In addition to this, we remark that the generic fiber of a connected component of
\mathscr{H}
is non-empty and can be decomposed into a disjoint union of some
H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},f_{i}}
.
Let
E
be a connected component of
\mathscr{H}
.
From the first remark,
E
is an open and closed subscheme of
\mathscr{H}
.
In particular,
E
is flat and locally of finite presentation over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
from (a) and (d).
Moreover, from (h),
\mathscr{H}_{k}
is reduced, so
E_{k}
is reduced too.
Thus, from Lemma
8.35
(b),
E_{\mathscr{K}}
is connected.
This shows that there exists a unique
1\leq i\leq r
such that
E_{\mathscr{K}}=H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},f_{i}}
.
We recall that
E
and
\mathscr{W}
is flat over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
and
E
is a closed subscheme of
\mathscr{W}
.
Thus, from Lemma
8.32
,
E=\mathscr{H}_{i}
.
Hence, for any
1\leq i\leq r
,
\mathscr{H}_{i}
is flat over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
,
(\mathscr{H}_{i})_{\mathscr{K}}=H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},f_{i}}
, and
\mathscr{H}_{i}
is a connected component of
\mathscr{H}
.

(k)

From (c) and (j),
\mathscr{H}
is proper over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
, and
\mathscr{H}_{i}
is a closed subscheme of
\mathscr{H}
for any
1\leq i\leq r
.
Thus,
\mathscr{H}_{i}
is proper over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
for any
1\leq i\leq r
.
From (g) and (i),
\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}
is smooth over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{K})
and
(\mathscr{H}_{i})_{\mathscr{K}}
is an open subscheme of
\mathscr{H}_{\mathscr{K}}
for any
1\leq i\leq r
.
Thus,
(\mathscr{H}_{i})_{\mathscr{K}}
is smooth over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{K})
for any
1\leq i\leq r
.
From (j),
\mathscr{H}_{i}
is flat over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathscr{R})
for any
1\leq i\leq r
.
Thus, from (h),
\mathscr{H}_{i}
is a proper strictly toroidal model of a smooth
\mathscr{K}
-variety
H_{Y_{\mathscr{K}},f_{i}}
for any
1\leq i\leq r
.

∎

8.
Appendix

In this section, we prove the lemmas needed in this article.
We use the notation in Section 2.
The lemmas presented here are all fundamental and well-known, but they are frequently used in the proofs of propositions in the main article.
Therefore, they are compiled here as an appendix.

8.1.
Toric Varieties Part.1

In this subsection, we prove some lemmas about toric varieties.
While some lemmas have already been published in the literature(
[
1
]
and
[
2
]
), the author has decided to describe them here because he thought the details of the proofs are crucial.

The following lemma is elementary, but it is important for the main article.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
V,V^{\prime}
be
\mathbb{R}
-linear spaces and
f\colon V\rightarrow V^{\prime}
be an
\mathbb{R}
-linear map.
Let
\Delta
denote a polyhedral convex fan in
V
and assume that the restriction of
f
to
\operatorname{Supp}(\Delta)
is injective.
Then the following statements follow:

(a)

For any
\sigma\in\Delta
,
f(\sigma)
is a polyhedral convex cone.

(b)

There exists a one-to-one correspondence with faces of
\sigma
and those of
f(\sigma)

by
f
.

(c)

The following set
\Delta^{\prime}
is a polyhedral convex fan in
V^{\prime}
:

\Delta^{\prime}=\{f(\sigma)\mid\sigma\in\Delta\}

(d)

If
\Delta
is a strongly convex polyhedral fan,
\Delta^{\prime}
is so.

Proof.

We prove the statements from (a) to (d) in order.

(a)

Because
f
is a linear map,
f(\sigma)
is a polyhedral convex cone in
V^{\prime}
.

(b)

From the assumption,
\langle\sigma\rangle\cap\ker(f)=0
, so that there exists a linear map
g_{\sigma}\colon V^{\prime}\rightarrow V
such that
(g_{\sigma}\circ f)|_{\langle\sigma\rangle}
is the identity map of
\langle\sigma\rangle
.
Let
W
and
W^{\prime}
denote the dual spaces of
V
and
V^{\prime}
, respectively.
Then for any
\omega\in\sigma^{\vee}
, we claim that
g^{*}_{\sigma}(\omega)\in(f(\sigma))^{\vee}
and
f(\sigma\cap\omega^{\perp})=f(\sigma)\cap g^{*}_{\sigma}(\omega)^{\perp}
.
Indeed, for any
v\in\sigma=g_{\sigma}(f(\sigma))
, the following equation holds:

\displaystyle\langle f(v),g^{*}_{\sigma}(\omega)\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle g_{\sigma}(f(v)),\omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle v,\omega\rangle

On the other hand, for any
\omega^{\prime}\in(f(\sigma))^{\vee}
, we claim that
f^{*}(\omega^{\prime})\in\sigma^{\vee}
and
f(\sigma\cap f^{*}(\omega^{\prime})^{\perp})=f(\sigma)\cap(\omega^{\prime})^{\perp}
.
Indeed, for any
v\in\sigma
, the following equation holds:

\displaystyle\langle v,f^{*}(\omega^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle f(v),\omega^{\prime}\rangle

Thus,
f
induces a one-to-one correspondence faces of
\sigma
and those of
f(\sigma)
because the restriction of
f
for
\langle\sigma\rangle
is injective.

(c)

For any
\sigma\in\Delta
, all faces of
f(\sigma)
are contained in
\Delta^{\prime}
from (b).
In addition, for any
\sigma
and
\tau\in\Delta
,
f(\sigma)\cap f(\tau)=f(\sigma\cap\tau)
from the injectivity of
f
.
From (b),
f(\sigma\cap\tau)
is a common face of
f(\sigma)
and
f(\tau)
, so that
\Delta^{\prime}
is a fan.

(d)

Because
f
is an injective linear map, the statement holds.

∎

The following elementary lemma is needed to prove Lemma
8.5
.

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
N,N^{\prime}
be lattices of finite rank and
\pi\colon N\rightarrow N^{\prime}
be a surjective morphism.
Let
N_{0}
be a sublattice of
N
and assume that
N^{\prime}/\pi(N_{0})
is torsion free and
N_{0}\cap\mathrm{ker}(\pi)=\{0\}
.
Then there is a section
s
of
\pi
such that
s(N^{\prime})
contains
N_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
\pi^{\prime}\colon N\rightarrow N^{\prime}/(\pi(N_{0}))
be a composition of
\pi
and the quotient map from
N^{\prime}
to
N^{\prime}/\pi(N_{0})
.
From the assumption, there is a section
s^{\prime}
of
\pi^{\prime}
.
Then
N
can be decomposed into a direct sum
\mathrm{ker}(\pi^{\prime})\oplus s^{\prime}(N^{\prime}/\pi(N_{0}))
.
By the assumption of
\pi
,
\mathrm{ker}(\pi^{\prime})
can be decomposed into a direct sum
\mathrm{ker}(\pi)\oplus N_{0}
, so that we would take a section
s
of
\pi
as the inverse morphism of the following composition:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}\oplus s^{\prime}(N^{\prime}/\pi(N_{0}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

Then
N_{0}\subset s(N^{\prime})
.
∎

In this article, there are many cases when toric morphisms are algebraic torus fibrations.
Therefore, the author has decided to summarize this morphism here.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
N,N^{\prime}
be lattices of finite rank,
\pi\colon N\rightarrow N^{\prime}
be a surjective morphism,
s\colon N^{\prime}\rightarrow N
be a section of
\pi
, and
\sigma\subset N_{\mathbb{R}}
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral cone.
We assume that
\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N\subset s(N^{\prime})
.
Then the following statements follow:

(a)

Let
\sigma^{\prime}
denote
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
.
Then,
\sigma^{\prime}
is a strongly convex rational polyhedral cone in
N^{\prime}
.

(b)

For any
\tau\preceq\sigma
, we have
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\preceq\sigma^{\prime}
.

(c)

Conversely, for any
\tau^{\prime}\preceq\sigma^{\prime}
, there exists a unique
\tau\preceq\sigma
such that
\tau^{\prime}=\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)
.

(d)

For any
\tau\preceq\sigma
, let
\tau^{\prime}
denote
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)
.
Then, the following diagram of toric morphisms is a Cartesian diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}

(e)

For the notation in (d),
\pi_{*}(O_{\tau})=O_{\pi_{\tau^{\prime}}}
and the following diagram induced by toric morphisms is a Cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{O_{\tau}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{O_{\tau^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}

Proof.

We prove these statements from (a) to (e) in order.

(a)

From the assumption, the restriction of
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}
to
\langle\sigma\rangle
is injective.
Thus, from Lemma
8.1
(a) and (d),
\sigma^{\prime}
is a strongly convex polyhedral cone.
Because
\pi
is a morphism of lattices,
\sigma^{\prime}
is a rational polyhedral cone.

(b), (c)

The statements follow from the injectivity of the restriction
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\langle\sigma\rangle}
and Lemma
8.1
(b).

(d), (e)

Let
\lambda\colon N^{\prime}\oplus\mathrm{ker}(\pi)\rightarrow N
be a lattice morphism defined as follows:

N^{\prime}\oplus\mathrm{ker}(\pi)\ni(v^{\prime},w)\mapsto s(v^{\prime})+w\in N

Because
s
is a section of
\pi
,
\lambda
is isomorphic.
In addition, from the assumption of
s
,
\lambda_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma^{\prime}\times\{0\})=\sigma
, so that
\lambda
induces the toric isomorphism
\lambda_{*}\colon X(\sigma^{\prime})\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rank}(\mathrm{ker}(\pi))}=X(\sigma^{\prime}\times\{0\})\rightarrow X(\sigma)
and there exists the following diagram of toric morphisms:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma^{\prime})\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{rank}(\mathrm{ker}(\pi))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}

Hence, we may replace
N
with
N^{\prime}\oplus\ker(\pi)
,
\pi
with
\mathrm{pr}_{1}
, and
\sigma
with
\sigma^{\prime}\times\{0\}
.
Therefore, statements (d) and (e) follow immediately from the diagram above.

∎

The following lemma is needed for Lemma
8.5
.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
N,N^{\prime},L
be lattices of finite rank,
f\colon N\rightarrow N^{\prime}
and
\pi\colon L\rightarrow N
be surjective morphisms, and
s\colon N^{\prime}\rightarrow N
and
u\colon N\rightarrow L
be sections of
f
and
\pi
respectively.
Let
L^{\prime}
denote a lattice
L/u(\ker(f))
and
g
denote the quotient morphism from
L
to
L^{\prime}
.

Then the following statements follow:

(a)

There exist unique morphisms
\pi^{\prime}\colon L^{\prime}\rightarrow N^{\prime}
and
u^{\prime}\colon N^{\prime}\rightarrow L^{\prime}
such that the following diagram commutes and
\pi^{\prime}\circ u^{\prime}=\mathrm{id}_{N^{\prime}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}

(b)

There exists a unique section
t
of
g
such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

(c)

Let
\lambda\colon N^{\prime}\oplus\ker(f)\rightarrow N
and
\mu\colon L^{\prime}\oplus\ker(f)\rightarrow L
be morphisms defined as follows:

N^{\prime}\oplus\mathrm{ker}(f)\ni(x^{\prime},x)\mapsto s(x^{\prime})+x\in N

L^{\prime}\oplus\mathrm{ker}(f)\ni(y^{\prime},x)\mapsto t(y^{\prime})+u(x)\in L

Then
\lambda
and
\mu
are isomorphic and the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\prime}\oplus\ker(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}\oplus\ker(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi^{\prime}{,}\mathrm{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}

Proof.

We prove these statements from (a) to (c) in order.

(a)

From the definition of
L^{\prime}
,
g\circ u(\ker(f))=0
and
f\circ\pi(\ker(g))=0
.
Thus,
u^{\prime}
and
\pi^{\prime}
are induced so that
g\circ u=u^{\prime}\circ f
and
f\circ\pi=\pi^{\prime}\circ g
.

(b)

From sections
u
and
s
,
L
can be decomposed into a direct sum
u(s(N^{\prime}))\oplus\ker(\pi)\oplus u(\ker(f))
.
Thus, we would take a section
t_{0}
of
g
as the inverse morphism of the following isomorphism:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u(s(N^{\prime}))\oplus\ker(\pi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

Then we claim that
t_{0}\circ u^{\prime}=u\circ s
and
\pi\circ t_{0}=s\circ\pi^{\prime}
.
First, because
t_{0}\circ g|_{u\circ s(N^{\prime})}=\mathrm{id}_{u\circ s(N^{\prime})}
, the following equation holds:

\displaystyle t_{0}\circ u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=t_{0}\circ u^{\prime}\circ f\circ s

\displaystyle=t_{0}\circ g\circ u\circ s

\displaystyle=u\circ s

Second, for any
y^{\prime}\in L^{\prime}
, there exists
x^{\prime}\in N^{\prime}
and
y\in\ker(\pi)
such that
y^{\prime}=g(u\circ s(x^{\prime})+y)
and
t_{0}(y^{\prime})=u\circ s(x^{\prime})+y
from the definition of
t_{0}
.
Then the following equation holds:

\displaystyle\pi\circ t_{0}(y^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\pi(u\circ s(x^{\prime})+y)

\displaystyle=\pi\circ u\circ s(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=s(x^{\prime})

On the other hand, the following equation holds:

\displaystyle s\circ\pi^{\prime}(y^{\prime})

\displaystyle=s\circ\pi^{\prime}\circ g(u\circ s(x^{\prime})+y)

\displaystyle=s\circ f\circ\pi(u\circ s(x^{\prime})+y)

\displaystyle=s\circ f(s(x^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=s(x^{\prime})

Thus,
\pi\circ t_{0}=s\circ\pi^{\prime}
.
Finally, we show the uniqueness of such
t
.
For any
y^{\prime}\in L^{\prime}
, there exists
x^{\prime}\in N^{\prime}
and
y\in\ker(\pi)
such that
y^{\prime}=g(u\circ s(x^{\prime})+y)
from the direct sum decomposition of
L
above.
Then for such
t
,

\displaystyle t(g(u\circ s(x^{\prime})))

\displaystyle=t\circ g\circ t\circ u^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=t\circ u^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=u\circ s(x^{\prime})

On the other hand, for such
t
, we have
t\circ g(y)-y\in\ker(g)
.
In addition, the following equation holds:

\displaystyle\pi(t\circ g(y)-y)

\displaystyle=\pi(t\circ g(y))

\displaystyle=s\circ\pi^{\prime}\circ g(y)

\displaystyle=s\circ f\circ\pi(y)

\displaystyle=0

Thus,
t\circ g(y)-y\in\ker(g)\cap\ker(\pi)
.
Because
u
is a section of
\pi
, we have that
\ker(g)\cap\ker(\pi)=u(\ker(f))\cap\ker(\pi)

=0
.
In particular,
t\circ g(y)=y
.
Therefore, for such
t
, we have
t(y^{\prime})=u\circ s(x^{\prime})+y
, so that this shows that
t
is unique.

(c)

Because
s
is a section of
f
,
\lambda
is isomorphic.
Likewise, because
\ker(f)
and
\ker(g)
is isomorphic by the restriction of
u
to
\ker(f)
and
t
is a section of
g
,
\mu
is ismorphic too.
We can check the commutativity of the diagram because
s\circ\pi^{\prime}=\pi\circ t
.

∎

When we consider the structure of mock toric varieties induced by a dominant toric morphism, the following lemma is very important.

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
N,N^{\prime},L
be lattices of finite rank,
f\colon N\rightarrow N^{\prime}
and
\pi\colon L\rightarrow N
be surjective morphisms,
u\colon N\rightarrow L
be sections of
\pi
.
Let
L^{\prime}
denote a lattice
L/u(\ker(f))
and
g
denote the quotient morphism from
L
to
L^{\prime}
.
Let
\sigma
and
\tau
be strongly convex rational polyhedral cones in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
and
L_{\mathbb{R}}
, respectively.
We assume that
\ker(f)\cap(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)=0
,
N^{\prime}/f(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)
is torsion free, and
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma
.

Then the following statements follow:

(a)

Let
\sigma^{\prime}
denote
f_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
and
\tau^{\prime}
denote
g_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)
.
Then
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\tau^{\prime}
are strongly convex rational polyhedral cones in
N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
and
L^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
, respectively.
In addition,
\pi^{\prime}
is compatible with the cones
\tau^{\prime}
and
\sigma^{\prime}
.

(b)

The following diagram of toric varieties commutes and is a Cartesian diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}

Proof.

We prove these statements from (a) to (b).

(a)

From Lemma
8.2
, there exists a section
s
of
f
such that
\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N\subset s(N^{\prime})
.
Moreover, from Lemma
8.4
(b), there exists a section
t
of
g
such that
s\circ\pi^{\prime}=\pi\circ t
and
t\circ u^{\prime}=u\circ s
.
In particular,
t(L^{\prime})=u\circ s(N^{\prime})\oplus\ker(\pi)
.
From the assumption of
\tau
,
\langle\tau\rangle\cap L\subset u(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)\oplus\ker(\pi)
, so that
\langle\tau\rangle\cap L\subset t(L^{\prime})
.
Then from Lemma
8.3
(a), the sets
f_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)
and
g_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)
are strongly convex rational polyhedral cones in
N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
and
L^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
respectively.
In addition, because
\pi^{\prime}\circ g=f\circ\pi
and
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma
, we have
\pi^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau^{\prime})\subset\sigma^{\prime}
.
Thus,
\pi^{\prime}
is compatible with the cones with
\tau^{\prime}
and
\sigma^{\prime}
.

(b)

The commutativity of the diagram follows because
\pi^{\prime}\circ g=f\circ\pi
.
From the proof of Lemma
8.3
(d) and (e) and Lemma
8.4
(c), there exists the following commutative diagram of toric morphisms:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau^{\prime})\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma^{\prime})\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\tau^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\sigma^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{*}^{\prime}{,}\mathrm{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}_{*}}

,where
d
denotes
\mathrm{rank}(\ker(f))
and
\lambda\colon N^{\prime}\oplus\ker(f)\rightarrow N
and
\mu\colon L^{\prime}\oplus\ker(f)\rightarrow L
are isomorphisms defined by the proof of Lemma
8.3
(d) and (e).
In particular, the largest diagram is a Cartesian diagram, and
\lambda_{*}
and
\mu_{*}
are isomorphic.
Thus, the diagram in the statement is a Cartesian diagram.

∎

The following lemma is a basic well-known result about the torus orbit decomposition of the toric varieties.

Lemma 8.6
.

[
1
, Prop. 3.2.6]

Let
N
be a lattice of finite rank,
\Delta
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
,
M
be the dual lattice of
N
,
\sigma
be a cone in
\Delta
.
We recall that a global section ring of
X(\sigma)
is
k[\sigma^{\vee}\cap M]
.
Let
\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}
denote a prime ideal of
k[\sigma^{\vee}\cap M]
as follows:

\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}=\bigoplus_{\omega\in(\sigma^{\vee}\cap M)\setminus\sigma^{\perp}}k\chi^{\omega}

Let
O_{\sigma}
denote a closed subvariety of
X(\sigma)
associated with
\mathfrak{p}
.
Through an open immersion
X(\sigma)\hookrightarrow X(\Delta)
, we regard
O_{\sigma}
as a locally closed subscheme of
X(\Delta)
.

Then the following statements follow:

(a)

Let
\tau\in\Delta
be a cone such that
\sigma\not\subset\tau
.
Then
O_{\sigma}\cap X(\tau)=\emptyset
.

(b)

X(\sigma)=O_{\sigma}\amalg\bigcup_{\tau\precneqq\sigma}X(\tau)

(c)

X(\Delta)=\coprod_{\sigma\in\Delta}O_{\sigma}

(d)

\overline{O_{\sigma}}=\coprod_{\sigma\preceq\tau}O_{\tau}

Proof.

We prove the statement from (a) to (d) in order.

(a)

Let
\gamma
denote
\sigma\cap\tau\in\Delta
.
Because
\sigma\not\subset\tau
,
\gamma\neq\sigma
.
Let
\omega\in\sigma^{\vee}
be an element such that
\gamma=\sigma\cap\omega^{\perp}
.
We remark that
\omega\notin\sigma^{\perp}
because
\gamma\neq\sigma
.
In particular,
\chi^{\omega}\in\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}
.
On the other hand,
\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}k[\gamma^{\vee}\cap M]=k[\gamma^{\vee}\cap M]
because
k[\gamma^{\vee}\cap M]=k[\sigma^{\vee}\cap M]_{\chi^{\omega}}
.
Thus,
O_{\sigma}\times_{X(\sigma)}X(\gamma)=\emptyset
.
Therefore,
O_{\sigma}\cap X(\tau)=\emptyset
because
X(\gamma)=X(\sigma)\cap X(\tau)
.

(b)

From (a), for any
\tau\precneqq\sigma
,
O_{\sigma}\cap X(\tau)=\emptyset
.
Thus,
O_{\sigma}\cap\bigcup_{\tau\precneqq\sigma}X(\tau)=\emptyset
.
It is enough to show that
X(\sigma)\subset O_{\sigma}\amalg\bigcup_{\tau\precneqq\sigma}X(\tau)
.
Let
\mathfrak{p}\subset k[\sigma^{\vee}\cap M]
be a prime ideal.
We assume that
\mathfrak{p}\subset\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}
.
From the definition of
\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}
, there exists
\omega\in(\sigma^{\vee}\cap M)\setminus\sigma^{\perp}
such that
\chi^{\omega}\notin\mathfrak{p}
.
In particular,
\mathfrak{p}\in D(\chi^{\omega})
where
D(\chi^{\omega})
is a fundamental open subset of an affine scheme
X(\sigma)
associated with
\chi^{\omega}\in k[\sigma^{\vee}\cap M]
.
Let
\tau
be a denote a face
\sigma\cap\omega^{\perp}
of
\sigma
.
Then
D(\chi^{\omega})=X(\tau)
as an open subset of
X(\sigma)
.
Thus,
\mathfrak{p}\in X(\tau)
.
Because
\omega\not\in\sigma^{\perp}
,
\tau\neq\sigma
.
Therefore,
X(\sigma)\subset O_{\sigma}\amalg\bigcup_{\tau\precneqq\sigma}X(\tau)
.

(c)

First, we show that
O_{\sigma}\cap O_{\tau}=\emptyset
for any distinct
\sigma,\tau\in\Delta
.
Because
\sigma\neq\tau
,
\sigma\not\subset\tau
or
\tau\not\subset\sigma
holds.
We may assume
\sigma\not\subset\tau
.
Then
O_{\sigma}\cap X(\tau)
from (a).
Because
O_{\tau}\subset X(\tau)
,
O_{\sigma}\cap O_{\tau}=\emptyset
.
Next, we show that
X(\Delta)\subset\bigcup_{\sigma\in\Delta}O_{\sigma}
.
Let
x\in X(\Delta)
be a point.
Then there exists minimal cone
\sigma\in\Delta
such that
x\in X(\sigma)
because
\Delta
is a fan.
If
x\notin O_{\sigma}
, there exists
\tau\precneqq\sigma
such that
x\in X(\tau)
.
However, it is a contradiction to the minimality of
\sigma
.
Thus,
x\in O_{\sigma}
.

(d)

Let
\tau\in\Delta
be a cone.
First, we assume that
\sigma\not\subset\tau
.
Then
O_{\sigma}\cap X(\tau)=\emptyset
from (a).
Thus,
\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau)=\emptyset
.
Second, we assume that
\sigma\subset\tau
.
Let
\mathfrak{q}
denote
\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}\cap k[M\cap\tau^{\vee}]
.
From the definition of
\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}
,
\mathfrak{q}=\bigoplus_{\omega\in(\tau^{\vee}\cap M)\setminus\sigma^{\perp}}k\chi^{\omega}
.
Thus,
\mathfrak{q}\subset\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}
.
Hence,
O_{\tau}\subset\overline{O_{\sigma}}\cap X(\tau)
.
Therefore, from (c), the statement holds.

∎

The following lemma is a basic well-known result about the relation between a toric morphism and torus orbits.

Lemma 8.7
.

Let
N
and
N^{\prime}
be a lattice,
\Delta
and
\Delta^{\prime}
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
and
N^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
respectively.
Let
\pi
be a lattice morphism from
N^{\prime}
to
N
, and we assume that
\pi
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\prime}
and
\Delta
.
Then the following statements hold:

(a)

Let
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}
be a cone and
\sigma\in\Delta
be a minimal cone such that
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma
.
Then
\pi_{*}(O_{\tau})\subset O_{\sigma}
.

(b)

We use the notation of (a).
If
\pi
is surjective,
\pi_{*}(O_{\tau})=O_{\sigma}
.

(c)

Let
\sigma\in\Delta
be a cone and
U
be an open subscheme
X(\Delta^{\prime})\times_{X(\Delta)}X(\sigma)
of
X(\Delta^{\prime})
.
Then
U=\bigcup_{\tau\in\Delta^{\prime},\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma}X(\tau)
.

Proof.

Let
M
and
M^{\prime}
be the dual lattices of
N
and
N^{\prime}
, respectively.
Let
\pi^{*}
denote a lattice morphism
M^{\prime}\rightarrow M
or a ring morphism
k[M^{\prime}]\rightarrow k[M]
induced by
\pi
.
We prove the statements from (a) to (c) in order.

(a)

From the assumption,
\pi
induces the ring morphism
\pi^{*}\colon k[\sigma^{\vee}\cap M]\rightarrow k[\tau^{\vee}\cap M^{\prime}]
.
Thus, it is enough to show that
\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}\subset(\pi^{*})^{-1}(\mathfrak{p}_{\tau})
.
Let
\omega\in(\sigma^{\vee}\cap M)\setminus\sigma^{\perp}
be an element and
v^{\prime}\in\tau^{\circ}\cap N^{\prime}
be an element.
Then
\pi(v^{\prime})\in\sigma^{\circ}\cap N
from the minimally of
\sigma
.
Because
\omega\notin\sigma^{\perp}
and
\pi(v^{\prime})\in\sigma^{\circ}\cap N
, we can check that
\langle v^{\prime},\pi^{*}(\omega)\rangle=\langle\pi(v^{\prime}),\omega\rangle>0
.
Thus,
\pi^{*}(\omega)\notin\tau^{\perp}
.
Therefore,
\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}\subset(\pi^{*})^{-1}(\mathfrak{p}_{\tau})
.

(b)

Because
\pi
is surjective,
\pi^{*}\colon k[\sigma^{\vee}\cap M]\rightarrow k[\tau^{\vee}\cap M^{\prime}]
is injective.
Let
f=\sum_{\omega\in\sigma^{\vee}\cap M}a_{\omega}\chi^{\omega}\in(\pi^{*})^{-1}(\mathfrak{p}_{\tau})
be a Laurant polynomial.
From the injectivity of
\pi^{*}
,
\sum_{\omega\in\sigma^{\vee}\cap M}a_{\omega}\chi^{\pi^{*}(\omega)}
is a
T_{N^{\prime}}
-invariant decomposition of
\pi^{*}(f)
.
In particular, for any
\omega\in\sigma^{\vee}\cap M
such that
a_{\omega}\neq 0
,
\pi^{*}(\omega)\notin\tau^{\perp}
because
\pi^{*}(f)\in\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}
.
Let
\omega\in\sigma^{\vee}\cap M
be an element such that
a_{\omega}\neq 0
.
Then there exists
v^{\prime}\in\tau\cap N^{\prime}
such that
\langle v^{\prime},\pi^{*}(\omega)\rangle>0
.
Thus,
\langle\pi(v^{\prime}),\omega\rangle=\langle v^{\prime},\pi^{*}(\omega)\rangle>0
.
Because
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma
,
\pi(v^{\prime})\in\sigma\cap N
.
Hence,
\omega\notin\sigma^{\perp}
and
\chi^{\omega}\in\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}
.
Therefore,
f\in\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}=(\pi^{*})^{-1}(\mathfrak{p}_{\tau})
from (a).

The equation
\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}=(\pi^{*})^{-1}(\mathfrak{p}_{\tau})
induces an injective ring morphism
\pi^{*}\colon k[\sigma^{\perp}\cap M]\rightarrow k[\tau^{\perp}\cap M^{\prime}]
.
We can check that this ring morphism is faithfully flat because lattice morphism
\pi^{*}|_{M\cap\sigma^{\perp}}\colon M\cap\sigma^{\perp}\rightarrow M^{\prime}\cap\tau^{\perp}
is split injection.
Thus,
\pi_{*}(O_{\tau})=O_{\sigma}
.

(c)

Let
\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}
be a cone.
First, we assume that
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma
.
Then from (a),
\pi_{*}(O_{\tau})\subset O_{\sigma}
so that
O_{\tau}\subset U

Second, we assume that
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\not\subset\sigma
.
From the assumption of
\pi
, there exists a minimal cone
\gamma\in\Delta
such that
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\gamma
.
From the assumption of
\tau
,
\gamma\not\subset\sigma
.
Thus, from Lemma
8.6
(a),
O_{\gamma}\cap X(\sigma)=\emptyset
.
From (a),
\pi_{*}(O_{\tau})\subset O_{\gamma}
so that
O_{\tau}\cap U=\emptyset
.

In conclusion, from Lemma
8.6
(b) and (c), the following equation holds:

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma,\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}}O_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\subset\sigma,\tau\in\Delta^{\prime}}X(\tau)

∎

The following lemma is a basic well-known result.
We used it when we consider the mock toric structure of the closure of a stratum of a mock toric variety.

Proposition 8.8
.

[
1
, Prop.3.2.7]

Let
N
be a lattice of finite rank and
\Delta
be a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
N_{\mathbb{R}}
.
For
\sigma\in\Delta
, let
\Delta^{\sigma}
denote the following sub fan of
\Delta
.

\Delta^{\sigma}=\{\gamma\in\Delta\mid\exists\tau\in\Delta\ \ \mathrm{s.t.}\ \ \gamma\cup\sigma\subset\tau\}

Let
\pi^{\sigma}
denote the quotient map
N\rightarrow N/(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N)
and
\Delta[\sigma]
denote the following set:

\Delta[\sigma]=\{(\pi^{\sigma})_{\mathbb{R}}(\tau)\mid\sigma\subset\tau\in\Delta\}

Then the following statements follow:

(a)

The set
\Delta[\sigma]
is a strongly convex rational polyhedral fan in
(N/(\langle\sigma\rangle\cap N))_{\mathbb{R}}
.

(b)

The morphism
\pi^{\sigma}
is compatible with the fans
\Delta^{\sigma}
and
\Delta[\sigma]
.

(c)

A closed immersion
\overline{O_{\sigma}}\hookrightarrow X(\Delta)
passes through
X(\Delta^{\sigma})\subset X(\Delta)
.

(d)

The following composition is isomorphic:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{O_{\sigma}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta^{\sigma})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Delta[\sigma])}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi^{\sigma})_{*}}
